[2026-09-20 17:53:30] Now, I want to try to use the extended MegaLMM framework to analyze this oat-pea experiment.  The idea follows.  Starting from the point of view of the oat, consider each pea accession as a different "environment" for the oats.  Thus, if we have 400 different pea accessions, we have 400 different environments.  Say we also have 500 oat accessions.  In the MegaLMM framework, this defines a 500 x 400 matrix which is very sparsely populated.  It's an empirical question as to whether the MegaLMM analysis can overcome that degree of sparsity and give meaningful results, but I want to try.  A first question is what values, exactly, to put in the sparse matrix.  We have oat x pea combinations from different trials.  I can think of three possibilities, maybe you can think of others. 1. Just take the raw oat yield from each oat x pea combination and put that value in the corresponding oat accession x pea environment cell of the matrix. 2. Before putting values in the matrix, center within each trial by subtracting the mean oat yield from each raw oat yield.  Put the centered oat yield in the corresponding oat accession x pea environment cell of the matrix.  3. Before putting values in the matrix, standardize within each trial, subtracting the mean and dividing by the standard deviation of oat yield.  In principle, MegaLMM works better if we can provide meaningful environmental covariates.  Here, those covariates should characterize the pea accession.  First, I would use the pea yield BLUE across the full experiment as a characteristic of the pea.  I would also use pea flowering date or pea maturity date.  I suspect they are too strongly correlated for it to make sense to use both.  Then, because we have DNA markers for the peas, we can include eigenvectors from the pea GRM as pea characteristics.  I have no idea how many to include.  Maybe start by including enough to cover 80% of the pea genetic variance.  An example of the use of the MegaLMM model is in the sibling repo BarleyWinterSurvival.  An example of extended MegaLMM using environmental covariates is in the sibling repo DSFASrecommending.  Getting this to work will be a big lift.  Go ahead and give it a shot then write our standard documentation components of README, DESIGN, and BACKGROUND.  Thanks!

[2026-09-21 09:52:44] A couple things I didn't fully understand:

You explain this one briefly in SIMULATION.md but a fuller explanation would be useful to me.  Sometimes simulating using the model that the statistical analysis actually looks for can be useful: if the analysis still doesn't find it on this favorable terrain, you know you have a problem. 
The interaction is generated so that a single factor has covariance exactly G_oat[i,i'] × G_pea[j,j']. As rank grows the truth converges on DGE-IGE's assumption; at rank 1 it's MegaLMM's home ground. Without that, simulating interaction as a factor model and reporting that the factor model wins would have been circular.

For this one: which part of the code limits the Kronecker term?
- The Kronecker term is rank-limited (30 eigenvectors per species) for tractability — so dge_ige isn't fitting the exact model it claims, and that handicaps it in precisely the comparison it's winning least. Worth a sensitivity check at small sizes where the exact kernel fits.

For this one: "single replicate per cell" means that you do one simulation per scenario, where scenario is a combination of panel size, sparsity, interaction rank, etc.?
- Single replicate per cell, so individual numbers carry sampling noise; the pattern is consistent enough to trust, the third digit isn't. --reps 5 fixes that.

[2026-09-21 20:48:57] I asked Claude App if there was a way to force one factor with loadings all equal to 1 in MegaLMM.  Here's what it said pasted below.  I suggest in this case making a branch to test this out since it is a big departure in the MegaLMM modeling.
There's already a fixed_factors mechanism and a Lambda_mean in the sampler. Let me see how they're set up.Good news first: MegaLMM already has this feature. I cloned the current source from GitHub and read the sampler code. There's an undocumented-in-vignettes argument, Lambda_fixed, that does exactly what you want. So the job is mainly configuration and validation, not modifying the package. 
The default setting has a detail that matters for your problem. The horseshoe's global scale is set from prop_0 = 0.1, a prior expectation that about 10% of loadings in a factor are nonzero. A main-effect factor has every loading nonzero, which is the densest possible factor, and the default prior is tuned against exactly that. This is a fourth reason, alongside Code's three mechanisms, why MegaLMM struggles to reconstruct the oat main effect. A fixed factor sidesteps it entirely.
How Lambda_fixed works
The feature is threaded through the sampler as follows:
Setup. setup_model_MegaLMM(..., Lambda_fixed = ...) takes a matrix of the first k rows of Λ. It builds a fixed_factors logical vector and sets Kr = K − k as the number of free factors.
Initialization. initialize_variables_MegaLMM writes the fixed rows into Λ.
Loadings update. sample_latent_traits subtracts F[, fixed]Λ[fixed, ] from the data before sampling. It then updates only Lambda[!fixed_factors, ], so the fixed rows are never touched.
Factor scores. Scores are sampled against the full Λ, fixed rows included, so f₁ is estimated from all of each oat's observations across every pea column. That is exactly the row-pooling you want.
Shrinkage priors. Both the horseshoe and ARD samplers compute precisions only for the Kr free rows. The fixed factor is exempt from shrinkage.
Reordering. reorder_factors keeps fixed factors first and reorders only the free ones.
The fixed factor still gets the level-2 model: f₁ = U_F1 + E_F1, with its own sampled h². So you get an oat main effect with kinship borrowing plus an iid non-genetic part. That is essentially DGE-IGE's Pr_i ~ N(0, G_oat σ²), with free factors layered on top for interaction.
The fallback also changes in the right direction. When the free factors shrink away, predictions collapse to μ_j + f₁ᵢ + U_R[i,j], the correct margin, instead of μ_j + U_R[i,j].
Three things to get right
1. Which API Code is using. Lambda_fixed exists only in the classic setup_model_MegaLMM() pipeline, which both vignettes use. The newer modular setup (MegaLMM_F(), MegaLMM_add_trait_matrix() in new_MegaLMM_setup.R) has the fixed-factor block commented out. If Code built on the new API, it would need to switch or port that block.
2. scale_Y. Fixed loadings apply on the scale the sampler works in. With the default scale_Y = TRUE, each pea column is standardized, so loadings of 1 mean "equal effect in each column's SD units." At 10 observations per column those SDs are noisy.
The better option is scale_Y = FALSE, after dividing all of Y by one global SD yourself. The other priors assume roughly unit scale.
3. The prior on the factor's variance. This is the one most likely to bite. With loadings fixed at 1, the oat main-effect variance equals var(f₁), which is controlled by tot_F_prec. Its default prior is tot_F_var = list(V = 18/20, nu = 20), an inverse gamma concentrated near 1. For free factors that's harmless, because scale trades off against Λ. For your fixed factor, it would pin the main-effect variance near 0.9 of the unit scale regardless of the data.
The code accepts length-K vectors here, so you can loosen the prior for factor 1 only.
A sketch of the setup (I've written n_fac to avoid a clash with the kinship K; adapt the formula to your data):
p <- ncol(Y); n_fac <- 6
Y_s <- Y / sd(Y, na.rm = TRUE)          # one global scale, not per column

run_parameters <- MegaLMM_control(scale_Y = FALSE, K = n_fac, ...)
state <- setup_model_MegaLMM(
  Y_s, formula = ~ 1 + (1 | Oat), data = oat_data,
  relmat = list(Oat = K_oat),
  Lambda_fixed = matrix(1, nrow = 1, ncol = p),
  run_parameters = run_parameters, run_ID = "fixed_main_effect")

priors <- MegaLMM_priors(
  tot_F_var = list(V  = c(0.5, rep(18/20, n_fac - 1)),
                   nu = c(3,   rep(20,    n_fac - 1))))
state <- set_priors_MegaLMM(state, priors)

A cosmetic quirk: when posterior samples are saved, remove_nuisance_parameters rescales Λ by √var(F) so that factors have unit variance. Factor 1's saved loadings will therefore all equal the main-effect SD rather than 1. That's expected, not a bug.
Validate before trusting it
I couldn't run it here: there's no R in my sandbox and CRAN is outside its network allowlist. The code path also looks lightly exercised. The vignettes never use it, and there's a 2022 comment in the sampler noting that the line subtracting the fixed-factor contribution "was missing" until then.
Before relying on it, I'd have Code rerun the existing simulation grid with one fixed factor and check two things:
At 5% and 1.1% density, f₁'s posterior mean should correlate with the true producer effect at least as well as the row average does. It should track DGE-IGE if the free factors shrink away.
At 45%, accuracy for the interaction term shouldn't drop relative to the unconstrained fit. That would tell you the fixed factor isn't crowding out real structure.
If both hold, you have one model that behaves like DGE-IGE when the matrix is sparse and like MegaLMM when it's dense. If something misbehaves, the bookkeeping to debug is confined to sample_latent_traits.R and the two Lambda_prec samplers, a few hundred lines of R.

<pasted_content id="663e">
In what follows, there is a coding project but also first some thinking needed for designing the experiment.  When you write the plan, lay out clearly your considerations for design.
We have looked at many combinations of oat and pea accessions.  We will soon have eight trials from four locations over two years.  Each trial had ~400 plots.  But we also had about 400 accessions each of oat and pea.  So only a very small fraction of the combination space was evaluated.  From the data, using a producer associate model, we have estimated producer and associate effects for both oat and pea.  We now want to validate our estimates of those effects.  Validation involves showing, in a separate validation trial, that a set of accessions we predict to have a high effect has a statistically significantly higher effect than a set of accessions we predict to have a low effect.  I contend that identifying or predicting accessions with a low producer effect is not interesting.  In breeding trials, we can always find accessions that perform poorly.  Thus, what I am interested in is finding accessions, all of which have high producer effects but are contrasted for their associate effects.  Thus, I want to select two pools, called As+ and As-, that maximize either the Pr + As value or the Pr - As value.  These two pools will exist for both oat and pea.  Assume we will be able to evaluate either 60, 80, or 100 plots per location and we have 5 locations.  Some unknowns are:
How big should the As+ and As- pools be?  There are tradeoffs.  The larger the pools the more they smooth out estimation error.  The larger the pools, the fewer combinations we can test per accession.  The larger the pools, the less divergent they will be.  Accessions will need to be considered as random effects.  I don't have a clear idea how to optimize for pool size.
How should the validation trial be randomized?  I am not interested in estimating specific mixing abilities, so I am not concerned about repeating combinations for that purpose.  On the other hand, consider whether getting a clean estimate of error variance is important.  We can only get that if we replicate specific combinations.  Note that the randomization needs to consider both the combinations to make and where to place them among the 5 locations.  I'm assuming balancing representation across locations would be good.
Can you use the data that we have to estimate the power of the validation experiment?  What would the power be considering the 60, 80, or 100 plots per location.

# 1. `code/dge_ige_functions.R` — the fitter and the Kronecker basis

There is no `fit_dge_ige()` in this file. The core fitter is **`fit_producer_associate()`** at `/Users/jj332/Documents/GitRepo/B4I_Fall2026/code/dge_ige_functions.R:266`. `fit_dge_ige()` is a **thin simulation wrapper** in `code/sim_fit.R:123` (see §2).

## Kronecker interaction basis

**Eigenvectors / scaling** — `grm_basis()` at `dge_ige_functions.R:140-151`:
```r
grm_basis &lt;- function(G, rank = NULL, variance = NULL) {
  e &lt;- eigen(G, symmetric = TRUE)
  vals &lt;- pmax(e$values, 0)
  k &lt;- if (!is.null(rank)) { min(rank, sum(vals &gt; 1e-8 * max(vals))) }
       else { which(cumsum(vals) / sum(vals) &gt;= variance)[1] }
  V &lt;- sweep(e$vectors[, seq_len(k), drop = FALSE], 2, sqrt(vals[seq_len(k)]), "*")
  rownames(V) &lt;- rownames(G)
  V
}
```
So: `eigen(G, symmetric = TRUE)` leading `k` eigenvectors, **scaled by `sqrt(eigenvalue)`** (i.e. `A = U Λ^½`, so `A A' = G`), eigenvalues floored at 0, rank capped at the non-null count. **`rownames` are set; `colnames` are NOT.**

**Column ordering** — `kron_basis()` at `dge_ige_functions.R:168-172`:
```r
kron_basis &lt;- function(A, B, oat_idx, pea_idx) {
  ka &lt;- ncol(A); kb &lt;- ncol(B)
  A[oat_idx, rep(seq_len(ka), each  = kb), drop = FALSE] *
    B[pea_idx, rep(seq_len(kb), times = ka), drop = FALSE]
}
```
Column `c = (p-1)*kb + q` holds `A[,p] * B[,q]` — row-wise Kronecker / Khatri-Rao, **row-major in (oat index, pea index)**. Confirmed by the test at `tests/test_kronecker.R:46-52` and documented in `docs/specific-combination_kronecker.md` (step 2, the `rep()` table).

**Column names do NOT encode the eigenvector pair.** `grm_basis()` sets no colnames, and `kron_basis()` returns a bare elementwise product of two subsetted matrices — the result has no column dimnames at all. The `(p, q)` pair is recoverable only from arithmetic on the position, and the companion reshape at `dge_ige_functions.R:395-396` relies on that:
```r
Beta &lt;- matrix(b[, match(trait, DGE_IGE_TRAITS)],
               nrow = ncol(kron_A), ncol = ncol(kron_B), byrow = TRUE)
```
`byrow = TRUE` is load-bearing (comment at lines 388-391; "Why it is fragile" section of `docs/specific-combination_kronecker.md`).

## BGLR model type for the interaction term
**`BRR`**, both branches — `dge_ige_functions.R:342` (exact kernel) and `:349-350` (low-rank):
```r
ETA$G_mix &lt;- list(X = kron_basis(kron_A, kron_B, oat_idx, pea_idx),
                  model = "BRR")
```
Never `RKHS`. The rationale is the file header (`dge_ige_functions.R:14-17`): a BRR on `Z L` keeps coefficients on the accession scale, while an RKHS term's coefficients "live in the eigenvector basis of the plot kernel and carry no accession identity". All other genetic terms are also `BRR` (`:319-320`); `trial` is `FIXED` (`:318`).

## `saveAt`
Passed, but **to a temp dir by default** — `dge_ige_functions.R:355-361`:
```r
  fit &lt;- BGLR::Multitrait(
    y = Y, ETA = ETA, intercept = FALSE,
    resCov = list(df0 = 4, S0 = NULL, type = "UN"),
    nIter = nIter, burnIn = burnIn, thin = thin,
    saveAt = saveAt %||% file.path(tempdir(), "dge_ige_"),
    verbose = verbose
  )
```
All three callers point it at `tempdir()`:
- `code/sim_fit.R:144` — `saveAt = file.path(tempdir(), "sim_dge_")`
- `code/cross_validate_combinations.R:184` — `file.path(tempdir(), paste0("cv_", mdl, "_", k, "_"))`

Only the production script writes to a kept location: `code/BGLR_multi_trait_model.R:373`, `saveAt = file.path(out_dir, paste0("BGLR_seed_", seed_value, "_"))`. The actual files that landed there are **covariance samples only** — `output/BGLR_seed_12567_Omega_{2,3,4}.dat` and `..._R.dat`. There is no `*_beta.dat`: BGLR's `Multitrait` does not stream per-coefficient samples, so **MCMC samples of the interaction coefficients are never on disk** anywhere in this repo.

## Posterior SDs / PEV
**None retained from BGLR anywhere.** `accession_effects()` (`:117-128`) and the `interaction` surfaces (`:392-402`) use `fit$ETA[[term]]$beta` (the posterior mean) exclusively. No `SD.beta`, `getPostSd`, or credible interval is read in `dge_ige_functions.R`, `sim_fit.R`, or `cross_validate_combinations.R`. The only quantile work on BGLR samples in the repo is `code/pilot/pilot_dge_ige.R:230-266`, and that reads the saved `Omega`/`R` `.dat` files (variance components), not coefficients. For the one PEV path that does exist, see §5.

# 2. Reconstructing the fitted interaction surface for scoring

## The reconstruction itself — `dge_ige_functions.R:392-402`
```r
    interaction = if (fit_mix_term &amp;&amp; !is.na(kron_rank)) {
      b &lt;- fit$ETA$G_mix$beta
      surf &lt;- function(trait) {
        Beta &lt;- matrix(b[, match(trait, DGE_IGE_TRAITS)],
                       nrow = ncol(kron_A), ncol = ncol(kron_B), byrow = TRUE)
        out &lt;- kron_A %*% Beta %*% t(kron_B)
        dimnames(out) &lt;- list(oatAccs, peaAccs)
        out
      }
      list(oat = surf("oatYield"), pea = surf("peaYield"))
    } else NULL,
```
This is what makes never-observed combinations free: `kron_A %*% Beta %*% t(kron_B)` is the **full** `n_oat x n_pea` surface, so any cell is a lookup. `b` is `kron_rank^2 x 2` (one column per trait, trait order `DGE_IGE_TRAITS = c("peaYield", "oatYield")` at `:190`).

The **exact-kernel branch returns `interaction = NULL`** — guarded by `!is.na(kron_rank)`. `code/sim_kron_study.R:26-33` documents why: the exact kernel is defined only over observed combinations and "returns no value for a combination nobody grew", so `r_int` comes back `NA`.

## Per-combination prediction, simulation side — `code/sim_fit.R:123-180`
`fit_dge_ige()` pads to the full panel and adds the interaction into the surfaces:
```r
# sim_fit.R:161-176
  surface_oat &lt;- outer(oat_prod, pea_assoc, "+")
  surface_pea &lt;- outer(oat_assoc, pea_prod, "+")

  interaction &lt;- NULL
  if (with_interaction &amp;&amp; !is.null(f$interaction)) {
    pad_mat &lt;- function(M) {
      out &lt;- matrix(0, length(oat_names), length(pea_names),
                    dimnames = list(oat_names, pea_names))
      out[rownames(M), colnames(M)] &lt;- M
      out
    }
    interaction &lt;- list(oat = pad_mat(f$interaction$oat),
                        pea = pad_mat(f$interaction$pea))
    surface_oat &lt;- surface_oat + interaction$oat
    surface_pea &lt;- surface_pea + interaction$pea
  }
```
Returns `list(surface = list(oat, pea), interaction = interaction, varcomp = f$varcomp)` (`:178-179`). Both surfaces are always **oat-rows x pea-cols** (header, `sim_fit.R:31-34`).

## Per-combination prediction, real-data side — `code/cross_validate_combinations.R:201-215`
```r
    if (mdl == "dge_ige" &amp;&amp; !is.null(f$interaction)) {
      io &lt;- f$interaction$oat; ip &lt;- f$interaction$pea
      idx &lt;- cbind(match(held$oatAcc, rownames(io)),
                   match(held$peaAcc, colnames(io)))
      ok &lt;- !is.na(idx[, 1]) &amp; !is.na(idx[, 2])
      add_o &lt;- numeric(nrow(held)); add_p &lt;- numeric(nrow(held))
      add_o[ok] &lt;- io[idx[ok, , drop = FALSE]]
      add_p[ok] &lt;- ip[idx[ok, , drop = FALSE]]
      pred_oat &lt;- pred_oat + add_o
      pred_pea &lt;- pred_pea + add_p
    }
```
This is the only place a held-out (never-trained) combination's interaction is read off by name.

## `interaction_part()` — `code/sim_fit.R:304-306`
```r
interaction_part &lt;- function(M) {
  M - rowMeans(M) - rep(colMeans(M), each = nrow(M)) + mean(M)
}
```
Its complement, `additive_part()` at `sim_fit.R:325-327`:
```r
additive_part &lt;- function(M) {
  outer(rowMeans(M), colMeans(M), "+") - mean(M)
}
```
Doc comments at `:296-303` and `:308-324` explain the purpose: both frameworks get put on the same footing by double-centring, and `rowMeans(M)` *is* the oat main effect of the surface. Algebraic identities for both are tested at `tests/test_surface.R:27-55` (exactness, zero margins, idempotence, orthogonality, additive-surface → exactly zero).

## Where they are used in scoring — `sim_fit.R:343-352`
```r
  per_trait &lt;- function(surface, prod_vec, assoc_vec, I_true) {
    truth_gma   &lt;- prod_vec[idx[, 1]] + assoc_vec[idx[, 2]]
    truth_total &lt;- truth_gma + I_true[idx]
    c(
      total       = safe_cor(surface[idx], truth_total),
      gma         = safe_cor(additive_part(surface)[idx], truth_gma),
      interaction = safe_cor(interaction_part(surface)[idx],
                             interaction_part(I_true)[idx])
    )
  }
```
Note `interaction_part()` is applied to the **whole surface** and to the **whole truth matrix**, then subset at `idx`. The scored cells come from `scoring_index()` at `sim_fit.R:90-104` — `SIM_SCORE_SET &lt;- "unobserved"` (`code/sim_config.R:424`), i.e. **never-observed cells are the default test set**, with the three-point rationale at `sim_fit.R:70-89`. `prepare_scenario()` at `sim_fit.R:455-458` bundles `train` + `idx`.

## `code/evaluation.R` / `code/evaluation_snippets.R`
- `evaluation.R:93-96` registers the debug groups: `sim_bglr = c("fit_dge_ige", "run_scenario_bglr")` and `sim_score = c("interaction_part", "score_predictions", "baseline_predictions")`. No reconstruction logic of its own.
- `evaluation_snippets.R:92-111` is the hand-check for the basis and the reshape:
```r
# evaluation_snippets.R:98-102
set.seed(3); Beta &lt;- matrix(rnorm(25), 5, 5)
oi &lt;- c(1, 4, 9); pj &lt;- c(2, 7, 11)
lhs &lt;- as.vector(kron_basis(A, B, oi, pj) %*% as.vector(t(Beta)))
rhs &lt;- (A %*% Beta %*% t(B))[cbind(oi, pj)]
max(abs(lhs - rhs))                               # 6.9e-18
```
**Stale reference:** `evaluation_snippets.R:110` reads `dim(add$total); max(abs(add$interaction))`. `fit_dge_ige()` has no `$total` element (it returns `surface`), so that line is out of date with `sim_fit.R:178-179`.

# 3. Simulation ground truth for the interaction — `code/sim_generate.R`

## Construction from `n_factors` — `sim_generate.R:278-290`
```r
  draw_interaction &lt;- function() {
    if (!(n_factors &gt; 0 &amp;&amp; V_I &gt; 0)) {
      return(list(I = matrix(0, n_oat, n_pea), U = NULL, Lambda = NULL))
    }
    U   &lt;- vapply(seq_len(n_factors), \(f) draw_effect(L_oat, 1), numeric(n_oat))
    Lam &lt;- vapply(seq_len(n_factors), \(f) draw_effect(L_pea, 1), numeric(n_pea))
    I &lt;- (U %*% t(Lam)) / sqrt(n_factors)
    I &lt;- I * sqrt(V_I / stats::var(as.vector(I)))
    list(I = I, U = U, Lambda = Lam)
  }
  int_oat &lt;- draw_interaction()
  int_pea &lt;- draw_interaction()
```
- `U` is `n_oat x n_factors`, **oat scores**, each column a `draw_effect(L_oat, 1)` — covariance `G_oat`, empirically rescaled to **variance exactly 1**, mean-centred (`draw_effect()` at `:64-69`).
- `Lam` is `n_pea x n_factors`, **pea loadings**, same treatment with `L_pea`.
- Two surfaces drawn **independently** (`int_oat`, `int_pea`) — rationale at `:272-277`.
- **Important for any comparison:** the global rescaling `I * sqrt(V_I / var(vec(I)))` is folded into `I` only. The stored `U` and `Lambda` are *not* rescaled, so `U %*% t(Lambda)` does **not** equal `I`; it equals `I` up to the scalar `sqrt(n_factors) / sqrt(V_I/var(...))`. Comparison of a recovered score against `U_oat` is therefore valid only up to scale (and, for `n_factors &gt; 1`, up to rotation within the factor space).
- When `n_factors == 0` or `interaction_pct == 0`, `U` and `Lambda` are `NULL` and `I` is an all-zero matrix.

## `sim$truth` — `sim_generate.R:360-388`
```r
    truth = list(
      oat_prod = oat_prod, oat_assoc = oat_assoc,
      pea_prod = pea_prod, pea_assoc = pea_assoc,
      I_oat = int_oat$I, I_pea = int_pea$I,
      U_oat = int_oat$U, Lambda_oat = int_oat$Lambda,
      U_pea = int_pea$U, Lambda_pea = int_pea$Lambda,
      gxe = gxe_env,
      env_scale = list(oat = ..., pea = ...),
      env_mean  = list(oat = ..., pea = ...),
      gxe_cor = rho, realised_gxe_cor = realised_gxe_cor,
      V = list(oat = c(producer=, associate=, producer_env=, associate_env=,
                       interaction=, residual=), pea = c(...)),
      Sigma = list(oat = ..., pea = ..., R = R)
    ),
```
Top-level return is `list(obs, truth, G_oat, G_pea, settings)` (`:355-393`); `settings` carries `n_oat, n_pea, sparsity, n_factors, interaction_pct, n_envs, gxe_cor` (`:390-392`).

## Exactly which truth objects a recovered score/loading can be compared against
- **`truth$U_oat`** — `n_oat x n_factors`, oat scores of the **oat-yield** interaction surface.
- **`truth$Lambda_oat`** — `n_pea x n_factors`, pea loadings of the **oat-yield** surface.
- **`truth$U_pea`** / **`truth$Lambda_pea`** — the same pair for the **pea-yield** surface (independent draw).
- **`truth$I_oat`** / **`truth$I_pea`** — the `n_oat x n_pea` surfaces themselves (the only objects currently consumed anywhere).
- **`truth$V$oat[["interaction"]]`** / `V$pea[["interaction"]]` — the interaction variance share, for a magnitude check.

**`U_oat`, `Lambda_oat`, `U_pea`, `Lambda_pea` are written and never read.** A grep across `code/` and `tests/` returns only the two definition lines, `sim_generate.R:364-365`. Nothing in the repo currently scores a recovered score or loading against them. (`evaluation_snippets.R:69-70` references `sim$truth$interaction` and `sim$truth$producer`, which are also stale names — the current fields are `I_oat`/`I_pea` and `oat_prod`.)

---

# 4. SVD / eigen / Procrustes / varimax / low-rank — what exists

**No `svd()`, no `prcomp()`/`princomp()`, no Procrustes, no varimax/promax, no `irlba`/`rsvd` anywhere in `code/`.** (`grep -rniE "svd\(|procrustes|varimax|promax|irlba|rsvd|prcomp|princomp" code/` → zero hits for all of those.)

What exists is `eigen()` only:

| File:line | What |
|---|---|
| `code/dge_ige_functions.R:62-72` | `grm_factor()` — full `eigen`, drops directions below `tol*max`, returns `U Λ^½`; `tcrossprod(L) == G` |
| `code/dge_ige_functions.R:140-151` | `grm_basis()` — truncated `U Λ^½` by `rank` **or** by cumulative-variance `variance` |
| `code/dge_ige_functions.R:168-172` | `kron_basis()` — row-wise Kronecker of two such bases |
| `code/megalmm_build_inputs.R:233` | `grm_eigenvectors()` — pea GRM eigenvectors as `Lambda`-prior covariates |
| `code/sim_kron_study.R:123-130` | `trace_retained(G_oat, G_pea, rank)` — `share(G_oat) * share(G_pea)`, the fraction of the Kronecker trace a given rank keeps; eigenvalues only |
| `code/evaluation.R:461`, `code/create_GRMs_T3.R:208`, `code/BGLR_multi_trait_model.R:279`, `code/pilot/pilot_dge_ige.R:130` | min-eigenvalue PSD diagnostics |
| `code/sim_fit.R:216-219` | `grm_basis(..., variance = eigen_variance)` feeding MegaLMM's `X_Env` |

Reusable rank machinery: `grm_basis()`, `kron_basis()`, `trace_retained()`, `interaction_part()`/`additive_part()`, and the `byrow = TRUE` reshape at `dge_ige_functions.R:395-396`. The rank-truncation confound itself is written up at `code/sim_kron_study.R:1-70`.

# 5. Deregression / reliability / PEV / inverse-variance weighting

**No deregression anywhere** (`grep -rniE "dereg"` → zero hits).

Reliability and PEV exist in exactly one place, derived from variance components rather than from BGLR posterior spread — `code/validation_functions.R:152-172`:
```r
    # Var(true) = Var(BLUP) + E[PEV] for a conditionally unbiased predictor,
    # so the reliability of the BLUPs and the PEV both follow from the fit.
    # Clamped: a var(BLUP) above the component means the chains have not
    # settled, and a negative PEV would silently flatter every power estimate.
    rel &lt;- function(v_blup, sigma2) min(max(v_blup / sigma2, 1e-6), 0.999)
    ...
      rel_Pr       = rel(stats::var(acc$Pr), sigma2_Pr),
      rel_As       = rel(stats::var(acc$As), sigma2_As),
      PEV_As       = sigma2_As * (1 - rel(stats::var(acc$As), sigma2_As)),
```
`validation_inputs()` starts at `validation_functions.R:112`; it reads `output/BGLR_variance_components.csv` (`:116`) and the per-seed effect CSVs, averaging over MCMC seeds at `:141-144` ("the BLUP is the mean over MCMC chains"). Header context at `validation_functions.R:7`, `:26-27`.

Consumers:
- `code/validate_pool_selection.R:64`, `:88-89`, `:110-111` — prints and tabulates `PEV_As`, `reliability_As`.
- `code/validate_power.R:10` — `N(BLUP, PEV)` simulation of true effects; `:380` notes the limiting factor is reliability, not plot count.
- `code/validation_functions.R:475-480` — `sigma2_within` = "PEV + within-pool BLUP variance".
- `code/validate_crossval.R:219` — PEV deliberately set to 0 for the constrained selection rule.

**No inverse-variance weighting is implemented.** `grep` for `weight` yields only prose (`code/sim_anova.R:14` noting a plain ANOVA over-weights high cells; `code/validate_power.R:350` "weights by precision" as commentary; `validation_functions.R:414`; `sim_fit.R:323` about mean loadings). No `weights =` argument is passed to any model fit, and `BGLR::Multitrait` is called without weights at `dge_ige_functions.R:355-361` and `BGLR_multi_trait_model.R:364-376`.

# 6. MegaLMM factor scores and loadings

## What is sampled / computed — `code/megalmm_setup.R:221-245`
```r
  sample_params &lt;- c("Lambda", "F_h2", "resid_h2", "tot_Eta_prec", "B1")
  if (!is.null(envCov)) {
    sample_params &lt;- c(sample_params,
                       "U_F", "F", "B2_F", "Lambda_beta", "Lambda_beta_var")
  }
  MegaLMM_state$Posterior$posteriorSample_params &lt;- sample_params
  MegaLMM_state$Posterior$posteriorMean_params   &lt;- "Eta_mean"

  posterior_functions &lt;- list(
    U_CV2noU_R = "U_F %*% Lambda",
    U_CV2      = "U_F %*% Lambda + U_R",
    G  = "t(Lambda) %*% diag(F_h2[1,]) %*% Lambda + diag(resid_h2[1,]/tot_Eta_prec[1,])",
    R  = "t(Lambda) %*% diag(1-F_h2[1,]) %*% Lambda + diag((1-resid_h2[1,])/tot_Eta_prec[1,])",
    h2 = "(colSums(F_h2[1,]*Lambda^2)+resid_h2[1,]/tot_Eta_prec[1,])/(colSums(Lambda^2)+1/tot_Eta_prec[1,])"
  )
```
`U_F %*% Lambda` fixes the orientation: **`U_F` is `n_row x K` (accessions x factors), `Lambda` is `K x n_col` (factors x columns/partner-environments).**

## Extraction — `megalmm_setup.R:290-321`
```r
megalmm_posterior &lt;- function(MegaLMM_state, accessions = NULL) {
  .pm &lt;- function(p) MegaLMM::get_posterior_mean(
    MegaLMM::load_posterior_param(MegaLMM_state, p))
  ...
  out &lt;- list(
    Lambda   = .pm("Lambda"),
    U        = .pm("U_CV2"),
    U_noU_R  = .pm("U_CV2noU_R"),
    Eta_mean = MegaLMM::load_posterior_param(MegaLMM_state, "Eta_mean"),
    G = G, R = .pm("R"), h2 = .pm("h2"),
    G_cor = stats::cov2cor(G)
  )
  ...
  # U_F is the factor scores. Only saved when covariates are in play, and
  # needed to look at a fixed factor's score directly.
  if ("U_F" %in% MegaLMM_state$Posterior$posteriorSample_params) {
    out$U_F &lt;- tryCatch(.pm("U_F"), error = function(e) NULL)
  }
  out$U       &lt;- .undecorate_rows(out$U, accessions)
  out$U_noU_R &lt;- .undecorate_rows(out$U_noU_R, accessions)
  out$U_F     &lt;- .undecorate_rows(out$U_F, accessions)
```
So **`U_F` can be `NULL`** when no `envCov` was supplied (guard at `:309`).

## Naming
`.undecorate_rows()` at `megalmm_setup.R:325-335` strips MegaLMM's `"&lt;level&gt;::&lt;term&gt;"` row suffix and **hard-errors** if the result does not match the accession names passed in:
```r
  rownames(m) &lt;- stringr::str_remove(rownames(m), "::.*$")
  if (!is.null(accessions)) {
    if (!identical(rownames(m), as.character(accessions))) {
      stop("MegaLMM returned U rows that do not match the accessions passed ",
           "in. Predictions would be attached to the wrong accessions.", ...)
```
Applied to `U`, `U_noU_R` and `U_F` — so **`U_F` rownames are accession names**. `Lambda` is *not* undecorated; its columns inherit the `wideData` column names (the partner species' accessions).

## `factor_size` — `code/megalmm_oat_pea.R:289-296`
```r
cat("Lambda: ", nrow(post$Lambda), " factors x ", ncol(post$Lambda),
    " pea environments\n", sep = "")

# How much of each factor is actually used: the ARD prior shrinks surplus
# factors towards zero, so this says whether K was generous enough.
factor_size &lt;- rowSums(post$Lambda^2)
cat("factor sizes (sum of squared loadings):\n")
print(round(factor_size, 3))
```
`factor_size` is a **length-`K` vector, one entry per factor, `rowSums(Lambda^2)`** — the natural scale measure for a DGE-IGE-derived equivalent. It is a console print only; not saved to CSV. The whole posterior is persisted at `megalmm_oat_pea.R:304-308` as `output/megalmm_fit_&lt;fill&gt;.rds`.

## Other factor-object touchpoints
- `code/sim_fit.R:258-260` — `fit_megalmm_one()` returns `Lambda = post$Lambda, U_F = post$U_F` alongside the surface.
- `code/sim_fit.R:249-255` — `stats::sd(post$Lambda[1, ])` checks the pinned factor's loadings are constant **across columns**, confirming `Lambda` is factors-by-columns.
- `code/sim_fit.R:531-538` — `mainfactor()` correlates `side$U_F[, 1]` against the row species' true producer effect, matching `rownames(side$U_F)` into `rownames(G_row)`; takes `abs(r)` when the factor is free, because **a free factor's sign is arbitrary**. This is the closest existing precedent for comparing a recovered score to truth.
- `code/megalmm_setup.R:87-106` and `:132` — the `Lambda_fixed &lt;- matrix(1, nrow = 1, ncol = ncol(wideData))` pinning mechanism and why row 1 comes back constant-but-not-1.

# 7. Repo conventions for a new script in `code/`

Best templates: `code/sim_kron_study.R` (newest, Oct 1) and `code/cross_validate_combinations.R`.

## Header comment block
A `# ===...===` fence, a SHOUTING-CAPS title, then **the invocation lines first**, then prose in capitalised labelled paragraphs, then an `# Outputs:` manifest. From `code/sim_kron_study.R:1-6`:
```r
# ============================================================
# DOES SIM_KRON_RANK DECIDE THE MegaLMM-vs-DGE-IGE RESULT?
#
#   Rscript code/sim_kron_study.R --check        # one cell, fast
#   Rscript code/sim_kron_study.R                # the design
#   Rscript code/sim_kron_study.R --n-acc 60,100 --ranks 10,20,30,full
#
# THE CONFOUND. ...
```
And the outputs manifest, `code/cross_validate_combinations.R:44-48`:
```r
# Outputs: output/crossval/&lt;date&gt;/predictions.csv
#          output/crossval/&lt;date&gt;/accuracy.csv        overall, per model and trait
#          output/crossval/&lt;date&gt;/accuracy_by_support.csv
#          output/crossval/&lt;date&gt;/folds.csv
```
Shared-function files say **`# Sourced, not run.`** instead (`dge_ige_functions.R:19`, `sim_generate.R:54`, `sim_fit.R:36`), which is the marker `evaluation.R:117-130` uses to decide what is safe to `source()` (`EVAL_SOURCES`).

## `here::i_am` and sourcing — `code/sim_kron_study.R:72-80`
```r
library(tidyverse)

here::i_am("code/sim_kron_study.R")

source(here::here("code", "dge_ige_functions.R"))
source(here::here("code", "sim_config.R"))
source(here::here("code", "sim_generate.R"))
source(here::here("code", "sim_fit.R"))
source(here::here("code", "megalmm_setup.R"))
```
`library(tidyverse)` bare in scripts; `suppressPackageStartupMessages(library(tidyverse))` in sourced function files; everything else namespaced (`dplyr::`, `purrr::`, `readr::`, `stats::`, `tibble::`).

## The standard arg-parsing idiom — `code/sim_kron_study.R:82-98`
```r
args &lt;- commandArgs(trailingOnly = TRUE)
arg_value &lt;- function(flag, default) {
  i &lt;- match(flag, args)
  if (is.na(i) || i == length(args)) default else args[i + 1]
}
check   &lt;- "--check" %in% args
refresh &lt;- "--refresh" %in% args

ranks   &lt;- strsplit(arg_value("--ranks", "10,20,30,full"), ",")[[1]]
n_accs  &lt;- as.integer(strsplit(arg_value("--n-acc", "60,100,200"), ",")[[1]])
n_reps  &lt;- as.integer(arg_value("--reps", "3"))
n_iter  &lt;- as.integer(arg_value("--n-iter", "6000"))
burn_in &lt;- as.integer(arg_value("--burn-in", "1000"))
```
Defaults are **strings** coerced with `as.integer`/`as.numeric`; comma lists via `strsplit(...)[[1]]`. The richer variant (`code/sim_run.R:56-61`, `code/sim_int_run.R:47-61`) uses `default = NULL` plus a `has_flag()` helper:
```r
arg_value &lt;- function(flag, default = NULL) {
  i &lt;- match(flag, args)
  if (is.na(i) || i == length(args)) default else args[i + 1]
}
has_flag &lt;- function(flag) flag %in% args
```
Conditional defaults by mode: `code/cross_validate_combinations.R:66-67` — `as.integer(arg_value("--n-iter", if (quick) "3000" else "12000"))`. Standard flags across the repo: `--check`, `--quick`, `--pilot`, `--refresh`, `--combine`, `--reps`, `--filter`, `--seed`, `--n-iter`, `--burn-in`, `--task`/`--ntasks`.

## Caching to `output/`
Three directory variables declared together — `code/sim_run.R:47-49`, mirrored at `sim_int_run.R:43-45` and `sim_kron_study.R:105-107`:
```r
out_dir   &lt;- here::here("output")
cache_dir &lt;- here::here("output", "simulation_kron")
dir.create(cache_dir, showWarnings = FALSE, recursive = TRUE)
```
Per-cell RDS cache keyed by scenario/rep/seed, honouring `--refresh` — `code/sim_kron_study.R:132-138, 195-196`:
```r
run_cell &lt;- function(row) {
  f &lt;- file.path(cache_dir, paste0(row$scenario, "_rep", row$rep, "_s",
                                   row$seed, ".rds"))
  if (!refresh &amp;&amp; file.exists(f)) {
    message("  cached: ", row$scenario, " rep ", row$rep)
    return(readRDS(f))
  }
  ...
  saveRDS(out, f)
  out
}
```
Scenario names via `sprintf` with zero-padded numbers — `sim_kron_study.R:114-116`:
```r
    scenario = sprintf("kron_n%d_sp%03d_f%d", n_acc, round(sparsity * 1000),
                       n_factors),
    seed = BASE_SEED + dplyr::row_number())
```
with a documented seed block per design (`BASE_SEED &lt;- 20280000L   # clear of both other designs`, `:109`).

## Where results CSVs go
Flat into `output/`, named `&lt;pipeline&gt;_&lt;topic&gt;.csv`, written with `readr::write_csv` — `code/sim_kron_study.R:201`:
```r
readr::write_csv(res, file.path(out_dir, "simulation_kron_study.csv"))
```
Existing names: `output/simulation_kron_study.csv`, `output/simulation_int_results.csv`, `output/simulation_int_design.csv`, `output/simulation_results.csv`, `output/simulation_design_effects.csv`, `output/simulation_anova*.csv`, `output/simulation_compare*.csv`, `output/BGLR_variance_components.csv`, `output/megalmm_cv_results.csv`. Date-stamped subdirectories only for cross-validation: `code/cross_validate_combinations.R:70-71`:
```r
out_dir &lt;- here::here("output", "crossval", format(Sys.Date(), "%Y-%m-%d"))
dir.create(out_dir, showWarnings = FALSE, recursive = TRUE)
```
Scripts close with a console report (`cat("\n", strrep("=", 76), ...)` then `print(..., n = 60, width = 200)`, `sim_kron_study.R:209-217`) and a final `message("\nwrote:\n  output/...")` manifest (`megalmm_oat_pea.R:338-340`). Early exit uses `quit(save = "no")` (`sim_kron_study.R:207`).

## Registration obligations for a new script
- If it defines reusable functions with no side effects, mark it `# Sourced, not run.` and add it to `EVAL_SOURCES` in `code/evaluation.R:117-130`; otherwise consider `EVAL_DEFS_FILES` (`:133+`).
- New functions get a `debug()` group in `EVAL_GROUPS` (`code/evaluation.R:41-96`) and a slot in `EVAL_ORDER_SIM` / `EVAL_ORDER_ANALYSIS` (`:99-110`).
- Algebraic identities go in `tests/test_*.R` using `tests/helper.R`'s `check()` / `check_near()` and `load_code()`; the suite is aggregated by `tests/run_all.R`. `tests/test_kronecker.R:7-13` states the house style: oracles must be independent identities, deliberately non-square/non-equal-rank inputs, and negative tests that assert the wrong version is detectably wrong.</result>

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>## 1. Implementation sequence

**Step 1 — new `code/interaction_decomp.R`** (`# Sourced, not run.`, long `# ===` header stating the identity and why no deregression is needed). Write and test this before touching any fitting code; it needs no MCMC.

- `decompose_bilinear(A, Beta, B, centre = TRUE, rank = NULL)` → `list(d, scores, loadings, share, rank)`. Fast path: column-centre `A` and `B`, `qr()` each, `M &lt;- R_a %*% Beta %*% t(R_b)`, `svd(M)`, `scores = Q_a %*% U`, `loadings = Q_b %*% V`.
- `decompose_surface(M, centre = TRUE, rank = NULL)` — dense `svd(interaction_part(M))`. Make this the *reference implementation* and the QR route the fast path, with a test that they agree. This also buys the MegaLMM side for free: no `U_F`/`Lambda` needed (and `U_F` is `NULL` in some runs), and "the same orthogonalising operator applied to both models" becomes literally the same function call.
- `read_beta_draws(prefix, name = "ETA_G_mix", nIter, burnIn, thin, storage_mode, p, traits)` → `[n_keep, p, traits]`, dropping the first `floor(burnIn/thin)` rows and asserting file size `== 24 + (nIter/thin)*p*traits*size` and `!anyNA`.
- `align_to_reference(U, V, d, ref, gap_tol = 0.05)` → signed permutation across well-separated components, **block** orthogonal Procrustes inside near-degenerate blocks; returns diagnostics (`gap_ratio`, `rot_diag`, `switched`).
- `subspace_cors(X, Y)` (canonical correlations via `svd(t(qr.Q(qr(X))) %*% qr.Q(qr(Y)))`), `effective_rank(d)` (participation ratio `(Σd²)²/Σd⁴` plus `n90`), `projection_ceiling(truth_U, A)`.
- `decompose_draws(A, draws, B, rank, reference, centre)` → per-draw `d`, aligned score mean/sd, diagnostics. Never average unaligned scores.

**Step 2 — `code/dge_ige_functions.R:266`.** Add `save_effects = FALSE`, `storage_mode = "double"`. Set `ETA$G_mix$saveEffects &lt;- TRUE` and `$storageMode` only on `G_mix` (BGLR will happily save `G_oat`, `G_pea` and the FIXED trial term too). Return `kron = list(A = kron_A, B = kron_B)`, `mcmc = list(nIter, burnIn, thin)`, and `effects_file = paste0(saveAt_prefix, "ETA_G_mix_beta.bin")`. Document that `saveAt` is a **filename prefix**, not a directory (BGLR does `paste(saveAt, Name, "_beta.bin")`).

**Step 3 — `code/sim_fit.R:123`.** Thread `save_effects`/`saveAt`/`decomp_rank` through `fit_dge_ige`; return the **zero-row-padded bases**, not just the padded surface. This matters: `pad_mat(A Beta B') == pad_rows(A) Beta pad_rows(B)'`, so bilinearity survives padding, but decomposing the *unpadded* surface uses a different centring operator than `score_predictions` does, and the variance shares then aren't shares of the thing that was scored.

**Step 4 — one-cell `--check`** proving the plumbing (see §4) before any sweep.

**Step 5 — new `code/sim_decomp_run.R`.** `here::i_am`, `arg_value()`, flags `--check --pilot --reps --rank --filter --task --ntasks --refresh --combine`. **Reuse `sim_int_grid()` and `SIM_INT_BASE_SEED` unchanged** — same `(scenario, rep, seed)` keys — or the decomposition rows cannot be joined to the existing `r_int` rows and the head-to-head dies. Cache `output/simulation_decomp/&lt;scenario&gt;_rep&lt;k&gt;_s&lt;seed&gt;_v1_decomp.rds`; outputs `output/simulation_decomp_results.csv` (one row per cell/trait/model) and `output/simulation_decomp_spectrum.csv` (long, one row per component).

**Step 6 — `tests/test_decomp.R`** (§4), **`code/evaluation.R`**: add `code/interaction_decomp.R` to `EVAL_SOURCES$simulation` (after `sim_fit.R`) and `$analysis`; `EVAL_GROUPS$sim_decomp &lt;- c("decompose_bilinear","decompose_surface","read_beta_draws","align_to_reference","subspace_cors","effective_rank","projection_ceiling","decompose_draws")`; append `"sim_decomp"` to `EVAL_ORDER_SIM` and to the `sim_pipeline` union.

**Step 7 — `code/scinet/sim_decomp_array.sbatch`**, a copy of `sim_int_array.sbatch` with new names and a corrected hour estimate.

## 2. Risks you haven't named

**The burn-in trap (highest severity, silent).** In `BGLR::Multitrait` the `saveEffects` `writeBin` sits inside `if (iter %% thin == 0)` (deparsed line 332), *not* inside the `(iter &gt; burnIn) &amp; (iter %% thin == 0)` accumulation block (line 346). The header records `nRow = nIter/thin`, and `readBinMatMultitrait` hands back **all** of them, burn-in included. At the current `nIter = 6000, burnIn = 1000, thin = 10` that is 600 rows of which the first 100 are burn-in — not the "400–1000 draws" in your note. Forget the offset and every posterior SD inflates and the spectrum flattens, which is indistinguishable from the §(c) bias you are trying to measure.

**Also verified, so you can rely on it:** `storageMode` defaults to `"double"` (`setLT.BRR_mt`), compression is opt-in (`compressEffects`, default off) and `gzfile` reads uncompressed anyway, `as.vector(beta)` on a `p × traits` matrix is column-major and the reader's `for k in 1:traits { Beta[j,,k] &lt;- readBin(n = p) }` matches it, and `fit$ETA$G_mix$beta` is the posterior *mean* (`$beta &lt;- $post_beta`, line 419). That last fact gives you a decisive test.

**(a) Alignment.** Signed permutation is enough only where the spectrum separates. Near-ties produce a *continuous* rotation of the degenerate block that a signed permutation cannot represent; it will report large posterior SD on individual scores while the subspace is perfectly well determined. Near the noise floor nothing helps. So: make the rotation-invariant quantities primary (the `d_k²` shares and the top-`r` subspace), use block-diagonal Procrustes (full `Q` from `svd(U_ref' U_draw)` within blocks where `(d_k - d_{k+1})/d_1 &lt; 0.05`, signed permutation between blocks), and take the reference from the decomposition of the posterior-**mean** surface, not draw 1. Report four diagnostics: posterior distribution of the gap ratio; mean `|diag(Q)|` (1 = stable); posterior mean principal angles between each draw's top-`r` subspace and the reference (alignment-free); and the label-switch rate. One more: singular values are convex in `M`, so `E[d_k(M)] ≥ d_k(E[M])` — the draw-wise spectrum is systematically flatter and heavier than the spectrum of the mean surface. Report both, and name the mean-surface spectrum as the point estimate. Finally, 500 kept draws at thin 10 on 900 coefficients is thin: record `coda::effectiveSize(d_1)` per cell and mark cells with ESS &lt; 100 uncertified rather than quoting their intervals.

**(b) Disk and memory.** Per fit: 24-byte header + 600 × 900 × 2 × 8 = **8.64 MB** (4.32 MB at `storageMode = "single"`). The sweep is 600 `dge_ige` fits (`fixed_main_effect` only changes MegaLMM), so 5.2 GB / 2.6 GB. Peak memory is trivial (one 8.6 MB array). Real hazards: (i) `saveAt` is a prefix, so pointing it at `output/` in a 20-task array makes every task write `output/ETA_G_mix_beta.bin` and clobber the rest — keep it in the per-process `tempdir()` and decompose in-process; (ii) do **not** cache per-draw score matrices (400 × 5 × 500 × 8 ≈ 9.6 MB per trait per cell, &gt;11 GB); cache per-draw `d` (24 KB), aligned score mean/sd, and diagnostics; (iii) **do** cache the raw `[n_keep, 900, 2]` Beta array per cell — 2.6 GB at single precision buys you the ability to redo every decomposition choice (centred vs raw, rank, alignment method) in seconds instead of re-running a 70-hour refit. That is the single highest-value caching decision here; (iv) a killed job leaves a truncated `.bin` that reads as an NA-filled array with no error, hence the size assertion in `read_beta_draws`.

**(c) Does BRR flatten the spectrum?** Yes, two ways, and they are separable. The prior is one scalar variance over all `q²` coefficients (times a 2×2 UN `Omega` across traits). First, shrinkage that does not scale with signal strength compresses `d_1/d_k` and inflates any share- or participation-ratio-based effective rank. Second, isotropy *in Beta space* means the implied prior on the surface has covariance `Λ_a ⊗ Λ_b`, so the posterior is tilted toward leading GRM eigen-directions irrespective of where the truth sits, and the recovered loadings inherit the GRM spectrum. Measure both: **null calibration** — add `interaction_pct = 0` / `V_I = 0` cells (the generator supports it; `SIM_INT_LEVELS` deliberately has no such level) and record the fitted spectrum under no true interaction; that is your `d_k^null` noise floor, and effective rank must be read against it. If the `n_factors = 1` cells return the same effective rank as the null, the spectrum carries no rank information and the honest deliverable shrinks to the leading direction plus a caveat. **Rank tracking** — regress estimated effective rank on true `n_factors` (1/3/5) and report the slope; slope ≈ 0 with a large intercept *is* the flattening bias, quantified. **Tilt test** — compare recovered loadings' overlap with the leading `G_pea` eigenvectors against the truth's own overlap; systematic excess is the `Λ_a ⊗ Λ_b` tilt. **Sensitivity** — refit one cell with basis columns scaled as `Λ^α`, α ∈ {0.25, 0.5, 1}: same span, different implied surface prior; if the shares move materially they are prior-driven. Report shares on the centred surface only, consistently — centring removes the additive directions where much of the uniform-shrinkage bias lives.

**(d) The `kron_rank` confound, which is worse here than for `r_int`.** Loadings are confined *by construction* to `span(V_b[,1:q])` and scores to `span(V_a[,1:q])`, while the truth's `U`/`Lambda` are `draw_effect(L, 1)` draws with mass on every direction. So there is a hard analytic ceiling on any subspace-recovery number, and it is computable with **no fitting**: the canonical correlations between `span(truth$U_oat)` and `span(kron_A)`. Add that column and report recovery as a *fraction of ceiling*; without it, every number at `kron_rank = 30` is uninterpretable in exactly the way `sim_kron_study.R:1-70` describes. Two consequences: the `n_acc` main effect (30/200 vs 30/400 directions retained) is partly a truncation artefact that only the ceiling column separates; and the decomposition needs its own truncation-free anchor — get it free by adding the decomposition responses to `sim_kron_study.R`'s existing `n_acc = 60/100, ranks = ..., full` design rather than building a second anchor. At `rank = full` the spectrum question becomes purely about shrinkage, so that anchor serves (c) as well.

**Scope risk you haven't named.** `code/BGLR_multi_trait_model.R:94` sets `fit_mix_term &lt;- FALSE`, and when it is TRUE it builds the **exact** combination kernel inline (`Z_mix %*% L_mix`, lines 332–355) rather than calling `fit_producer_associate`. So (i) there is currently no fitted interaction surface for the real experiment at all, (ii) the exact-kernel form is not bilinear and this decomposition does not apply to it, and (iii) Step 2's plumbing does not reach that script. Meanwhile its own guard at line 233 says the mix term is near-inestimable on the real data (&gt;90% of combinations unreplicated). Decide explicitly now whether the deliverable is simulation-only, or whether the production script gets switched to `fit_producer_associate(kron_rank = ...)`.

**Smaller ones.** `grm_basis` caps `k` at the non-null directions, so `ncol(A)` and `ncol(B)` can differ and be below `kron_rank` — assert `ncol(A)*ncol(B) == nrow(beta)` and never assume square. `eigen()` sign is arbitrary-but-deterministic: recomputing `kron_A` post hoc under a different BLAS silently flips scores, so store the bases rather than rebuilding them. Component-level agreement must be reported as `|cor|` (joint score/loading sign is unidentified). And because the generator rescales only `I`, `truth$U %*% t(truth$Lambda) != truth$I_oat`; compute the truth-side spectrum from `truth$I_*` (or rescale `U`/`Lambda` identically) or the shares will look wrong by a constant and be misread as bias.

## 3. Scope: the full sweep is affordable, but not as specified

Correcting the arithmetic: `SIM_INT_LEVELS` is 2 × 5 × 3 × 2 × 2 = 120 scenarios; at 5 reps that is **600 datasets**, and 1200 is the count of *MegaLMM runs* (`fixed_main_effect` crossed), which does not change the `dge_ige` fit. From `output/simulation_results_from_ceres.csv` (`output/simulation_int_results.csv` holds only 2 `dge_ige` rows — the int sweep is 6 of 2400 fits done, essentially unrun), `dge_ige` medians are 17/45/–/147/422 s at n = 200 and 74/224/–/725/2283 s at n = 400 across the sparsity grid; interpolating 0.09 at ≈80 s and ≈390 s gives 142 s and 739 s per dataset, so **73.5 core-hours** for all 600 `dge_ige` refits. MegaLMM at 50 s / 175 s × 2 runs adds **37.5 core-hours**. Total ≈ **111 core-hours**, which is consistent with `sim_int_array.sbatch`'s own "~65 single-core hours" for 360 scenarios. On the existing 20-task array that is ~5.6 h per task — comfortably inside the 12 h wall clock. So the full sweep is practical *on Ceres*; it is not practical serially on the Mac (4.6 days).

What I would still cut, and why. Stage it: **Stage A** is `dge_ige` only (73 h) and delivers the actual product — scores, loadings, posterior intervals, spectrum — with no MegaLMM at all. **Stage B** adds the head-to-head, and there it only needs the cells near the crossover (`sparsity ∈ {0.048, 0.09, 0.16}`), cutting MegaLMM from 37.5 to ~22 h. Within Stage A, the decomposition responses (canonical correlations, share of `d_1`, effective rank) are averages over many dimensions and so have far lower replicate variance than `r_int`; reps 4–5 buy little. And `interaction_pct` is a pure SNR knob that will move everything monotonically. So the nearest thing to the full sweep, if you want it overnight rather than over a day: `interaction_pct = 0.20` only, 3 reps → 60 × 3 = 180 datasets ≈ **33 core-hours**, ~1.7 h per task, plus the `V_I = 0` null cells (cheap: sparse, and they are the calibration you cannot skip). Then run the full 120 × 5 only on the axes where Stage A shows the ranking is close. If you want the literal full sweep, submit it — the number says yes.

## 4. Verification

**`tests/test_decomp.R`, fast tier**, all algebraic, `tol = 1e-12`, with non-square and unequal-rank inputs as `test_kronecker.R` does: reconstruction `scores %*% diag(d) %*% t(loadings) == M` uncentred and `== interaction_part(M)` centred; `crossprod(scores) == I` and `colMeans(scores) == 0` in the centred case (both sides); `sum(d^2) == sum(interaction_part(M)^2)` and shares summing to 1; QR fast path equals `decompose_surface` on `d` and on subspaces; **zero-padding invariance** — decomposing padded bases equals decomposing `pad_mat(M)` (the trap in §1 Step 3, turned into a test); scale invariance — `decompose(c*M)` gives identical scores/loadings and `c*d` (this is what licenses comparing to a truth that was rescaled); `subspace_cors` = 1 on identical spans, 0 on orthogonal ones; `effective_rank` = 1 for rank-1 and = r for r equal values. Two **negative** tests in the house style: `byrow = FALSE` in the Beta reshape must be detectably wrong, and signed-permutation alignment must *fail* on a planted within-block rotation that block-Procrustes recovers.

**Added to `tests/test_fits.R`, slow tier** — the decisive plumbing test: fit a tiny `dge_ige` with `save_effects = TRUE, nIter = 400, burnIn = 100, thin = 10`, read the `.bin`, drop the first 10 rows, and assert `colMeans(draws) == fit$ETA$G_mix$beta` to 1e-10. Because BGLR's returned `beta` *is* the running posterior mean over exactly those draws, this one assertion simultaneously proves the burn-in offset, the byte order, the trait-major layout and the `storageMode`. Second: the mean over per-draw surfaces equals the surface built from the posterior-mean beta (linearity), 1e-10.

**Human end-to-end, under a few minutes:** `Rscript code/sim_decomp_run.R --check` at `n_acc = 120, sparsity = 0.48, n_factors = 1, nIter = 1500, burnIn = 300` (~20 s fit, matching `sim_int_run.R --check`'s scale), written as a positive control that `stop()`s: (1) draws read back and their mean equals the posterior mean; (2) rank-1 truth → `d_1` share &gt; 0.5 and `|canonical correlation|` between recovered oat scores and `truth$U_oat` &gt; 0.8; (3) the 90% posterior interval for the `d_1` share is finite and excludes 0, with `rot_diag` printed; (4) `decompose_surface(interaction_part(megalmm_surface))` shares sum to 1 — the direct demonstration that the fairness fix works, since the raw per-factor shares do not.

In VALIDATE_DESIGN.md, I still find the section on "Why pool size is not the hard question" confusing.  You said you re-wrote it but as far as I can tell it's very similar to what it used to be.  That is, the power_grid.csv file clearly shows increasing power with increasing pool size so that I would _not_ say that design geometry is the dominant question.  It's not irrelevant but of course maximizing power is very important in experiments.  I think the underlying reason for the change in power could be explained more clearly.  On the one hand, the delta_As is affected by the selection intensity: the mean of the selected tail of a normal distribution increases as the percentage selected goes down, but when selection is already intense, the mean starts to shift more slowly as that percentage goes down.  Since we have over 400 oat and pea accessions, going from n=40 to n=20 means going from ~10% to ~5% selected: what is the change in the selected tail mean then (assuming a normal distribution)? # 1. Signature and return value of `build_pools()` (question 3)

`/Users/jj332/Documents/GitRepo/B4I_Fall2026/code/validation_functions.R:218-275`

```r
build_pools &lt;- function(inp, n, pr_quantile = 0.5, tol = 1.0,
                        theta_max = 10, iterations = 60L) {
```

Pipeline: filter `inp$accessions` on `eligible` → `pr_min &lt;- quantile(cand$Pr, pr_quantile)` → filter `Pr &gt;= pr_min` → hard `stop()` if `nrow(cand) &lt; 2 * n` (line 224) → 60-iteration bisection on `theta` in `[0, theta_max]` driven by `sign(.pools_at(cand, n, theta)$dPr)` (lines 240-244) → `res &lt;- .pools_at(cand, n, theta)` (245) → `warning()` if `abs(res$dPr) &gt; tol` (247-252).

Returned list (lines 266-274), 12 elements:

| element | source |
|---|---|
| `pools` | tibble built from `res$plus` / `res$minus` via `bind_rows(mutate(res$plus, pool="As+"), mutate(res$minus, pool="As-"))`, then `transmute(species, pool, acc, Pr, As, n_partners, n_plots)`, `arrange(pool, desc(As))` — lines 254-260 |
| `n` | the argument |
| `theta` | last bisection midpoint |
| `pr_min` | the quantile threshold |
| `n_candidates` | `nrow(cand)` after both filters |
| `dAs` | `res$dAs` = `mean(plus$As) - mean(minus$As)` |
| `dPr` | `res$dPr` = `mean(plus$Pr) - mean(minus$Pr)` |
| `mean_Pr_plus`, `mean_Pr_minus` | `mean(res$plus$Pr)`, `mean(res$minus$Pr)` |
| `mean_As_plus`, `mean_As_minus` | `mean(res$plus$As)`, `mean(res$minus$As)` |
| `var_blup_within` | `mean(c(var(res$plus$As), var(res$minus$As)))` — line 264 |
| `sigma2_within` | `inp$PEV_As + var_blup_within` |

Everything except `n`, `pr_min`, `n_candidates` and `theta` is computed from `res$plus`/`res$minus`.

`.pools_at()` (lines 194-207) returns exactly four elements: `plus`, `minus`, `dAs`, `dPr`. It is the only place the selection index lives:

```r
  plus &lt;- cand |&gt; dplyr::mutate(score = As + theta * Pr) |&gt;
    dplyr::slice_max(score, n = n, with_ties = FALSE)
  minus &lt;- cand |&gt; dplyr::filter(!acc %in% plus$acc) |&gt;
    dplyr::mutate(score = As - theta * Pr) |&gt;
    dplyr::slice_min(score, n = n, with_ties = FALSE)
```

Note it requires `cand` to carry `acc`, `Pr`, `As`; `build_pools`' `transmute` additionally requires `n_partners` and `n_plots` (line 258-259). Any replacement must preserve those column requirements.

The prose contract above the function, `code/validation_functions.R:176-191`, states the algorithm (Lagrange multiplier, bisection, "Disjointness is enforced by building the As+ pool first and excluding its members from the As- candidate set") and would need rewriting too. Lines 230-239 are an in-code comment specifically about the bisection's non-monotonicity.

# 2. Every caller (question 1)

### A. `code/validate_pool_selection.R:75` — the production driver
```r
  bp &lt;- build_pools(inp, n, pr_quantile = pr_quantile, tol = pr_tolerance)
```
The only caller that passes `tol`. Reads: `bp$pr_min` and `bp$n_candidates` (:80-81), `bp$dAs` (:82), `bp$dPr` (:83), `bp$mean_Pr_plus`/`mean_Pr_minus` (:85-87), `bp$sigma2_within` and `bp$var_blup_within` (:88-90), `bp$pools` for the two `stopifnot` guards (:92-98). Then `purrr::map(built, "pools")` (:102) and the `pool_summary` tibble (:104-112) which reads `bp$n`, `bp$n_candidates`, `bp$pr_min`, `bp$theta`, `bp$dAs`, `bp$dPr`, `bp$mean_As_plus`, `bp$mean_As_minus`, `bp$mean_Pr_plus`, `bp$mean_Pr_minus`, `bp$sigma2_within`. Writes `pools.csv`, `pool_summary.csv`, `vintage.csv` (:114-116).

Its own in-script assertions (:92-98) are algorithm-agnostic:
```r
    "pools are not disjoint" =
      length(intersect(bp$pools$acc[bp$pools$pool == "As+"],
                       bp$pools$acc[bp$pools$pool == "As-"])) == 0,
    "a pool member falls below the partner filter" =
      all(bp$pools$n_partners &gt;= min_partners)
```
A new index that does not explicitly exclude the As+ members (e.g. top-n / bottom-n on one axis) still satisfies disjointness only if the two slices cannot collide — this `stopifnot` is the guard that would catch it.

Note the `theta` column in `pool_summary.csv` is written unconditionally; if the new algorithm has no single `theta` (e.g. a grid scan returning a chosen θ, or no θ at all), `:106` breaks.

## Would break or require rewriting if the selection index changes

- **`:63-64` — directly references `.pools_at` with the current index:**
```r
  check(abs(bp$dPr) &lt;= abs(.pools_at(cand_n, n, 0)$dPr) + 1e-8,
        sprintf("constraining never widens the producer gap at n = %d", n))
```
This asserts the achieved `|dPr|` is no worse than θ = 0. A single-index rule still has a θ = 0 reference point, but `.pools_at`'s signature/semantics must survive for the call to work.

- **`:65-68` — asserts the returned `theta` brackets a zero crossing. This is a direct assertion about the bisection:**
```r
  check(.pools_at(cand_n, n, bp$theta * 0.5)$dPr &lt;= 0 &amp;&amp;
          .pools_at(cand_n, n, bp$theta * 2)$dPr &gt;= 0,
        sprintf("the solution brackets the zero crossing at n = %d", n))
```
If the new rule targets "smallest feasible θ" or "feasible θ maximising ΔAs" instead of a zero crossing, this is false by construction and must be replaced.

- **`:88-101` — an entire block that pins properties of `dPr(θ)` under the current two-sided index:**
```r
theta_seq &lt;- seq(0, 5, by = 0.25)
dPr_by_theta &lt;- purrr::map_dbl(theta_seq, \(th) .pools_at(cand_m, 20, th)$dPr)
check(dPr_by_theta[1] &lt; 0, "selecting on As alone leaves the As+ pool short on Pr")
check(dPr_by_theta[length(dPr_by_theta)] &gt; 0,
      "and a large theta overshoots, so a crossing exists to bisect on")
check(stats::cor(theta_seq, dPr_by_theta) &gt; 0.8,
      "dPr rises with theta in trend, which is what the bisection needs")
check(any(diff(dPr_by_theta) &lt; 0),
      "but NOT monotonically -- so the crossing found is local, not optimal")
```
Note the θ range `[0, 5]` and the cor &gt; 0.8 threshold are tuned to the current index; `docs/B4I_followups.md` says the single-index rule reaches tolerance at θ ≈ 0.175 for oat vs 0.61 now, so the shape of `dPr(θ)` over `[0,5]` changes substantially. The last check is deliberately a record that monotonicity is false — it is the test most tightly bound to the current algorithm.

- **`:103-107` — a cross-`n` ordering, not a hard number, but it is an empirical property of the selection rule:**
```r
d10 &lt;- suppressWarnings(build_pools(inp, 10))$dAs
d30 &lt;- suppressWarnings(build_pools(inp, 30))$dAs
check(d10 &gt; d30, "a larger pool gives a smaller contrast")
```
VALIDATION_DESIGN.md:349-350 already records that the achieved contrast is *not* monotone in n for pea under the current rule, so this holds only for this synthetic fixture. A different index changes the achieved contrast at every n and could flip it.

- **`:109-112` warning-message coupling** — asserts the exact phrase:
```r
check(!is.null(w) &amp;&amp; grepl("Pr constraint could not be met", w),
      "an unmeetable producer tolerance raises a warning that says so")
```
The string must be preserved (currently emitted at `validation_functions.R:250`). Also `build_pools(inp, 10, tol = 0.01)` must *still* fail to meet 0.01 under the new rule for this to produce a warning at all — a better index might meet it and silently flip this to a FAIL.

## Hard-coded numeric results

**There are none.** No test compares a pool member, a `dAs`, a `dPr` or a `theta` against a literal. The only literals are tolerances (`1e-8`, `0.8`), the `theta_seq` grid bounds (`0, 5, by = 0.25`), the pool sizes (`10, 20, 30`), the `tol` values (`0.01`, `5`), `n = 100` for the refusal, and `n_partners &gt;= 8`. `tests/helper.R:8-12` states this explicitly as policy: *"most of them compare the code against a quantity known independently ... rather than against a value the code produced earlier. A test that only pins today's output freezes bugs as well as behaviour."*

`tests/README.md:38` also records the naive-rule oracle: *"singular; the naive Pr+As / Pr−As rule must produce overlapping pools)"*. `tests/README.md:22`: `| `test_validation.R` | pool construction, the field design's balance, the power formula | 2 |`.

# 4. Persistence and cross-run comparison (question 4)

### `pool_diff()` — `code/validation_functions.R:282-307`
Joins on `c("species", "acc")` only and classifies:
```r
    dplyr::mutate(status = dplyr::case_when(
      is.na(pool_old)          ~ "entered",
      is.na(pool_new)          ~ "left",
      pool_old != pool_new     ~ "switched pool",
      TRUE                     ~ "retained"
    ))
```
Returns `list(detail = ..., summary = ...)` where `summary` is a `count(species, status)` pivoted wide.

### How the previous vintage is found — `code/validate_pool_selection.R:129-137`
```r
previous &lt;- list.dirs(out_root, recursive = FALSE) |&gt;
  setdiff(out_dir) |&gt;
  (\(d) d[file.exists(file.path(d, "pools.csv"))])() |&gt;
  sort()

if (length(previous) &gt; 0) {
  prev_dir &lt;- utils::tail(previous, 1)
  prev &lt;- readr::read_csv(file.path(prev_dir, "pools.csv"), show_col_types = FALSE)
  dif &lt;- pool_diff(prev, pools)
```
Purely a lexicographic sort of directory names — there is no algorithm/version marker anywhere in `pools.csv` or `pool_summary.csv`, so a new algorithm's first run will diff against the old algorithm's output with no signal that the comparison is apples-to-oranges. (Side note on the existing sort: `output/validation/` currently holds `2026-09-24`, `2026-09-27`, `2026-10-02`, `2026-10-02_1`, and `"2026-10-02_1"` sorts after `"2026-10-02"`, so `tail(sort(...), 1)` picks the `_1` suffix directory.)

### What the churn report says — `code/validate_pool_selection.R:139-158`
Writes `pool_diff.csv`, prints `dif$summary`, then:
```r
  churn &lt;- dif$detail |&gt; dplyr::filter(status != "retained") |&gt;
    dplyr::count(species, name = "n_changed")
  retained &lt;- dif$detail |&gt; dplyr::filter(status == "retained") |&gt;
    dplyr::count(species, name = "n_retained")
  ...
                  pct_retained = round(100 * n_retained / (n_retained + n_changed))) |&gt;
  ...
  cat("\nA pool that retains well under 70% of its members across a data\n",
      "vintage is a warning about the estimates, not about the design.\n", sep = "")
```
That 70% rule is the thing an algorithm change invalidates for one run.

### Second place the churn is advertised — `code/validate_refresh.R:184-195`
```r
  vints &lt;- sort(list.dirs(vint_dir, recursive = FALSE, full.names = FALSE))
  ...
    cat("\nThe pool churn between the last two vintages is in\n  ",
        file.path(vint_dir, utils::tail(vints, 1), "pool_diff.csv"), "\n",
        "A pool retaining well under 70% of its members across one data",
        " vintage is\na warning about the EFFECT ESTIMATES, not about the",
        " design.\n", sep = "")
```

### On-disk vintages
`output/validation/{2026-09-24, 2026-09-27, 2026-10-02, 2026-10-02_1}/`. `pools.csv` + `pool_summary.csv` in all four; `pool_diff.csv` in all but `2026-09-24`. `pool_summary.csv` header: `species,n_per_pool,n_candidates,pr_threshold,theta,dAs,dPr,mean_As_plus,mean_As_minus,mean_Pr_plus,mean_Pr_minus,sigma2_within,PEV,reliability_As`. (`2026-10-02_1`: oat θ = 0.830, dAs = 13.014; pea θ = 1.884, dAs = 13.723.)

# 5. Does `validate_crossval.R` build pools independently? (question 5)

Yes, at two places. Note the line numbers are **225** and **421**, not 224/379.

### Call 1: the rehearsal, `code/validate_crossval.R:220-227`
```r
    # same constrained rule the real selection uses. PEV is set to 0 because
    # the rehearsal only needs the pools, not their power.
    fake_inp &lt;- list(species = species,
                     accessions = dplyr::mutate(cand, eligible = TRUE),
                     PEV_As = 0)
    bp &lt;- tryCatch(build_pools(fake_inp, n, pr_quantile = pr_quantile),
                   error = function(e) NULL)
    if (is.null(bp)) return(NULL)
```
Independent inputs: `cand` is built from the **training-fold BLUPs** (`blups`), joined to training-fold partner counts and restricted to accessions present in the held-out trial (`:212-215`), with `n &lt;- min(rehearsal_max_n, floor(nrow(cand) / 3))` and `if (n &lt; 5) return(NULL)` (`:217-218`). `rehearsal_max_n &lt;- 25L` (`:76`). Default `tol = 1.0` is used (not `pr_tolerance`). Note `fake_inp` has no `sigma2_e`, `rel_As` etc. — only `species`, `accessions`, `PEV_As` — so a changed `build_pools` must not start reading other `inp` fields, or this call breaks.

Reads: `bp$pools` (`:229`, selecting `acc, pool`) and `bp$dAs` (`:245, :248`). Produces per-fold `predicted_contrast = bp$dAs`, `realised_contrast`, `contrast_se`, `lambda_pool = realised / bp$dAs`, `p_one_sided` (`:243-251`).

### Call 2: the power statement, `code/validate_crossval.R:421`
```r
  bp  &lt;- build_pools(inp, n_pool[[sp]], pr_quantile = pr_quantile)
  ...
  pw &lt;- function(lam, int) contrast_power(
    bp$dAs, bp$sigma2_within, n_pool[[sp]], inp$sigma2_e, p_loc * n_loc,
    lambda = lam, sided = 1, interaction_frac = int, n_loc = n_loc)$power
```
Reads `bp$dAs`, `bp$sigma2_within`. Feeds `power_at_lambda`, `power_with_interaction`, `power_at_lambda_lo` in `crossval_power.csv` (`:433-444`) and the verdict text at `:456-465`.

### Would a change in pool composition alter λ / interaction_frac?

**No for the headline λ and interaction_frac.** Both come from `calibration`, a plot-level regression that never touches pools — `code/validate_crossval.R:149-161`:
```r
    m &lt;- stats::lm(stats::reformulate(c(as_col, partner_pr_col), response), data = dat)
    ...
      lambda = s[as_col, "Estimate"], lambda_se = s[as_col, "Std. Error"],
```
and `interaction_frac` is purely the across-fold spread of those slopes — `:342`:
```r
      interaction_frac = stats::sd(lambda) / abs(mean(lambda)),
```
The jackknife (`:372-387`: `lambda_all`, `interaction_all`, `lambda_without`, `interaction_without`, `narrows_by`, `raises_lambda_by`) is likewise built from `same$lambda`, so it is pool-independent. The informative/uninformative split at `:323-324` uses `response_sd`, also pool-independent.

**Yes for the rehearsal columns and the power columns.** `lambda_pool`, `predicted_contrast`, `realised_contrast`, `contrast_se`, `p_one_sided`, and the aggregates `lambda_pool_mean` / `lambda_pool_sd` (`:344-345`) all move with pool composition. So does everything in `crossval_power.csv`, because `bp$dAs` and `bp$sigma2_within` change. The fold-by-fold print at `:312-316` includes `lambda_pool, realised_contrast, predicted_contrast`.

Practical consequence: λ stays put, so `power_at_lambda` moves only through `dAs`/`sigma2_within` — and per `docs/B4I_followups.md:630-633` that is +9.1 points for oat and +19.1 for pea.

# 6. Documentation that states the algorithm or quotes its numbers (question 6)

## `VALIDATION_DESIGN.md` — the main burden

**Section 2, "Building the pools" (lines 51-96).** This is where the algorithm is specified.

- `:53-66` "The obvious rule does not work", including a hard-coded overlap table:
```
| n per pool | oat accessions in *both* pools |
| 10 | 1 |  | 20 | 8 |  | 30 | 13 |  | 50 | 20 |
```
  and `:66` *"At n = 30 that is 13 of 30 shared, which guts the contrast."*
- `:70-75` the three constraints.
- **`:77-79` the algorithm statement — must be rewritten:**
```
Implemented with a Lagrange multiplier on `Pr`, pushed in opposite directions
for the two pools and bisected until the producer difference crosses zero
(`build_pools()` in `code/validation_functions.R`).
```
- `:81-85` candidate filter and *"leaving 265 oat and 254 pea"*.
- **`:87-96` "What it delivers" — hand-written numbers, NOT inside a GENERATED block:**
```
| species | n per pool | ΔAs (g/m²) | ΔPr | mean Pr, As+ | mean Pr, As− | population mean Pr |
| oat | 20 | **14.3** | 0.15 | 14.0 | 13.8 | 0.06 |
| pea | 30 | **16.5** | 0.01 | 10.5 | 10.5 | 0.26 |
```
  `:94-96` *"The producer constraint is essentially free: it costs about 1 g/m² of associate contrast and buys two pools whose producer means differ by less than 0.2 g/m² while both sit 10–14 g/m² above the population mean."*

**Section 5 (lines 261-356) — the pool-size argument, built on achieved contrasts.**
- `:267-273` power-by-n table (oat 0.654→0.724, pea 0.414→0.695) and `:275-276` *"Oat gains about 7 points from n = 10 to n = 50; **pea gains 28**."*
- `:285-294` tail-mean arithmetic, *"after the Pr filter below there are 221 oat and 212 pea candidates"*, *"Halving n from 40 to 20 therefore buys a contrast about **1.24×** larger."*
- `:317-326` *"430 eligible oat become 221, 407 pea become 212"*, *"×1.168 at p = 0.09 against ×1.130 at p = 0.047"*.
- `:328-333` *"the two pools must have nearly equal mean Pr (within 1.0 g/m²), and Pr and As are negatively correlated — −0.51 across all accessions, −0.27 for oat and −0.40 for pea among the candidates"*.
- `:337-347` the "% of theory" table (oat n=10: 27.8/18.3/66%; n=20: 23.8/15.2/64%; n=40: 19.2/13.6/71%; n=50: 17.6/13.0/74%) and *"The constraints cost a quarter to a third of the achievable contrast throughout"*, *"the achieved contrast falls by only 29% from n = 10 to n = 50 where theory says 37%"*.
- **`:349-356` names the algorithm again and is the exact sentence the change supersedes:**
```
Pea is noisier still — its achieved contrast is 44–65% of theory and not
monotone in *n*. The pool builder is not a greedy search, as an earlier version
of this sentence said; it is a Lagrangian selection index bisected on one
multiplier, which is the right family of method. But it is leaving a large
amount on the table, for reasons measured in
[docs/B4I_followups.md](docs/B4I_followups.md) item 13 — enough that pea's curve
should be read as a trend rather than cell by cell, and enough that these power
figures are a floor rather than an estimate.
```

## `docs/B4I_followups.md:610-660` — item 13, the pre-written diagnosis
This file already contains the full analysis of the current index and the proposed replacement, including measured before/after numbers. Key lines:
- `:614-616` *"`.pools_at()` (`code/validation_functions.R:194`) scores the As⁺ pool by `As + θ·Pr` and the As⁻ pool by `As − θ·Pr`, then `build_pools()` bisects θ until the pools' mean Pr difference crosses zero."*
- `:617-620` *"Scoring the As⁻ pool by `As − θ·Pr` *rewards* high Pr in that pool, so raising θ drags **both** pools toward high Pr rather than equalising them ... it takes a large θ to get there — 0.61 for oat."*
- `:622-625` the proposed single index and *"It reaches the same tolerance at θ = 0.175 for oat"*.
- `:627-633` the measured comparison table on the 2026-10-03 data (oat 15.24 → 19.80, power 0.691 → 0.782; pea 18.01 → 25.58, power 0.656 → 0.847).
- `:640-645` the two smaller problems (bisection targets ΔPr = 0 when tolerance is ±1.0; *"A 4,001-point scan finds **three** sign changes for oat, so bisection can land on the wrong crossing"*).
- `:647-649` *"The fix, in order of value: use one index for both pools; scan θ rather than bisect; among feasible θ take the one maximising ΔAs rather than the one zeroing ΔPr."*
- **`:658-660` — the exact risk you asked about:**
```
**Not yet implemented.** It changes pool membership, so it changes who is in the
validation experiment and makes `pool_diff` churn against the previous vintage
meaningless for one run. That is a decision, not a refactor.
```
This whole item would need to move from "unsettled" to settled/done.

Summary of the useful signal:
- **Phenology (oat heading + pea flowering): plot-level, 8 of 9 trials, ~100% of plots.** The strongest non-yield asset in the data.

- **`B4I_2026_IA` has no phenology, height, lodging or establishment at all** — yields, grain weight, test weight, moisture only.

## A2. What is carried into `output/B4I_intercrop_pheno.rds`

`code/assemble_B4I_phenotypes.R` hard-codes exactly two traits:

```r
oat_yield_trait &lt;- "Grain yield - g/m2|CO_350:0000260"
pea_yield_trait &lt;- "Pea Grain Yield - g/m2|CO_xxx:0003008"
```
and filters `observationVariableName %in% c(oat_yield_trait, pea_yield_trait)`. **Everything else in the 38-trait matrix is dropped at assembly.** There is no trait loop, no trait argument, no settings vector of traits — it is two scalars and an `if_else`.

Columns of `output/B4I_intercrop_pheno.rds` — 3,575 rows × **13** columns:

```
trialDbId, studyYear, studyName, observationUnitDbId, observationUnitName,
germplasmName, intercropGermplasmName, repNumber, blockNumber,
pea_yield, oat_yield, oat_renamed, pea_renamed
```

Non-yield columns: there are **no other phenotype columns at all**. The nine non-yield/non-flag columns are identifiers and design factors (`trialDbId`, `studyYear`, `studyName`, `observationUnitDbId`, `observationUnitName`, `germplasmName`, `intercropGermplasmName`, `repNumber`, `blockNumber` — all `chr` except `studyYear` `int`); `oat_renamed`/`pea_renamed` are logical bookkeeping flags from the analysis-name substitution. All of these are complete (zero NA) in every trial, so "missing per trial" for non-yield columns is vacuous — there is nothing there to be missing. Plot counts per trial: AL 392, IA 387, IL 400, ND 400, NY 398 (2025); IA 394, IL 400, ND 396, NY 408 (2026).

Note the README (line 200) still says "2,371 plots"; the current file has 3,575 (the 2026 trials were added).

The trait data itself is not lost — `data/B4I_observations.rds` is 158,740 rows × 11 columns (`trialDbId, trialName, observationUnitDbId, observationUnitName, germplasmDbId, germplasmName, observationVariableDbId, observationVariableName, value, season, observationTimeStamp`) and covers all 37 trials including every trait above. Adding a trait is a matter of widening the pivot in `assemble_B4I_phenotypes.R`, not of re-downloading.
