# B) How the repo already computes trait BLUEs

All of it is in `/Users/jj332/Documents/GitRepo/B4I_Fall2026/code/megalmm_build_inputs.R`. Two functions:

### `pea_blue(plot_data, value_col)` — the model
```r
fit &lt;- stats::lm(stats::reformulate(c("studyName", "peaAcc"), response = value_col), data = x)
```
- **Package: base `stats::lm`.** No lme4, no ASReml, no sommer. Both terms **fixed**, additive, no interaction, no block, no spatial term, no weights.
- **Trials:** `studyName` enters as a fixed factor. The BLUE is then recovered by *marginalising over trials* rather than by reading a coefficient: it builds `expand_grid(peaAcc = levels, studyName = levels)`, `predict()`s the full balanced grid, and averages the fitted values within `peaAcc`. The comment states the intent: "an accession seen only in a high-yielding trial is not credited with that trial's mean." Because the model is additive this is estimable even for a pea seen in one trial, and the result is a least-squares mean on a balanced trial set.
- **Single-trial fallback:** `if (nlevels(x$studyName) &lt; 2)` it returns the plain accession mean.
- **Missing handling:** rows with `is.na(value)` or `is.na(peaAcc)` are filtered first.

### `pea_trait_blues(obs_file, cache_dir, trait_names, name_file)` — the driver
- Reads `data/B4I_observations.rds` and joins to observation units assembled from **every** `output/trial_cache/units_*.rds` file (`units_6821, 6822, 6823, 6824, 6881, 6954, 6997, 7002, 7014` — i.e. all nine B4I trials). It maps `intercropGermplasmName → peaAcc`, then applies `output/pea_analysis_names.csv` so BLUEs key to the collapsed analysis names used as matrix columns.
- Loops over `c("pea_yield", "flower", "maturity")`, skipping any trait with zero rows, and `full_join`s the three into one table.
- Trait names it knows:
```r
trait_names &lt;- c(
  oat_yield = "Grain yield - g/m2|CO_350:0000260",
  pea_yield = "Pea Grain Yield - g/m2|CO_xxx:0003008",
  flower    = "Pea Flowering Date - 10% - Julian Day|CO_xxx:0003014",
  maturity  = "Pea Maturity Date - Julian Day|CO_xxx:0003015")
```
`oat_yield` is listed but **never used** by `pea_trait_blues` — the loop is hard-coded to the three pea traits.

### Missing-trial handling, and what the AL note actually means
```r
phenology_trait &lt;- "flower"   # "flower" or "maturity"
```
with the comment: "Flowering and maturity BLUEs correlate 0.68, so using both adds little; flowering is the default because it was scored in all six trials while maturity was not scored in AL." The driver prints the realised correlation (I confirmed **0.679**). In practice the "missing in AL" handling is implicit — maturity simply has fewer plots feeding its `lm`; it is not excluded, and because every pea appears in several trials the stored `blues` table has **423 rows with zero NA in all three columns** (`pea_yield`, `flower`, `maturity`). So the AL gap costs precision, not coverage, and switching `phenology_trait &lt;- "maturity"` would work today.

A separate missing-value path does exist downstream: peas that are matrix columns but have no BLUE take the column mean (`tidyr::replace_na(x, mean(x, na.rm = TRUE))`) with a count reported as "N pea environment(s) had no BLUE and took the mean".

### What it writes
`output/megalmm_inputs_{raw,centered,standardized}.rds`, each a list of `fill, Y, G_oat, G_pea, X_Env, X_Env_groups, blues, settings`. The BLUEs enter as the phenotypic block of `X_Env`:
- `X_Env = cbind(intercept = 1, scale(cbind(pea_yield, &lt;phenology&gt;)), pea-GRM eigenvectors)`
- `X_Env_groups = c(1, rep(2, 2), rep(3, n_eig))` so MegaLMM's ARD prior shrinks the intercept, the two phenotypic covariates, and the markers as three separate groups — explicitly so "98 eigenvectors cannot swamp the two phenotypic covariates by sheer number."
- Realised in `output/megalmm_inputs_centered.rds`: `Y` is 237 oat × 222 pea; `X_Env` columns begin `intercept, pea_yield, flower, PC1, PC2, …`; `settings$n_eigenvectors = 58`, `eigen_variance = 0.80`, `min_obs_per_env = 3`, `min_obs_per_oat = 2`. The full 423-row `blues` table (including the unused `maturity` column) is saved inside each `.rds`.

### Factored, or inline?
**Inline in the driver script.** `pea_blue()` and `pea_trait_blues()` are defined in `code/megalmm_build_inputs.R` itself, not in a shared functions file. The repo's shared function files are `code/dge_ige_functions.R`, `code/curation_functions.R`, `code/t3_functions.R`, `code/validation_functions.R` — none contains BLUE code (a repo-wide grep for `blue|BLUE` outside `megalmm_build_inputs.R` hits only `code/evaluation.R`).

They are *reachable* without being reusable: `code/evaluation.R` lists them in `EVAL_GROUPS$matrix` (`"scale_within_trial", "build_matrix", "trim_matrix", "grm_eigenvectors", "pea_blue", "pea_trait_blues"`) and pulls them out of the driver via `eval_defs("code/megalmm_build_inputs.R")`, which evaluates only function definitions and literal settings and skips anything that reads a file or fits a model. So the functions can be inspected interactively, but nothing else in the pipeline can `source()` them. There is also **no unit test**: `tests/` (`test_curation.R`, `test_fits.R`, `test_trial_qc.R`, `test_grm.R`, …) never mentions `pea_blue`.

### Anything equivalent for oat traits?
**No.** `pea_blue` is hard-named for peas (`peaAcc` is a literal column name in the function body, and the loop is over the three pea traits). No script computes an oat height, heading, maturity or lodging BLUE anywhere. The oat side is only ever the response: oat yield enters MegaLMM as the matrix cells via `scale_within_trial()`, which does within-trial centering/standardizing of `oat_yield`, not a BLUE.

Generalising `pea_blue` to oat traits is small — the only pea-specific thing is the `peaAcc` literal, and `pea_trait_blues` already fetches arbitrary `observationVariableName`s and maps analysis names for either species. The real work is (i) parameterising the grouping column, (ii) extending `assemble_B4I_phenotypes.R` past the two yields, and (iii) deciding what to do about the subplot-level height in 2025 IA/ND/NY.

---

# C) `code/curate_trials.R` — the `--traits` flag

```r
TRIAL_QC_TRAITS &lt;- strsplit(arg_value("--traits", "oat_yield,pea_yield"), ",")[[1]]
```

- **Default: `oat_yield,pea_yield`** — note the header's usage line `--traits oat_yield,pea_yield,oat_biomass` is **aspirational**. `oat_biomass` does not exist as a column in `B4I_intercrop_pheno.rds`, is not produced by `assemble_B4I_phenotypes.R`, and appears nowhere else in the repo (that header line is its only occurrence). Passing it today triggers the graceful skip: `"not in the phenotype file, skipped: oat_biomass"`.
- **What it can screen on:** anything numeric already in `output/B4I_intercrop_pheno.rds` — so, as the file stands, only `oat_yield` and `pea_yield`. The settings-block comment is explicit: *"TRIAL_QC_TRAITS is the line to edit when the trials start carrying more than the two yields. Anything numeric in output/B4I_intercrop_pheno.rds can go here; a trait absent from a given trial is reported as such rather than failing it."* The gate is `have &lt;- intersect(TRIAL_QC_TRAITS, names(pheno))`, with an error only if **none** match.
- **The model per trial × trait** (no GRM, deliberately — "borrowing strength through a GRM would partly manufacture the answer"):
  `trait ~ (1 | g) + (1 | ic) [+ (1 | bl)]` via `lme4::lmer`, with `g = germplasmName`, `ic = intercropGermplasmName`, `bl = blockNumber`; **the block term is included only if `nlevels(bl) &gt; 1`** because four of the trials carry a single block. Bail-outs: `n_obs &lt; 10`, non-finite or non-positive mean, or `try`-error on the fit.
- **Diagnostics per trial × trait:** `n_plots, n_obs, pct_missing, mean, sd, cv, var_geno, var_partner, var_resid, repeat_geno, repeat_partner, singular, fitted, rel_mean` where `repeat_geno = var_geno/(var_geno+var_resid)`, `repeat_partner = var_partner/(var_partner+var_resid)`, and `rel_mean = mean / median(mean across trials)` — computed within trait, so each trait sets its own scale and unitless traits like Julian days or 0–9 ratings would work without new thresholds.
- **Other flags and defaults:** `--min-h2 0.05` (`min_repeat`), `--max-cv 0.75`, `--min-rel-mean 0.20`, `--min-plots 50`, `--max-missing 0.25`. Flags: `model_failed, too_few_plots, missing_data, high_cv, low_mean, low_repeatability`. ≥2 flags or zero genotypic variance = `fail`; 1 flag = `review`; a trial fails if any of its traits fails.
- **Outputs:** `output/trial_diagnostics.csv` (one row per trial × trait; `cut -f2` confirms only `oat_yield` and `pea_yield` present) and `output/trial_qc.csv`. Manual overrides come from `data/trial_qc_manual.csv`, which wins and is reported loudly.
- Current verdict in `output/trial_qc.csv`: `B4I_2025_AL` fail/`keep=FALSE` (high_cv; low_mean; low_repeatability); `B4I_2025_IA` fail but manually overridden to `keep=TRUE`; `B4I_2026_IA` review (low_repeatability), kept; the other six pass. Consumed by `apply_trial_qc()` in `code/dge_ige_functions.R:234`, which is called by `code/BGLR_multi_trait_model.R:127`, `code/summarise_experiment.R:72` and `b4i_plot_table()` — but **not** by `code/megalmm_build_inputs.R`, which reads the raw pheno, so the pea BLUEs currently include the AL crop-failure trial.

---

# What there is to build on for a trait benchmark

**Already there and directly reusable:**
1. `pea_blue()` / `pea_trait_blues()` in `code/megalmm_build_inputs.R` — a working trial-adjusted BLUE pipeline that already handles analysis-name collapsing, the single-trial degenerate case, and absent-trait skipping, and whose output already feeds a covariate design matrix with its own ARD shrinkage group (`X_Env_groups == 2`). Pea flowering BLUE is the existing proof that a phenology covariate can be fitted and used.
2. `code/curate_trials.R` is already trait-generic — variance-component repeatability per trial × trait on relative rather than absolute scales. Point `--traits` at a new height/heading column and it works as-is.
3. `data/B4I_observations.rds` already holds every trait listed in A1 for all nine trials. No new download needed to prototype.
4. `output/trial_cache/units_*.rds` already resolves the pea half of every plot (`intercropGermplasmName`) for all nine trials, which is what makes any plot-level partner-trait analysis possible.

**Gaps that would have to be closed:**
1. `assemble_B4I_phenotypes.R` is hard-wired to two trait scalars. Nothing numeric beyond the yields reaches any downstream script.
2. No oat-side BLUE machinery of any kind; `pea_blue` is pea-named in its body.
3. The BLUE code is not in a shared functions file and is untested, so a second consumer means either `source()`ing a driver script or lifting the code.
4. Height — the trait most obviously relevant to an associate (competition) effect — is the *least* usable historically: plot-level in only 5 of 9 trials, subplot-level in 3, absent in 1, with 2–4 inconsistent timepoints and two different trait identities. Heading/flowering is the trait with near-complete plot-level coverage (8 of 9 trials) and is the natural benchmark to regress against. Biomass is not a real option retrospectively (≤19 observations, one trial).
5. `megalmm_build_inputs.R` does not apply trial QC before computing BLUEs, so a new-trait BLUE would inherit AL 2025 unless that is changed.
6. Nothing in `VALIDATION_DESIGN.md` mentions height, flowering or maturity at all — the validation trial design is currently yield-only, so recording these on every plot would be new scope rather than a documented plan.</result>

### The whitelist is currently the binding constraint

`output/trial_qc.csv` keeps 8 of 9 trials (only `B4I_2025_AL` has `keep = FALSE`), including `B4I_2026_IA`, `B4I_2026_ND`, `B4I_2026_NY`. The `trials` vector at `:72-79` **omits all three 2026 non-IL trials and still lists `B4I_2025_AL`**. So:

- `B4I_2025_AL` is dropped twice (QC at `:127`, then absent from the fit anyway — no, it *is* in `trials`, so QC is what drops it).
- `B4I_2026_IA`, `B4I_2026_ND`, `B4I_2026_NY` pass QC but are silently excluded by the whitelist at `:181`.

Removing the whitelist would therefore change the data that reaches the fit (3 extra trials).

### Per-seed loop structure

`:369-385`:

```r
fit_one &lt;- function(seed_value) {
  message("\n--- fitting, seed ", seed_value, " ---")
  set.seed(seed_value)
  BGLR::Multitrait(
    y         = Y,
    ETA       = ETA,
    intercept = FALSE,
    resCov    = list(df0 = 4, S0 = NULL, type = "UN"),
    nIter     = nIter,
    burnIn    = burnIn,
    thin      = thin,
    saveAt    = file.path(out_dir, paste0("BGLR_seed_", seed_value, "_")),
    verbose   = FALSE
  )
}

fits &lt;- rlang::set_names(purrr::map(seeds, fit_one), seeds)
```

Note: **this script does not call `fit_producer_associate()`** — it builds its own ETA inline (`:351-363`) and calls `BGLR::Multitrait()` directly. `saveAt` is per-seed; `saveEffects` is **not** passed on any term. All four fits are held in memory simultaneously in `fits`.

ETA (`:351-363`), in final order `trial, block, G_pea, G_oat`:

```r
ETA &lt;- list(
  trial = list(X = incTrials,      model = "FIXED"),
  G_pea = list(X = Z_pea %*% L_pea, model = "BRR"),
  G_oat = list(X = Z_oat %*% L_oat, model = "BRR")
)
if (ncol(incBlocks) &gt; 0) {
  ETA &lt;- append(ETA, list(block = list(X = incBlocks, model = "BRR")), after = 1)
}
if (fit_mix_term) {
  ETA$G_mix &lt;- list(X = Z_mix %*% L_mix, model = "BRR")
}
```

`Y &lt;- as.matrix(grainWgt[, c("peaYield","oatYield")])` at `:293-294` — trait 1 = pea, trait 2 = oat.

### Outputs

**`output/BGLR_variance_components.csv`** — built at `:411-443`. It is a `bind_rows` of two differently shaped tibbles, so the union of columns with `NA` padding. Observed header:

```
seed,term,var_Pr,var_As,cov_PrAs,cor_PrAs,component,var_pea,var_oat,cov_pea_oat,cor_pea_oat
```

- genetic rows (`component = "genetic"`): one per seed × genetic term, from `covariance_components()`; `var_pea`/`var_oat`/`cov_pea_oat`/`cor_pea_oat` are `NA`.
- residual rows (`term = "residual"`, `component = "residual"`): one per seed, built at `:421-432` from `fit$resCov$R`; `var_Pr`…`cor_PrAs` are `NA`.
- 12 data rows currently (4 seeds × 2 genetic terms + 4 residual).

**`output/BGLR_oat_effects_all_seeds.csv`** — `:461`, `:486`. Columns `seed, oatAcc, PrEff, AsEff, GMA`. 442 rows per seed × 4 seeds = 1768. Built by `species_effects("G_oat", L_oat, "oatAcc")` (`:452-459`), which wraps `accession_effects()` and renames `accession` → `oatAcc`. **Posterior means only — one row per accession per seed, no SD, no draws.**

**`output/BGLR_pea_effects_all_seeds.csv`** — `:462`, `:487`. Columns `seed, peaAcc, PrEff, AsEff, GMA`. 423 rows per seed.

**`output/BGLR_oat_rank_stability.csv`** / **`BGLR_pea_rank_stability.csv`** — `:464-465`, `:488-489`, built by `rank_summary()` (`dge_ige_functions.R:504-519`). Columns:

```
oatAcc,mean_rank,sd_rank,mean_GMA,sd_GMA,mean_Pr,mean_As
```

The `sd_*` columns are **between-chain** SDs over 4 seeds — not posterior SDs. This is the closest thing to a per-accession uncertainty that currently exists, and it is not one.

**`.rds`** — `:387-390`: `fits[[1]]` (seed 12567 only) → `output/BGLR_multitrait_pea_oat_yield_Gpea_Goat_Gmix.rds` (87 KB; `X`, `x2`, `post_beta*` are stripped by BGLR before return).

**`.dat`** — written by BGLR itself from `saveAt`, not by this script: `BGLR_seed_&lt;s&gt;_Omega_2.dat`, `_Omega_3.dat`, `_Omega_4.dat`, `_R.dat` for each of the 4 seeds. Each has 2000 lines (= `nIter/thin`), 3 whitespace-separated numbers per line (`vech` of a 2×2). `Omega_2/3/4` = the `block`, `G_pea`, `G_oat` terms (index `j` in ETA; `trial` is FIXED so has no Omega). **These include burn-in** (see §C). No `mu.dat` because `intercept = FALSE`.

**Figures** — `:528-531`: `BGLR_oat_Pr_vs_As.png`, `BGLR_pea_Pr_vs_As.png` (8×6 in, 150 dpi), faceted by seed, built by `pr_as_plot()` (`:495-520`). Also `print()`ed, which is what produces the stray `Rplots.pdf` in the repo root.

Console-only (not written): design diagnostics `:210-244`, GRM diagnostics `:280-287`, top-20 rank tables `:467-470`, between-chain Spearman matrices `:473-484`.

### Does anything record which trials entered the fit?

**No.** `:197` `print(table(grainWgt$studyName))` and the `:190-195` message go to stdout only. Nothing in any written artefact records the trial set, the whitelist, `study_years`, the seeds, or the MCMC settings. Downstream consumers re-derive the trial set independently and inconsistently — see `validation_functions.R:121-122`, which reads `B4I_intercrop_pheno.rds` with **no** `apply_trial_qc()` and **no** whitelist, then reports `n_trials = dplyr::n_distinct(pheno$studyName)` (`validation_functions.R:170`). That number does not describe the fit.

### `fit_producer_associate()` — `:324-502`

Signature (`:324-329`):

The `BGLR::Multitrait()` call (`:435-443`):

```r
  set.seed(seed)
  save_prefix &lt;- saveAt %||% file.path(tempdir(), "dge_ige_")
  fit &lt;- BGLR::Multitrait(
    y = Y, ETA = ETA, intercept = FALSE,
```

ETA (`:394-402`), identical in structure and order to the production script:

| term | model | X |
|---|---|---|
| `trial` | `"FIXED"` | `dummy_matrix(dat$trialF)` |
| `block` | `"BRR"` | `incBlocks` (appended `after = 1`, only if `ncol &gt; 0`) |
| `G_pea` | `"BRR"` | `Z_pea %*% L_pea` |
| `G_oat` | `"BRR"` | `Z_oat %*% L_oat` |
| `G_mix` | `"BRR"` | exact `Z_mix %*% L_mix` (`:419`) or `kron_basis(...)` (`:426`) |

`saveAt` is **always** passed (`:436`, defaulting to `tempdir()`). `saveEffects` is set on **exactly one term, and only the mix term** (`:428-431`):

```r

        ETA$G_mix$saveEffects &lt;- TRUE
        ETA$G_mix$storageMode &lt;- storage_mode
      }
```

Guards at `:331-346`: `save_effects` requires `fit_mix_term &amp;&amp; !is.na(kron_rank)` and an explicit non-NULL `saveAt`.

Returns a 12-element list (`:453-501`): `fit`, `L_oat`, `L_pea`, `L_mix`, `effects` (list of `oat`/`pea` tibbles from `accession_effects()`), `varcomp`, `resid_cov`, `accessions` (the sorted name vectors), `interaction`, `kron` (with `effects_file = paste0(save_prefix, "ETA_G_mix_beta.bin")` at `:493`), `mcmc = list(nIter, burnIn, thin)` (`:499`), `n_plots`, `n_blocks_fitted`.

The `mcmc` element exists precisely so burn-in can be stripped from the streamed draws — comment at `:497-498`: *"BGLR writes every thinned iteration, including burn-in. See read_beta_draws()."*

Constants at `:190-196`: `DGE_IGE_TRAITS &lt;- c("peaYield","oatYield")` and `DGE_IGE_ROLES`.

### `covariance_components()` — `:105-115`

```r
covariance_components &lt;- function(fit, term, trait_names, roles) {
  Omega &lt;- fit$ETA[[term]]$Cov$Omega
  dimnames(Omega) &lt;- list(trait_names, trait_names)
  tibble::tibble(
    term        = term,
    var_Pr      = Omega[roles[["Pr"]], roles[["Pr"]]],
    var_As      = Omega[roles[["As"]], roles[["As"]]],
    cov_PrAs    = Omega[roles[["Pr"]], roles[["As"]]],
    cor_PrAs    = cov_PrAs / sqrt(var_Pr * var_As)
  )
}
```

Posterior **mean** of Omega only (BGLR replaces `Cov$Omega` with `post_Omega` on return). `Cov$SD.Omega` is also present in the fit object but unused here.

### `accession_effects()` — `:117-128`

```r
accession_effects &lt;- function(fit, term, L, trait_names, roles) {
  g &lt;- L %*% fit$ETA[[term]]$beta
  dimnames(g) &lt;- list(rownames(L), trait_names)

  tibble::as_tibble(g, rownames = "accession") |&gt;
    dplyr::transmute(
      accession,
      PrEff = .data[[roles[["Pr"]]]],
      AsEff = .data[[roles[["As"]]]],
      GMA   = PrEff + AsEff   # GMA = Pr + As, docs/B4I_Proposal_Models.docx
    )
}
```

**This is the single point where per-accession uncertainty is destroyed.** `fit$ETA[[term]]$beta` is the posterior mean matrix (`p × 2`); `L %*% beta` is a point estimate. The per-draw analogue is exactly `L %*% draws[s, , ]` → `442 × 2` per draw, so this function generalises to draws with no change in algebra.

Note `fit$ETA[[term]]$SD.beta` *is* available (BGLR computes it, see §C) but is **not** a usable route to per-accession PEV: it is the elementwise SD of `beta` on the L basis, and `Var(L b) = L Var(b) L'` needs the full `p × p` posterior covariance of `b`, which BGLR does not accumulate. Streaming draws is the only correct route.

### `grm_factor()` — `:62-72`

```r
grm_factor &lt;- function(G, tol = 1e-8) {
  e &lt;- eigen(G, symmetric = TRUE)
  keep &lt;- e$values &gt; tol * max(e$values)
  ...
  L &lt;- sweep(e$vectors[, keep, drop = FALSE], 2, sqrt(e$values[keep]), "*")
  rownames(L) &lt;- rownames(G)
  L
}
```

I verified the actual ranks by re-running the pipeline's own GRM path read-only: **`ncol(L_oat) = 442` (442 accessions, full rank), `ncol(L_pea) = 423`** (423 accessions, full rank). So `p` for the `G_oat` BRR is 442 and for `G_pea` is 423 — no dimensions are dropped, and the coefficient vector length equals the accession count. (`collapse_grm` reduces 508→466 oat and 435→434 pea lines before subsetting to the phenotyped set.)

### `collapse_grm()` — `:28-51`

Reads `output/{oat,pea}_analysis_names.csv`, requires columns `germplasmName` and `analysis_name`, builds an averaging matrix `A` and returns `t(A) %*% G %*% A`. Returns `G` untouched if the file is missing or lacks the columns.

### `incidence()` — `:75-79`

```r
incidence &lt;- function(x, levels) {
  Z &lt;- stats::model.matrix(~ 0 + factor(x, levels = levels))
  colnames(Z) &lt;- levels
  Z
}
```

Used at `BGLR_multi_trait_model.R:328-330` and `dge_ige_functions.R:387-388`. Forces factor levels to GRM row order, which is what makes `L %*% beta` row-aligned with `rownames(L)`. `dummy_matrix()` (`:88-97`) is the single-level-tolerant variant used for `trialF`/`blockNumberF` inside `fit_producer_associate()`; the production script uses raw `model.matrix(~ 0 + ...)` instead (`:301`, `:320`).

## C) BGLR capability — definitive

Installed at `/Users/jj332/Library/R/arm64/4.6/library/BGLR`, **version 1.1.4**. No `R/*.R` sources (lazy-load DB only), so I inspected the parsed function bodies.

### Yes — `Multitrait()` supports `saveEffects = TRUE` inside an ETA term

Setup, in `BGLR:::setLT.BRR_mt` (the handler dispatched at `Multitrait` deparsed line 78):

```r
    if (is.null(LT$saveEffects)) {
        LT$saveEffects &lt;- FALSE
    }
    if (LT$saveEffects) {
        if (is.null(LT$storageMode)) {
            LT$storageMode &lt;- "double"
        }
        if (!LT$storageMode %in% c("single", "double")) {
            stop("storageMode of LP ", j, " can either be 'single' or 'double' (default)")
        }
        fname &lt;- paste(saveAt, LT$Name, "_beta.bin", sep = "")
        LT$fileEffects &lt;- file(fname, open = "wb")
        writeBin(object = c(nRow, traits, LT$p), con = LT$fileEffects,
            size = ifelse(LT$storageMode == "single", 4, 8))
    }
```

The write, in the `Multitrait` MCMC loop (deparsed lines 332-347):

```r
            for (j in 1:nLT) {
                if (ETA[[j]]$model %in% c("SpikeSlab", "BRR", "FIXED")) {
                  if (ETA[[j]]$saveEffects) {
                    writeBin(object = as.vector(ETA[[j]]$beta),
                      con = ETA[[j]]$fileEffects, size = ifelse(ETA[[j]]$storageMode ==
                        "single", 4, 8))
```

Supported models: **`SpikeSlab`, `BRR`, `FIXED`**. **`RKHS` is not** — another reason the project's BRR-on-`Z L` parameterisation matters. `setLT.FIXED_mt` has the identical `saveEffects` block (with a different header when `common = FALSE`).

`ETA[[j]]$Name` is `paste("ETA_", names(ETA)[j], sep = "")` (deparsed line 69), so term `G_oat` → `ETA_G_oat`.

### Filename pattern and binary format

**`paste0(saveAt, "ETA_", &lt;term name&gt;, "_beta.bin")`** — e.g. with the production `saveAt`, `output/BGLR_seed_12567_ETA_G_oat_beta.bin`.

Layout: a 3-number header `(nRow, traits, p)` then, for each saved iteration, `as.vector(beta)` where `beta` is `p × traits` — column-major, i.e. all `p` coefficients for trait 1, then all `p` for trait 2. Element size 8 bytes (`double`) or 4 bytes (`single`). No compression unless you additionally set `ETA[[j]]$compressEffects &lt;- TRUE`, in which case `BGLR:::compressFile` writes `&lt;name&gt;.bin.gz` and **unlinks the original** at the end of the run (deparsed lines 498-510) — that would break the repo's `read_beta_draws()`, which looks for the uncompressed name and asserts on `file.size()`. Leave it off.

### Reader: `BGLR::readBinMatMultitrait()`

Exported (`NAMESPACE:25`). Full body:

```r
function (filename, storageMode = "double")
{
    if (!storageMode %in% c("single", "double")) {
        stop("storageMode can either be 'single' or 'double' (default)")
    }
    fileIn &lt;- gzfile(filename, open = "rb")
    nRow &lt;- readBin(fileIn, n = 1, what = numeric(), size = ifelse(storageMode == "single", 4, 8))
    traits &lt;- readBin(fileIn, n = 1, what = numeric(), size = ifelse(storageMode == "single", 4, 8))
    p &lt;- readBin(fileIn, n = 1, what = numeric(), size = ifelse(storageMode == "single", 4, 8))
    Beta &lt;- array(data = NA, dim = c(nRow, p, traits))
    for (j in 1:nRow) {
        for (k in 1:traits) {
            Beta[j, , k] &lt;- readBin(fileIn, n = p, what = numeric(),
                size = ifelse(storageMode == "single", 4, 8))
        }
    }
    close(fileIn)
    return(Beta)
}
```

**Returns a 3-d array `[nRow, p, traits]` — draws × effects × traits**, exactly the shape you want. `gzfile` reads plain files transparently, so it works compressed or not. Two traps, both already documented in the repo:

1. The array is pre-allocated `NA` from the header, and `readBin` short-reads silently, so a **truncated file returns NAs with no error**.
2. `nRow` comes from the header, which is `nIter/thin` — see next point.

(`BGLR::readBinMat()` is the single-trait reader: header `(n, p)`, returns an `n × p` matrix, `byrow = TRUE` default. Not applicable to `Multitrait`.)

### `thin` is honoured; `burnIn` is NOT

- Header: `nRow &lt;- nIter/thin` (deparsed line 61), with `if (nRow &lt; 1) stop("Check nIter, thin\n")`.
- The `saveEffects` write is gated **only** by `if (iter %% thin == 0)` (deparsed line 291, enclosing the block at 332-347).
- The posterior-mean accumulation is gated separately by `if ((iter &gt; burnIn) &amp; (iter %% thin == 0))` (deparsed line 349).

So the file contains **every thinned iteration including burn-in**: `nIter %/% thin` rows, of which the first `burnIn %/% thin` must be discarded by the caller. With `nIter = 20000, burnIn = 3000, thin = 10`: **2000 rows written, drop the first 300, keep 1700.**

The `.dat` files behave identically (same `iter %% thin == 0` gate, deparsed lines 291-298) — which is confirmed by `wc -l output/BGLR_seed_12567_Omega_4.dat` = **2000**, not 1700. Anything summarising those `.dat` files must drop 300 rows too.

### What `Multitrait` saves by default

With `saveAt` set and no `saveEffects` anywhere — exactly what is in `output/` now:

- **`&lt;saveAt&gt;Omega_&lt;j&gt;.dat`** — from `BGLR:::setCov.UN`: `Cov$fName_Omega &lt;- paste(saveAt, "Omega_", j, ".dat", sep = "")`. One per BRR/RKHS/SpikeSlab term with `Cov$type %in% c("UN","DIAG")`, written as `vech(Omega)` (3 numbers for 2 traits). `j` is the ETA index: `Omega_2` = `block`, `Omega_3` = `G_pea`, `Omega_4` = `G_oat`. No `Omega_1` because `trial` is `FIXED`.
- **`&lt;saveAt&gt;R.dat`** — `vech(resCov$R)`, 3 numbers per row.
- **`&lt;saveAt&gt;mu.dat`** — only `if (intercept)`; absent here because `intercept = FALSE` (deparsed lines 48-54).
- (`W.dat`/`PSI.dat` only for `REC`/`FA` covariance types — not used.)

All files are closed and the handles nulled before the fit object is returned (deparsed lines 480-521). The returned object keeps `beta` (= `post_beta`), `SD.beta` (= `sqrt(post_beta2 - post_beta^2)`), `u = X %*% post_beta`, `Cov$Omega`, `Cov$SD.Omega`, `resCov$R`, `resCov$SD.R`, `ETAHat`, `SD.ETAHat`, `fit` (DIC); it **drops** `X`, `x2`, `post_beta`, `post_beta2` (deparsed lines 418-462).

### On-disk size implication

Bytes = `3 * size + (nIter %/% thin) * p * traits * size`.

At the production settings (`nIter = 20000`, `thin = 10` → 2000 rows; `traits = 2`):

| term | `p` | `double` (8 B) | `single` (4 B) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `G_oat` | 442 | 14,144,024 B ≈ **13.5 MiB** | ≈ **6.7 MiB** |
| `G_pea` | 423 | 13,536,024 B ≈ **12.9 MiB** | ≈ **6.5 MiB** |
| both | — | ≈ **26.4 MiB / seed** | ≈ **13.2 MiB / seed** |
| both × 4 seeds | — | ≈ **106 MiB** | ≈ **53 MiB** |

(`block` would add `2000 * ncol(incBlocks) * 2 * 8`; `trial` would add `2000 * n_trials * 2 * 8` ≈ 0.2 MiB — both negligible, but neither is needed.)

Two notes for planning:

- **The file is 15% larger than the useful content** (2000 rows stored, 1700 kept) — unavoidable, BGLR sizes the file from `nIter/thin`.
- Reading is pure R double loops: `nRow * traits` = 4000 `readBin` calls per file, each of `p` doubles. Cheap. The returned array is `1700 × 442 × 2` doubles ≈ 12 MiB in memory after burn-in removal, and per-accession summaries (`L %*% draws[s,,]` per draw) can be streamed draw-by-draw if you prefer not to hold `L %*% draws` for all draws (`442 × 2 × 1700` ≈ 12 MiB, also fine to hold).

## D) Existing code that reads BGLR draws from disk

**`read_beta_draws()` — `/Users/jj332/Documents/GitRepo/B4I_Fall2026/code/interaction_decomp.R:259-304`. This is the reusable helper.** Signature:

```r
read_beta_draws &lt;- function(prefix, term = "ETA_G_mix", nIter, burnIn, thin,
                            p, traits, storage_mode = "double")
```

It does all four things you need: builds `paste0(prefix, term, "_beta.bin")`, asserts `file.size(f) == 3*size + (nIter %/% thin)*p*traits*size` (because a truncated file reads back as NA), calls `BGLR::readBinMatMultitrait(f, storageMode = storage_mode)`, checks `anyNA`, then drops the first `burnIn %/% thin` rows. Returns `[n_kept, p, traits]`.

**It is directly reusable for the producer/associate terms** — only `term` changes, to `"ETA_G_oat"` / `"ETA_G_pea"`, with `p = ncol(L_oat)` / `ncol(L_pea)` and `traits = 2L`. The function has no mix-term-specific logic; `term` is already a parameter with a mix-term default. Its doc comment at `:246-257` is the authoritative in-repo statement of the burn-in trap and matches the source I read above exactly.

Everything else in `interaction_decomp.R` is bilinear/Kronecker-specific and **not** reusable for per-accession PEV: `beta_from_vector()` (`:236`), `decompose_draws()` (`:355`), `align_to_reference()` (`:307`), `decompose_bilinear()` (`:209`), `.whiten()` (`:194`), `double_centre()` (`:60`), `effective_rank()` (`:73`), `component_summary()` (`:446`).

Callers of `read_beta_draws()`, i.e. the existing streaming patterns to copy:

- **`code/sim_decomp_run.R:224-237`** — `fit_dge_ige(..., save_effects = TRUE, saveAt = prefix, storage_mode = "single")` then `read_beta_draws(prefix, nIter = n_iter, burnIn = burn_in, thin = thin, p = ka*kb, traits = 2L, storage_mode = "single")`. Cleanup at `:309`: `if (!keep_draws) unlink(paste0(prefix, "ETA_G_mix_beta.bin"))`. Second call at `:336-339`.
- **`tests/test_fits.R:283-342`** — the test that proves the whole chain. `:321-324` asserts `dim(draws)[1] == nIter %/% thin - burnIn %/% thin`; `:326-328` asserts `colMeans(draws[,,k]) == fit beta` to 1e-10 — **this is the exact assertion you want for the per-accession version**, since BGLR's returned `beta` is the running mean over precisely the post-burn-in thinned draws, so `colMeans(draws) == beta` can only hold if burn-in offset, byte order, trait-major layout and storage mode are all correct. `:331-333` is the negative control (keeping burn-in must disagree).
- **`code/sim_fit.R:126-150`** — `fit_dge_ige()` passes `save_effects`/`saveAt`/`storage_mode` straight through to `fit_producer_associate()`.
- **`code/scinet/sim_decomp_array.sbatch:38`** — comment on why `saveAt` must be a per-task prefix, not a bare directory.

`code/evaluation.R:98-104` registers `read_beta_draws` in the `sim_decomp` evaluation group, so adding a per-accession reader there would want a matching registration.

`saveAt` elsewhere, for completeness: `code/cross_validate_combinations.R:184` and `code/pilot/pilot_dge_ige.R:180` both pass a `saveAt` prefix but no `saveEffects`, so they only produce `.dat` files. Nothing in the repo reads any `.dat` file, and there is no `read_omega` helper — the `Omega_*.dat` / `R.dat` files in `output/` are currently write-only byproducts.

### Two things worth flagging for your two changes

1. **The global reliability you want to replace lives at `/Users/jj332/Documents/GitRepo/B4I_Fall2026/code/validation_functions.R:152-171`**, inside `validation_inputs()`. It derives reliability from the variance-component ratio rather than from draws:

```r
    rel &lt;- function(v_blup, sigma2) min(max(v_blup / sigma2, 1e-6), 0.999)
    ...
      rel_Pr       = rel(stats::var(acc$Pr), sigma2_Pr),
      rel_As       = rel(stats::var(acc$As), sigma2_As),
      PEV_As       = sigma2_As * (1 - rel(stats::var(acc$As), sigma2_As)),
```

`var(acc$Pr)` is the variance of the chain-averaged BLUPs **across accessions**, and `sigma2_*` is the across-seed mean of `var_Pr`/`var_As` from `BGLR_variance_components.csv`. One scalar per species per role, clamped. Consumers: `code/validate_power.R:132` (`rnorm(nrow(a), 0, sqrt(inp$PEV_As))` — a single PEV for every accession), `validation_functions.R:339` (`sigma2_within = inp$PEV_As + var_blup_within`), `:639`, `code/validate_pool_selection.R:64,89,110-111`, `code/validation_report.R:77-81`. `code/validate_crossval.R:224` passes `PEV_As = 0` deliberately. A per-accession PEV column would need to flow into `validate_power.R:132` as a vector rather than a scalar.

2. **Removing the whitelist changes the fitted data, and nothing downstream will notice.** `validation_inputs()` already reads the raw phenotype table with neither QC nor whitelist, so its `n_trials`, `n_plots` and partner counts are computed over a different plot set than the fit's. If the whitelist goes away, the mismatch shrinks from 4 trials to 1 (`B4I_2025_AL`, dropped by QC) but does not vanish. Writing the realised trial set from the fit — there is currently no such artefact — would be the thing that makes the change verifiable.</result>

### How it finds the previous vintage — L129-132

```r
previous &lt;- list.dirs(out_root, recursive = FALSE) |&gt;
  setdiff(out_dir) |&gt;
  (\(d) d[file.exists(file.path(d, "pools.csv"))])() |&gt;
  sort()
```

then `prev_dir &lt;- utils::tail(previous, 1)` (L135). Lexicographic sort of full paths; any directory with a `pools.csv` qualifies, so the hand-made `output/validation/2026-10-02_1` is a legitimate candidate (and sorts *after* `2026-10-02`). On-disk vintages: `2026-09-24, 2026-09-27, 2026-10-02, 2026-10-02_1, 2026-10-03`.

### Rule-version guard — L157-178

Reads `pool_index` out of the previous `pool_summary.csv` and compares to `POOL_INDEX_VERSION` (`"single-v1"`, `validation_functions.R:221`). On mismatch it prints that the churn measures the rule change and that *"The 70% retention guidance does not apply to it."* Otherwise it prints the 70% guidance. Purely advisory — no failure either way.

### Figure — L187-219

`plot_data` left-joins `pools` onto `inp$accessions` per species, `replace_na(pool, "not selected")`; `Pr` vs `As` scatter, faceted by species, manual colour/alpha/size scales keyed on the three pool levels. ### What `design_check.txt` contains

Verified against `output/validation/2026-10-02/design_check.txt`. In order:

2. `plots per location` — `chk$plots_per_location` as a data frame
3. `cells per location (all four should be equal)` — `chk$cells_per_location` pivoted wide on `pea_pool`, columns prefixed `pea `
4. `plots per accession` — median and range of `chk$oat_reps$plots` and `chk$pea_reps$plots`

7. `replicated combinations (for plot error and SCA)` — `chk$anchor_plots`, `nrow(chk$replicated_combinations)`, and the sorted unique replication counts
8. `VERDICT` — six `[ok  ]` / `[FAIL]` lines, then one of `design passes every check` / `DESIGN HAS A PROBLEM -- see the FAIL lines above`

### Exactly which assertions are checked — L128-146

```r
  ok &lt;- TRUE
  say &lt;- function(pass, msg) {
    cat("  [", if (pass) "ok  " else "FAIL", "] ", msg, "\n", sep = "")
    pass
  }
  ok &lt;- say(dplyr::n_distinct(chk$plots_per_location$plots) == 1,
            "every location has the same number of plots") &amp;&amp; ok
  ok &lt;- say(dplyr::n_distinct(chk$cells_per_location$plots) == 1,
            "all four cells equally represented at every location") &amp;&amp; ok
  ok &lt;- say(chk$max_oat_imbalance == 0,
            "every oat meets both pea pools equally often") &amp;&amp; ok
  ok &lt;- say(chk$max_pea_imbalance == 0,
            "every pea meets both oat pools equally often") &amp;&amp; ok
  ok &lt;- say(chk$anchor_plots == 2 * 4 * n_anchor_per_cell * n_locations,
            "anchors duplicated within every location") &amp;&amp; ok
  ok &lt;- say(nrow(chk$replicated_combinations) &gt; 0,
            "some combinations are replicated, so plot error is estimable") &amp;&amp; ok
```

**What makes it fail vs just report — important:**

- **Hard failure (non-zero exit)**: only the missing-`pools.csv` `stop()` at L62, and any `stop()` raised *inside* `make_validation_design()` — `validation_functions.R:407-414` (`cell_size &lt; 4L`; `n_anchor &gt; cell_size`) and L439-443 (a pool too small to supply anchors and still rotate).
- **Report only**: *all six* `VERDICT` assertions. `ok` is accumulated and printed but **never used to set an exit status** — no `stopifnot`, no `quit(status=)`. So a design that fails partner balance writes `field_book.csv` anyway, exits 0, and `validate_refresh.R` records the step as `ok`. The only trace is the `FAIL` line inside `design_check.txt` and, because `run_step()` only tails 6 lines of the log (`validate_refresh.R:169`), the verdict line is roughly at the edge of what gets echoed.
- Note `n_distinct(chk$cells_per_location$plots) == 1` compares across *all* location×cell rows globally, not within location.
- The anchor-count expectation `2 * 4 * n_anchor_per_cell * n_locations` hard-codes the 2×2 factorial (the `4`) and the within-location duplication (the `2`).

### Figure — L156-181

Pivots `chk$oat_partner_balance` and `chk$pea_partner_balance` long over the literal column names `` `As+` `` / `` `As-` `` (so the pool labels are hard-coded in three places: `validation_functions.R:446-447`, `:507`, and here at L158/163), then a dodged bar chart of plots-per-partner-pool, faceted by species.

### Key → source file → table

| key | reads | builder | output |
|---|---|---|---|
| `vintage` | `vintage.csv` + `output/trial_qc.csv` | `block_vintage()`, L73-94 | an italic *"Numbers below are from vintage **\&lt;x\&gt;**"* line, then a 6-column table `species / trials / plots / accessions / eligible / reliability of As` from `vint$species, n_trials, n_plots, n_accessions, n_eligible, reliability_As`; then, if `qc` is present, a `Trials kept by the QC screen (n): ...` line from `qc$studyName[qc$keep]` and a `Dropped (n): ...` line |
| `pools` | `pool_summary.csv` | `block_pools()`, L99-110 | 8-column table `species / n per pool / candidates / θ / ΔAs (g/m²) / ΔPr / mean Pr, As+ / mean Pr, As−` from `psum$species, n_per_pool, n_candidates, theta, dAs, dPr, mean_Pr_plus, mean_Pr_minus`; ΔAs is bolded; trailer `*Selection rule: \`&lt;pool_index&gt;\`.*` (or `unstamped`) |
| `lambda` | `crossval_summary.csv` | `block_lambda()`, L112-121 | filters `set == "informative folds only"`, falls back to all rows if empty; 5-column table `λ / across-fold spread (interaction_frac) / folds / accession-level r` from `lambda_mean, interaction_frac, n_folds, r_accession_mean` |
| `power` | `power_grid.csv` | `block_power()`, L123-167 | see below |

`fmt &lt;- function(x, k = 2) formatC(x, format = "f", digits = k)` (L67) is the only number formatter; every block builds markdown by `sprintf()` over vectorised columns, so one row per input row.

### `block_power()` in detail — the most logic of the four

```r
  n_pool  &lt;- validation_setting("n_per_pool")
  n_loc   &lt;- validation_setting("n_locations")
  g &lt;- grid |&gt;
    dplyr::filter(n == unname(n_pool[species])) |&gt;
    dplyr::arrange(species, P)
  if (nrow(g) == 0) { message("  power grid has no rows at the configured pool sizes -- skipped"); return(NULL) }
```

It filters to the *configured* pool size per species (oat 20, pea 30) — the comment at L125-128 records that getting this wrong "quietly reported pea at oat's pool size." Then a staleness detector:

```r
  per_sp &lt;- g |&gt;
    dplyr::group_by(species) |&gt;
    dplyr::summarise(nl = dplyr::n_distinct(lambda),
                     ni = dplyr::n_distinct(interaction_frac), .groups = "drop")
  swept &lt;- any(per_sp$nl &gt; 1) || any(per_sp$ni &gt; 1) ||
           ("sided" %in% names(g) &amp;&amp; dplyr::n_distinct(g$sided) &gt; 1)
```

If `swept`, it returns a four-line blockquote telling the reader the grid predates the measured λ, instead of a table. Otherwise:

```r
  c("One-sided α = 0.05, at the configured pool size, with λ and the",
    "pool × location interaction as measured (see §6).",
    "",
    "| | plots/loc | n per pool | total plots | λ | interaction | power |",
    "|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|",
    sprintf("| **%s** | %d | %d | %d | %s | %s | %s |",
            g$species, as.integer(g$P / n_loc), g$n, g$P,
            fmt(g$lambda), fmt(g$interaction_frac), fmt(g$power)))
```

`plots/loc` is *derived* as `P / n_loc`; `α = 0.05` and "One-sided" are hard-coded strings, not read from the grid's `sided` column.

### The splice mechanism — L176-205

```r
doc &lt;- readLines(doc_file, warn = FALSE)

splice &lt;- function(doc, key, body) {
  b &lt;- sprintf("&lt;!-- BEGIN GENERATED: %s --&gt;", key)
  e &lt;- sprintf("&lt;!-- END GENERATED: %s --&gt;", key)
  i &lt;- which(doc == b); j &lt;- which(doc == e)
  if (length(i) != 1 || length(j) != 1 || j &lt;= i) {
    message("  no markers for '", key, "' in ", basename(doc_file), " -- skipped")
    return(list(doc = doc, changed = FALSE))
  }
  old &lt;- doc[(i + 1):(j - 1)]
  changed &lt;- !identical(trimws(old), trimws(body))
  list(doc = c(doc[seq_len(i)], body, doc[j:length(doc)]), changed = changed)
}

changed_any &lt;- FALSE
for (k in names(blocks)) {
  if (is.null(blocks[[k]])) {
    message("  no data for '", k, "' in this vintage -- skipped")
    next
  }
  r &lt;- splice(doc, k, blocks[[k]])
  doc &lt;- r$doc
  if (r$changed) { changed_any &lt;- TRUE; message("  '", k, "' block: numbers differ from the document") }
  else message("  '", k, "' block: unchanged")
}
```

Mechanics that matter if you add a key:

- Match is **exact whole-line equality** (`doc == b`). No leading whitespace, no trailing spaces, marker must be alone on its line.
- Requires **exactly one** BEGIN and one END with `j &gt; i`; anything else is skipped with a message and `changed = FALSE` (so a typo'd marker reports "up to date", not an error).
- `doc` is reassigned each iteration and indices are recomputed from the updated `doc`, so earlier splices shifting line numbers is handled correctly.
- A `NULL` block is skipped and does **not** set `changed_any` — a missing vintage CSV makes `--check` report clean.
- Comparison is `identical(trimws(old), trimws(body))` — per-line whitespace trimmed, but line count and content must match exactly.
- The content between markers is fully replaced; nothing outside is touched. Final write is `writeLines(doc, doc_file)` (L214) — whole-file rewrite.

### How `--check` works — L207-212

```r
if (check_only) {
  message(if (changed_any)
            "\nDRIFT: VALIDATION_DESIGN.md does not match this vintage. Re-run without --check."
          else "\nVALIDATION_DESIGN.md is up to date with this vintage.")
  quit(save = "no", status = if (changed_any) 1L else 0L)
}
```

Exit 1 on drift, 0 otherwise. The in-memory `doc` is discarded. This is the only exit-status signal in the whole reporting layer.

### To add a new table (e.g. a power table keyed on a new analysis model)

Four edits, all small:

1. **Read the file** — add a `read_if("&lt;new&gt;.csv")` line next to L59-62.
2. **Write the builder** — a `block_&lt;key&gt;()` function alongside L73-167, returning a `character` vector of markdown lines or `NULL` when the data is absent. By convention it does its own staleness/consistency checks and returns an explanatory blockquote rather than a wrong table (see `block_power()`'s `swept` path).
3. **Register the key** — add `&lt;key&gt; = block_&lt;key&gt;()` to the `blocks` list at L169-170. The list's name order is the splice order.
4. **Add the markers** — insert `&lt;!-- BEGIN GENERATED: &lt;key&gt; --&gt;` / `&lt;!-- END GENERATED: &lt;key&gt; --&gt;` as standalone lines in `VALIDATION_DESIGN.md` at the point the table should appear. Without them the key is skipped with a message, silently.

Nothing else is key-aware: no registry, no schema, no test. (`tests/` does not exercise `validation_report.R` at all — see F.) If the new table's producing script writes a new vintage CSV, also add it to `validate_refresh.R`'s `STEPS` so `--refresh-doc` runs after it, and update the header comment at L19-21 (which is already stale).

### Stage filtering — L81-97

```r
steps &lt;- STEPS
if (!is.null(only)) {
  if (!only %in% STAGE_ORDER) stop("--only must be one of: ", paste(STAGE_ORDER, collapse = ", "), call. = FALSE)
  steps &lt;- dplyr::filter(steps, stage == only)
}
if (!is.null(from)) {
  if (!from %in% STAGE_ORDER) stop("--from must be one of: ", paste(STAGE_ORDER, collapse = ", "), call. = FALSE)
  keep &lt;- STAGE_ORDER[match(from, STAGE_ORDER):length(STAGE_ORDER)]
  steps &lt;- dplyr::filter(steps, stage %in% keep)
}
if (nrow(steps) == 0) stop("nothing to run", call. = FALSE)
```

`--only` takes exactly one stage (not a list); if both flags are given they compose as an intersection. Execution order is the tribble's row order, which happens to agree with `STAGE_ORDER`.

### Pre-flight

- **Plan print** — L99-117: numbered listing with `T3` / `slow` markers; then a line about `sim_config.R` being untouched; then whether `VALIDATION_DESIGN.md` will be refreshed; then, if the `qc` stage is in the run, how to overrule it (`data/trial_qc_manual.csv` with `studyName,keep[,note]`, then `--from qc`).
- **`--dry-run`** — L119-125: prints each script with its `why`, then `quit(save = "no")`. Nothing runs, no log dir created.
- **Credentials** — L129-138: if `any(steps$needs_t3)`, `readRenviron(.Renviron)` then `stop()` unless both `T3_USERNAME` and `T3_PASSWORD` are non-empty. Checked *after* filtering, so `--from assemble` skips the check entirely.
- **`--refresh-doc` guard** — L142-146:
  ```r
  if (refresh_doc &amp;&amp; !any(steps$stage %in% c("validate"))) {
    message("--refresh-doc given, but no validate step is in this run; the ",
            "document would be rewritten from an older vintage. Refusing.")

  }
  ```
  A message and a silent downgrade, not an error.

### How it logs — L148-172

```r
log_dir &lt;- here::here("output", "refresh_logs")
dir.create(log_dir, showWarnings = FALSE, recursive = TRUE)
stamp &lt;- format(Sys.time(), "%Y%m%d_%H%M%S")

run_step &lt;- function(script) {
  path &lt;- here::here("code", script)
  if (!file.exists(path)) stop("missing script: ", path, call. = FALSE)
  log &lt;- file.path(log_dir, paste0(stamp, "_", tools::file_path_sans_ext(script), ".log"))
  ...
  status &lt;- system2("Rscript", shQuote(path), stdout = log, stderr = log)
  ...
}
```

One log per step at `output/refresh_logs/&lt;YYYYmmdd_HHMMSS&gt;_&lt;script&gt;.log` — the stamp is taken **once** for the whole run, so all logs from one invocation share a prefix. stdout and stderr both go to the same file (nothing is echoed live). Each step is a separate `Rscript` process, so no state leaks between steps.

### How it decides failure — L164-171

```r
  if (status != 0) {
    cat(paste0("  ", tail_n(25), collapse = "\n"), "\n")
    stop(script, " failed after ", round(mins, 1), " min (exit ", status,
         "). Full log: ", log, call. = FALSE)
  }
  cat(paste0("  ", tail_n(6), collapse = "\n"), "\n")
  cat(sprintf("  ok (%.1f min) -&gt; %s\n", mins, basename(log)))
  mins
```

Failure is **the child process's exit status only**. On non-zero: last 25 log lines echoed, then `stop()` — which, because the driver is `timings &lt;- purrr::map_dbl(steps$script, run_step)` (L174), aborts the whole run at the first failure; no later step executes and no summary is printed. On success: last 6 log lines plus a timing line. Consequence worth flagging for your redesign: `validate_design.R` exits 0 even when its VERDICT block reports `FAIL` (see B), so a balance failure is invisible to this driver.

### After the run — L176-215

- Timing summary table `tibble(step = steps$script, minutes = round(timings, 1))` (L178).
- Vintage listing + a pointer to `pool_diff.csv` and the 70%-retention warning, when ≥2 vintages exist (L184-195).
- `--refresh-doc` tail (L201-215): `system2("Rscript", shQuote(here::here("code", "validation_report.R")))` with **no flags and no `--vintage`** — so it always reports to stdout and writes the *latest* vintage on disk. Non-zero status produces a `warning()`, not a `stop()`.
- Closing "Read next:" pointer list (L217-221) naming `VALIDATION_DESIGN.md`, `pool_summary.csv`, `power_grid.csv`, `field_book.csv`.

## E) `tests/test_validation.R` (294 lines)

### The harness

**Not testthat.** `tests/helper.R` (68 lines) is the whole framework — four assertions plus two utilities:

| function | helper.R line | semantics |
|---|---|---|
| `check(cond, msg)` | 18-26 | `isTRUE(cond)` → `ok &lt;&lt;- ok + 1L`, else `fail &lt;&lt;- fail + 1L` and print `  FAIL: &lt;msg&gt;` |
| `check_near(actual, expected, tol = 1e-6, msg)` | 33-43 | `max(abs(actual - expected)) &lt;= tol`; prints the discrepancy on failure |
| `check_error(expr, msg)` | 50-53 | `tryCatch({force(expr); FALSE}, error = function(e) TRUE)` — the expression **must** raise |
| `load_code(...)` | 56-62 | `source(here::here("code", f))` with messages and stdout suppressed |
| `finish(label)` | 65-68 | prints `"&lt;label&gt;: N passed, M failed"`, `quit(status = 1)` if `fail &gt; 0` |

Module-level counters `ok &lt;- 0L; fail &lt;- 0L` (L14-15) with `&lt;&lt;-` assignment. No grouping construct at all — there is no `test_that` equivalent; "blocks" are comment banners and the file runs top-to-bottom in one flat scope.

`tests/run_all.R` (61 lines) globs `^test_.*[.]R$`, runs each in **its own `Rscript` process** (L31-46, deliberate — L3-6: "a suite whose outcome depends on file order is worse than no suite"), greps the output for `"passed,|FAIL:|SKIP:|Error"` (L41), times each file, and exits 1 if any child exited non-zero. `slow_files &lt;- c("test_fits.R")` (L21) is excluded unless `--all`.

`test_validation.R` preamble: `load_code("validation_functions.R")` (L17) — the *only* file it loads — and `set.seed(5)` (L19).

### The fixture: a hand-built fake `validation_inputs()` element

L23-39:

```r
make_inputs &lt;- function(n = 120, cor_pr_as = -0.4, pev = 50, sigma2_e = 500) {
  z1 &lt;- rnorm(n); z2 &lt;- rnorm(n)
  z2 &lt;- cor_pr_as * z1 + sqrt(1 - cor_pr_as^2) * residuals(lm(z2 ~ z1))
  list(
    species = "test",
    accessions = tibble::tibble(
      acc = sprintf("acc%03d", seq_len(n)),
      Pr = as.vector(scale(z1)) * 10,
      As = as.vector(scale(z2)) * 8,
      n_partners = sample(3:10, n, replace = TRUE),
      n_plots = sample(4:12, n, replace = TRUE),
      eligible = TRUE),
    PEV_As = pev, sigma2_e = sigma2_e, sigma2_Pr = 100,
    rel_As = 0.3, response = "partner yield", n_trials = 6L, n_plots = 2000L
  )
}
inp &lt;- make_inputs()
```

So: it fakes **one species element**, not the two-element list `validation_inputs()` returns. `Pr` and `As` are standardised then scaled (SD 10 and 8) with `cor_pr_as` imposed by Gram-Schmidt against `z1`. Eligibility is **pre-set to TRUE for everyone**; `min_partners`, `sigma2_As`, `rel_Pr` are absent — i.e. the fixture encodes exactly the contract `build_pools()` currently needs (`species`, `accessions{acc,Pr,As,n_partners,n_plots,eligible}`, `PEV_As`).

A second instance `make_inputs(130)` is built at L244 for the pea side of the design test.

### Every assertion block, in file order

**§1 `build_pools` — L41-87.** A `for (n in c(10, 20, 30))` loop (L49) with 7 `check()`s per iteration (**21 assertions**):
- L54 `n = %d gives n per pool`
- L57 `the pools are disjoint at n = %d`
- L63 `constraining never widens the producer gap at n = %d` — oracle: `abs(bp$dPr) &lt;= abs(.pools_at(cand_n, n, 0)$dPr) + 1e-8`
- L72 `the scan finds the global best |dPr| at n = %d` — **independent oracle**: a 1001-point sweep `purrr::map_dbl(seq(0, 10, length.out = 1001), \(th) .pools_at(cand_n, n, th)$dPr)` (L70-71), asserting no theta beats the chosen one
- L74 `the associate contrast is positive at n = %d`
- L75 `every member clears the Pr threshold at n = %d`
- L77 `both pools are above the population mean Pr at n = %d`

Then the **negative control** at L83-87: the naive `slice_max(Pr + As)` vs `slice_max(Pr - As)` rule at n=30 *must* overlap — "which is why it is not used".

**§1b the index is the Lagrangian — L89-122.** Builds `cand_m` (eligible, `Pr &gt;= median`), `theta_seq &lt;- seq(0, 5, by = 0.05)`, sweeps `.pools_at(cand_m, 20, th)`. Five assertions:
- L105 `selecting on As alone leaves the As+ pool short on Pr` (`dPr_by_theta[1] &lt; 0`)
- L107 `dPr rises with theta in trend` (`cor(theta_seq, dPr_by_theta) &gt; 0.8`)
- L111 `theta = 0 maximises the contrast: it is the unconstrained solution`
- L113 `and buying Pr balance costs contrast, monotonically in trend` (`cor &lt; -0.8`)
- L121 `top-n and bottom-n of one ordering are disjoint at every theta` (checked at *every* theta, not just the chosen one)

**§1c negative control on the retired two-index rule — L124-146.** Reimplements the old scoring (`two_index`, L132-140) and asserts `best_new &gt; best_old` at the same `|dPr| &lt;= 1.0` tolerance (L144). The comment is explicit about why: "Without this check a revert would pass the whole suite."

**§ pool-size / tolerance / guard — L148-173.** Five assertions:
- L152 `a larger pool gives a smaller contrast` (`d10 &gt; d30`)
- L158 `an unmeetable producer tolerance raises a warning that says so` — `grepl("Pr constraint could not be met", w)`, i.e. it pins the warning **text** at `validation_functions.R:311`
- L160 `and a tolerance it can meet raises none`
- L165 `check_error(build_pools(inp, 100, pr_quantile = 0.5), "a pool bigger than the candidate set is refused")`
- L169-173 `ineligible accessions are never selected` — sets `inp_partial$accessions$eligible &lt;- n_partners &gt;= 8`, builds at n=8, asserts `all(bp_e$pools$n_partners &gt;= 8)`

**§2 `pool_diff` — L175-191.** Four assertions: `entered` ×1 (L183), `left` ×1 (L184), `retained == nrow(old_p) - 1` (L185), and all-`switched pool` after flipping every label (L190).

**§3 `contrast_power` — L193-236.** Eleven assertions. The local helper is `pw &lt;- function(...) contrast_power(dAs = 15, sigma2_within = 100, n = 20, sigma2_e = 500, P = 400, ...)` (L201-202), but *every other call in the section is fully positional*: `contrast_power(15, 100, 20, 500, 400, ...)`.
- L204 power is a probability; L205 `SE &gt; SE_floor`; L206 `pct_SE_irreducible` in (0,100]
- L211 `se_of(300) &gt; se_of(500)` — more plots reduce the SE
- L212-213 `se_of(1e9)` within ±0.1% of `SE_floor` — "converges on the floor, not on zero"
- L216 more accessions lower the floor
- L221 one-sided beats two-sided
- L226 attenuation (`lambda = 0.6`) costs power
- L229 interaction (`interaction_frac = 0.5`) costs power
- L235 `check_near(p0$power, 0.05, tol = 1e-6, "a zero contrast gives power equal to alpha")`

**§4 `make_validation_design` / `check_validation_design` — L238-292.** Twelve assertions on a 5-location, 80-plot, 1-anchor, block-10, `seed = 1L` design:
- L251 every location gets exactly 80 plots
- L253 all four cells equally represented
- L255 `max_oat_imbalance == 0` — "the load-bearing property"
- L257 `max_pea_imbalance == 0`
- L259 `chk$anchor_plots == 2 * 4 * 1 * 5`
- L261 some combinations replicated
- L263, L265 both pools appear on each side
- L274 `each anchor combination appears at all five locations` — this is the regression test for the bug `tests/README.md:48-51` records
- L282 `anchor accessions appear in more plots than the rest, as documented`
- L287 `anchors are inside the plot budget` (`sum == 5 * 80`)
- L291 `check_error(make_validation_design(oat_pools, pea_pools, n_loc = 5L, p_loc = 8L), "too small a budget is refused")`

Closes with `finish("validation tests")` (L294). `tests/README.md:22` logs the whole file at ~2 s.

### Impact of your three proposed changes

**(1) `validation_inputs()` gains per-accession reliability columns; eligibility moves from partner count to a reliability threshold.**

`test_validation.R` never calls `validation_inputs()` (README L72-73: "Nothing reads `output/`"), so there is no direct break. The breakage is all through the fixture and the eligibility semantics:

- `make_inputs()` (L23-38) would need the new per-accession column. If `build_pools()` starts reading it, **every assertion in §1, §1b, §1c, §2 and §4 fails at once** (all of them route through `build_pools(inp, ...)`), because the fixture would not supply it. ~50 of the file's ~53 assertions.
- **L169-173 is the test that encodes the old rule** and is the one to rewrite: `inp_partial$accessions$eligible &lt;- n_partners &gt;= 8` then `all(bp_e$pools$n_partners &gt;= 8)`. If `eligible` stays a precomputed boolean column it passes vacuously but now asserts the wrong thing; if the threshold moves *inside* `build_pools()`, it breaks.
- `make_inputs(n_partners = sample(3:10, ...))` at L32 exists only to satisfy the partner filter and becomes vestigial.
- `.pools_at()`/`cand_n`/`cand_m` constructions at L62, L98-99 replicate `build_pools`'s candidate filter by hand (`dplyr::filter(inp$accessions, eligible, Pr &gt;= bp$pr_min)`). Any change to that filter must be mirrored in **three** places in this file or the oracles at L63 and L72 silently compare against the wrong candidate set.

Outside `tests/`, the same change hits: `validate_pool_selection.R:97` (`stopifnot(... all(bp$pools$n_partners &gt;= min_partners))`), `validate_pool_selection.R:78` (the "eligible accessions (&gt;= N partners)" console line), `validation_functions.R:319-320` (`transmute(... n_partners, n_plots)` fixes `pools.csv`'s columns), `validation_functions.R:638` (`n_eligible` in `vintage.csv`), `validation_report.R:78-81` (the `eligible` column in the generated `vintage` table), `validate_crossval.R:207-226` (its own `fake_inp` — see below), and `validate_pool_selection.R:211-213` (the figure subtitle that states the rule to the reader).

**(2) `contrast_se()` / `contrast_power()` gain terms or change df.**

- **Positional-argument fragility is the headline.** `contrast_power(15, 100, 20, 500, 400, ...)` appears at L210, L216-217, L221-222, L226-227, L229-230, L234 — nine calls that rely on the order `dAs, sigma2_within, n, sigma2_e, P`. Inserting any parameter before `P` silently re-binds all of them and the tests will still "pass" while testing nothing. Same for `contrast_se`'s positional call from `contrast_power` at `validation_functions.R:581-582` (`contrast_se(sigma2_within, n, sigma2_e, P, interaction_frac, delta, n_loc)`).
- **L212-213 breaks if a new term does not vanish as P → ∞**: `se_of(1e9)` is asserted within ±0.1% of `SE_floor`, and `SE_floor` is hard-coded as `sqrt(2 * sigma2_within / n)` at `validation_functions.R:601`. Any added term that is not a function of `P` alone (e.g. another accession-level or location-level variance) must be folded into `SE_floor` or this test fails — and so do L205 (`SE &gt; SE_floor`) and L206/`pct_SE_irreducible` in a subtler way. Note the current `(interaction_frac * delta)^2 / n_loc` term is already excluded from `SE_floor` even though it does not shrink with `P`, so L212 only passes because `interaction_frac` defaults to 0 in that call.
- **L216-218** asserts `SE_floor` strictly decreases in `n`; a reparameterised floor must keep that.
- **L234-236 `check_near(p0$power, 0.05, tol = 1e-6)`** survives a pure df change (central t gives exactly α at any df), but breaks if a new term makes the statistic non-central at `delta = 0` or makes `se` depend on `delta` in a way that does not vanish there.
- **Nothing pins `df`.** There is no assertion anywhere that `df == 2 * n - 2`. It is set at `validation_functions.R:583`, documented at L536 and surfaced as a `df` column in the returned tibble (and in `power_grid.csv`). A Satterthwaite or location-aware df would pass the existing suite silently. Worth adding a test before you change it.
- Also note `contrast_se` has **no caller other than `contrast_power`** in the whole repo (`validation_functions.R:581`); the `contrast_se` hits in `validate_crossval.R:247,267` are an unrelated *column name* in the rehearsal tibble, not a call.
- Downstream, `validation_report.R:144-157` detects staleness only by counting distinct `lambda`, `interaction_frac` and `sided`. A new swept term would **not** be detected and a stale power table would be published silently — that detector must be extended in lockstep.

**(3) `build_pools()` returns pool-specific `sigma2_within`.**

- **No test in `test_validation.R` reads `bp$sigma2_within` at all.** The entire change would pass the suite. That is a real gap: the only places `sigma2_within` is pinned are the §3 literals (`sigma2_within = 100`), which bypass `build_pools()` entirely.
- The breakage is all downstream and would be runtime errors, not test failures:
  - `validation_functions.R:339` currently returns a scalar `sigma2_within = inp$PEV_As + var_blup_within`, with `var_blup_within = mean(c(var(res$plus$As), var(res$minus$As)))` (L325) — the averaging is exactly what you would be removing.
  - `contrast_se` (L551-556) and `contrast_power` (L577) take a **scalar**; `2 * sigma2_within / n` would need to become something like `(sigma2_plus + sigma2_minus) / n`.
  - `power_grid()` L618-625 passes `bp$sigma2_within` into `contrast_power` and then `mutate(sigma2_within = bp$sigma2_within)` — a length-2 value breaks the mutate.
  - `validate_pool_selection.R:88` (`sqrt(bp$sigma2_within)` printed as one number) and `:110` (`sigma2_within = bp$sigma2_within` as a single `pool_summary.csv` column).
  - `validate_power.R:385` (`contrast_power(bp$dAs, bp$sigma2_within, ...)`) and `validate_crossval.R:421-429`.
  - `power_grid.csv` and `pool_summary.csv` both carry a single `sigma2_within` column today (confirmed on disk).
- §4 of the test file (L242-249) also feeds `build_pools(...)$pools` straight into `make_validation_design`, so if the return shape changes in a way that touches `$pools`, that section breaks too.

### `tests/README.md` — tiers and conventions

**Tiers.** Two: a *fast* tier (~45 s, the default) and a *slow* tier (`test_fits.R` only, real BGLR Multitrait + MegaLMM chains, adds ~10 s per the README / minutes per `run_all.R`'s comment), run with `Rscript tests/run_all.R --all`. Single files can be run directly. Each file runs in its own process, prints `&lt;name&gt;: N passed, M failed`, exits non-zero on failure; `run_all.R` aggregates and reports timings. `test_validation.R` is in the fast tier at 2 s, pinning "pool construction, the field design's balance, the power formula" (README L22).

**The stated philosophy (L26-41)**: "Not the framework — `tests/helper.R` is four assertions. It is the **oracles**." Four oracle kinds are named: an **algebraic identity** (to 1e-12), a **planted answer**, a value that was **requested**, and a **negative control** — with "the naive Pr+As / Pr−As rule must produce overlapping pools" cited as the canonical negative control (that is `test_validation.R:86-87`). "A test that only pins today's output freezes bugs as well as behaviour, so there are as few of those as possible."

**Conventions (L62-73), verbatim:**
- `check(cond, msg)`, `check_near(actual, expected, tol, msg)`, `check_error(expr, msg)` — from `tests/helper.R`, sourced by every file.
- "Tolerances say what kind of claim is being made: `1e-12` for an identity, `1e-9` for an empirically rescaled draw, loose bounds for anything Monte Carlo. A tight tolerance on a short MCMC chain is a test that fails for the wrong reason."
- "Every file seeds its RNG at the top. A suite that fails one run in twenty teaches people to ignore it."
- "Nothing reads `output/`, so the suite runs in a fresh clone with no credentials and no network."

**Payoffs recorded (L43-53)** — one is yours: "`test_validation.R` found `make_validation_design()` taking its anchor plots from the *rotated* selection window, so the anchor combination differed at every location — the opposite of what anchors are for. Combination × location was not estimable in any design the script would actually have produced." The fix is the `pick()`/`anchor_n` logic at `validation_functions.R:430-444`; the guard is `test_validation.R:270-275`.
