# Implementation plan: oat–pea validation-trial design chain

## 0. Things in your theory I think are wrong or incomplete

Read these first, because three of them change the plan's content, not just its ordering.

### 0.1 `interaction_frac` stops being self-consistent the moment λ becomes a judgement (change 5 breaks change 4/6)

You wrote "keep the existing `interaction_frac` parameterisation — it is correct and self-consistent. I verified the current code reproduces its published numbers exactly." I reproduced your arithmetic exactly too — `/Users/jj332/Documents/GitRepo/B4I_Fall2026/code/validation_functions.R:551-556` with your Satterthwaite extension gives oat SE² = 8.91 + 20.0 + 6.55 = 35.5, df = 12.3, power 0.700 at λ = 0.7. Your numbers are right.

But `interaction_frac` is defined at `code/validate_crossval.R:342` as `sd(lambda)/abs(mean(lambda))` — a **coefficient of variation of λ**. The SE term is `(interaction_frac · delta)²/n_loc` with `delta = lambda · dAs`. So the interaction SD is `interaction_frac · lambda · dAs`. Substituting a *judgement* λ = 0.7 for the *measured* λ = 0.83 silently rescales the interaction SD down by 0.7/0.83:

| | interaction SD (oat) | v_int | SE | t | df | power |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ratio form, λ=0.7 (your plan) | 0.73·0.7·19.59 = 10.0 | 20.0 | 5.96 | 2.30 | 12.3 | **0.700** |
| absolute form (SD as measured) | 0.73·0.83·19.59 = 11.9 | 28.2 | 6.61 | 2.07 | 9.5 | **0.61** |

The across-location spread of the realised contrast is a property of the environments. It is not proportional to whatever λ you decide to design against. Holding the ratio fixed while lowering λ is the optimistic choice, and it costs **nine points of oat power** — enough to move oat from "adequately powered" to "marginal" under `validate_crossval.R:456-461`'s own verdict thresholds.

**Fix:** have `summarise_folds()` emit `lambda_sd` as a first-class column (it already computes it, line 337) and have `contrast_se()` accept **either** `interaction_frac` (ratio, back-compatible default) **or** `interaction_sd` (absolute, in the units of `delta`), with a guard that exactly one is supplied. Report both rows side by side and let the reader see the choice. I would make the absolute form the headline.

### 0.2 Bumping `POOL_INDEX_VERSION` destroys the A4 stability test you most want

Change 2 replaces the partner-count eligibility rule with a reliability threshold. That is a change to the *selection rule*, so `POOL_INDEX_VERSION` (`validation_functions.R:221`) must be bumped to `single-v2`. But `validate_pool_selection.R:167-173` then correctly announces that churn against `2026-10-03` "measures the RULE CHANGE, not the stability of the effect estimates. The 70% retention guidance does not apply to it."

That is exactly the comparison SELF_CRITIQUE §A4 calls the go/no-go, and it would be voided by the same commit that fixes A1.

**Fix — build the pools twice at the new vintage:**

- **Arm D (data):** new 8-trial fit + the **old** rule (`min_partners &gt;= 3`, counted on the *fitted* data), stamped `single-v1`. Diff against `2026-10-03` → pure data churn. **This is the A4 test.**
- **Arm R (rule):** new 8-trial fit + the new reliability rule, stamped `single-v2`. These are the pools actually proposed. Diff against arm D → pure rule churn.

Cost: one extra `build_pools()` call per species and one extra `pool_diff()`. Without it you get one confounded churn number and the critique's headline question stays unanswered.

### 0.3 A reliability filter alone does not fix the aliasing, which is the actual harm

SELF_CRITIQUE §6 says the cost of finding A "is not mainly power. It is that 12 accessions in the pools have their producer and associate effects perfectly aliased." An accession with one partner has a *structural* pathology: its As BLUP is entirely borrowed from relatives and from the Pr/As covariance, with zero own-plot information separating Pr from As. A well-connected relative set can give such a line a respectable *reliability* while leaving the aliasing intact — reliability measures posterior precision, not identifiability.

**Fix:** keep a hard connectivity floor **alongside** the reliability threshold — `n_partners &gt;= 2` counted on the fitted data, plus `rel_As_i &gt;= rel_min`. Change 2 as stated ("a reliability threshold **instead of** a partner count") would leave the aliased lines eligible if they happen to be well-connected genomically. Make it `and`, not `instead of`.

### 0.4 Estimand 3 is *not* better powered once the location interaction is carried through

Your estimand-3 figure (`t ≈ 2.93`) is explicitly "before any location-interaction term", while estimands 1 and 2 carry it as 57% of the variance. Carrying it through consistently (ratio form, 0.73 × 0.7 = 0.511 slope SD across locations, `v_int = 0.0522`, `v_other = 0.239² = 0.0571`):

SE = 0.331, t = 2.12, df ≈ 15.6, **power ≈ 0.64**.

So estimand 3 is *comparable* to estimand 1, not better. Your verified arithmetic is fine (I reproduced the additivity: 9.28 − (−10.35) = 19.63 ≈ ΔGMA_oat; 35.21 = −10.35 + 19.63 + 25.93). But the headline must become "comparable once treated consistently", not "better powered than feared", or the plan oversells it exactly the way it rightly refuses to oversell estimand 2.

Two further points on estimand 3:
- Because ΔPr ≈ 0 by construction, ΔGMA ≈ ΔAs. So estimand 3 adds a different **response** (total yield), not a different **predictor**. Its value is in the response. Say that, because it is easy to misread as independent evidence.
- `GMA = PrEff + AsEff` is the total-yield predictor only under equal physical weighting of oat and pea grain. Pea is worth more per kg. Make the weight an explicit parameter `w_pea` defaulting to 1 (physical total, the co-primary) with an economically weighted index as a pre-registered **secondary** — otherwise a physical-yield claim gets read as an economic one.

### 0.5 Three co-primary directional tests on the same plots is a multiplicity problem, and estimand 2 does not dissolve it

SELF_CRITIQUE §I says making the slope primary "dissolves §8 item 4 (multiplicity across two primary tests): one estimand per species." But change 7 then adds total yield as a **co-primary**, reinstating the problem with three strongly correlated tests on one set of plots.

**Fix:** pre-register a **fixed-sequence (gatekeeping) procedure**: the two per-species slopes at one-sided α = 0.05 first; the total-yield co-primary tested only if at least one species slope passes. A fixed sequence spends no α and settles §8 item 4 in one sentence. Alternative is a single global claim. Either is fine; leaving it unstated is not.

### 0.6 `df_acc = 2n − 2` is wrong once `sigma2_within` is pool-specific

Your Satterthwaite formula uses `v_acc²/(2n−2)`. With `s2w_plus ≠ s2w_minus` the accession term's own df is Welch:

```
df_acc = (A + B)^2 / ( A^2/(n-1) + B^2/(n-1) ),   A = s2w_plus/n,  B = s2w_minus/n
```

which collapses to `2n − 2` exactly when `A == B`. Changes 3 and 6 interact here; nest the Welch df inside the outer Satterthwaite rather than hard-coding `2n − 2`. The identity at equal variances is the test oracle (see §5.1 below).

Also: `df_e = P − cells` barely matters (`v_plt²/df_e` is 0.1 of a denominator of 102). Make it an argument with a documented default and say it is insensitive, so nobody spends time on it.

### 0.7 Estimand 2 does not escape the 4-df problem

You correctly say estimand 2 should be adopted for interpretability, not power. One thing to add: under `(0 + x_F | location)` replacing `(1|location:poolF)`, the slope test's denominator is dominated by the slope×location variance on `n_loc − 1 = 4` df, exactly as in estimand 1. Its apparent `2n − 2` df is illusory. Do not let the plan imply the continuous estimand buys df.

And two pre-registration mechanics nobody has stated: **`x_F` must be centred** (or the location random slope confounds with the location intercept), and **`x_F` must be frozen into `field_book.csv` at design time**. If the predictor is re-derived from a later refit, estimand 2 is not pre-registered at all. This is a concrete new requirement on `validate_design.R`.

### 0.8 The exact-posterior pool-mean variance is a different question, not a better one

Tempting thought: with draws in hand, compute `Var_s(mean_{i∈plus} g_i − mean_{i∈minus} g_i)` directly, correlations included, instead of `2·sigma2_within/n`. These answer different questions:

- `2·sigma2_within/n` = **broad inference**: "accessions are a random sample of the class we predicted." This is what §4 and `validation_functions.R:532-537` want, it is correctly justified, and SELF_CRITIQUE §7 defends it. Keep it as the power denominator.
- `Var_s(...)` = **narrow inference**: "given these exact 40 lines, how uncertain is their mean true effect." Strictly smaller or larger depending on the sign of the mean off-diagonal posterior covariance — and for lines selected on one tail of a genomic index, that covariance is likely **positive**, meaning the broad-inference formula may be anti-conservative about the *estimates* even while conservative about the *sampling*.

**Recommendation:** keep the broad-inference denominator; add the posterior-covariance number as a **diagnostic** (`pool_posterior.csv`), and report the ratio. It is two lines of code once the draws are read and it bounds the exposure. Do not substitute it.

### 0.9 `lmerTest` is not installed; you do not need it

`lme4 2.0.6` and `pbkrtest 0.5.5` are present; **`lmerTest` is absent**. Do not put `lmer` in the 400-rep simulation loop — it is slow, it adds a dependency, and it is unnecessary.

The pre-registered pool test, when the pool×location term dominates, is algebraically a **one-sample t on the n_loc location-level contrasts, n_loc − 1 = 4 df**. That closed form needs no new package, runs in microseconds, and gives an exact bracket:

```
power(location-contrast t, 4 df)  &lt;=  power(Satterthwaite, 12.3 df)  &lt;=  power(pooled t, 38 df)
```

That bracketing is itself an excellent oracle (§5.5). Use `lme4` only as a low-rep confirmation that the REML fit sits inside the bracket, and get Satterthwaite-equivalent df from `pbkrtest` if you want it, not `lmerTest`.

### 0.10 `validation_vintage()` cannot assert "fit trials == phenotype-table trials" as written

Change 1 says make it "ASSERT it matches the phenotype table." It cannot: the assembled table has **9** trials (3,575 rows, confirmed), the fit will have **8**. The assertion must be against the **QC-filtered** table, which means `validation_inputs()` must itself call `apply_trial_qc()` — **it currently does not** (`validation_functions.R:121-122` reads the raw RDS). That is a second instance of the same structural hole as finding A, and it is why `n_trials = 9` was reported. Spelled out in §2.1.

### 0.11 `interaction_frac` should be corrected for fold-level estimation noise

SELF_CRITIQUE §6/§"What this rests on" says the split between genuine λ×environment and fold-level noise "cannot be made with five trials". It can be made approximately with eight folds, because `calib()` already records `lambda_se` per fold (`validate_crossval.R:156`). Under a constant true λ, `E[var(λ̂)] = var_true + mean(se²)`, so:

```
interaction_frac_corrected = sqrt(max(0, var(lambda_hat) - mean(lambda_se^2))) / |mean(lambda_hat)|
```

This directly attacks the single dominant variance term. It composes with change 4: `lambda_se` must come from the clustered/accession-level fit first, since the plot-level `lambda_se` is anti-conservative (too small), which would make the correction too *small* and the interaction too *large*. Do change 4's SE fix before trusting the correction.

---

## 1. Sequencing

The hard constraints are: **tests before signatures**, **archive before re-run**, **refit before pools**, **crossval before power**, **prose last**. Nine stages.

```
S0  archive the 2026-10-03 vintage                        (5 min, no code)
S1  harden the test suite against the coming changes      (no production code touched)
S2  trial set: single source of truth + fit-set assertion (change 1)
S3  retain BGLR draws; write per-accession PEV            (change 2a) -- THE REFIT
S4  reliability eligibility + pool-specific sigma2_within (changes 2b, 3)
S5  lambda: QC the folds, two scales, by year, noise-corrected (changes 4, 5)
S6  one pre-registered model; three estimands; new SE/df  (changes 6, 7)
S7  null simulation with As x location                    (change 6)
S8  reporting, registry, driver hardening, prose          (changes 8, 9 hooks)
```

S1 must precede S2–S7 entirely. S2 and S3 are one commit and one refit (both touch `BGLR_multi_trait_model.R`; refitting twice wastes 15 min and creates a half-fixed vintage). S4 cannot start until S3's PEV file exists. S6's power numbers cannot be final until S5 gives λ. S7 depends on S6's SE/df. S8 last, because `--refresh-doc` reads the finished vintage.

**Do not** reorder S1 after S2. The positional-argument hazard and the missing-df pin are the two ways this work can silently produce wrong numbers through a green suite.

---

## 2. Stage by stage

### S0. Archive the baseline

`output/validation/2026-10-03/` holds the 5-trial pools and is the only baseline for the churn comparison. `validate_pool_selection.R:129-135` picks the previous vintage by lexicographic sort of directories containing `pools.csv`, so the archive name must sort **after** `2026-10-03` and **before** `2026-10-04`:

```bash
mv output/validation/2026-10-03 output/validation/2026-10-03_5trial-whitelist
```

`_` is 0x5F &gt; `3` is 0x33, and `2026-10-04` &gt; `2026-10-03_...` on the 10th character. Verified. The existing `2026-10-02_1` directory shows this convention is already in use. `output/*` is gitignored, so nothing to commit.

Also drop a one-line `output/validation/2026-10-03_5trial-whitelist/PROVENANCE.txt` saying it is the 5-trial whitelist fit, since nothing in the directory records that — which is finding A in miniature.

### S1. Harden the test suite (before any signature changes)

All in `/Users/jj332/Documents/GitRepo/B4I_Fall2026/tests/test_validation.R`. Nothing in `code/` changes. Suite must be green at the end of S1 and green again after every later stage.

**S1.1 — pin `df` before the df changes.** Nothing anywhere pins `df == 2*n-2`, so a Satterthwaite change would pass silently.

```r
check(contrast_power(dAs = 15, sigma2_within = 100, n = 20,
                     sigma2_e = 500, P = 400)$df == 38,
      "df is 2n-2 when no pool x location term is present")
check(contrast_power(dAs = 15, sigma2_within = 100, n = 30,
                     sigma2_e = 500, P = 400)$df == 58,
      "and tracks n")
```
Oracle: requested value, algebraically known. After S6 these become the `interaction_frac = 0` boundary case of the Satterthwaite formula, which they must still satisfy exactly — that is the whole point of adding them now.

**S1.2 — pin `SE_floor`'s *semantics*, not its formula.** `test_validation.R:212-213` asserts `se_of(1e9)` ≈ `SE_floor` and only passes because `interaction_frac` defaults to 0. Add the nonzero-interaction case now, so that S6 is forced to fold the interaction term into the floor rather than break the test:

```r
se_big &lt;- contrast_power(15, sigma2_within = 100, n = 20, sigma2_e = 500,
                         P = 1e9, interaction_frac = 0.5)
check(abs(se_big$SE - se_big$SE_floor) &lt; 1e-6 * se_big$SE,
      "SE_floor is the P -&gt; infinity limit, interaction included")
# NEGATIVE: the accession-only floor must be strictly below it
check(sqrt(2 * 100 / 20) &lt; se_big$SE_floor - 1e-8,
      "the accession-only floor is NOT the P -&gt; infinity limit when interaction &gt; 0")
```
This test **fails today** — it is the test that forces the §4 redefinition. Add it as an expected-fail in S1 and let S6 turn it green, or add it in the same commit as S6 with the redefinition. I prefer the former: an explicitly-recorded red test is the clearest statement of intent.

**S1.3 — pin the candidate-filter oracle in one place.** `test_validation.R:62`, `:98-99` and `:101` re-implement `build_pools()`'s candidate filter by hand in three places. Any filter change (S4) must be mirrored in all three or the oracles at `:63` and `:72` silently compare the wrong candidate set. Replace the three copies with one helper used everywhere:

```r
.cand_of &lt;- function(inp, bp) dplyr::filter(inp$accessions, eligible, Pr &gt;= bp$pr_min)
```
and in S4 extend `build_pools()` to **return** `candidates` (the filtered tibble) so the test can assert `setequal(bp$candidates$acc, .cand_of(inp, bp)$acc)` — turning a duplicated re-implementation into a single algebraic identity. This is the single highest-value structural change to the test file.

**S1.4 — pin the `make_inputs()` fixture against the shape change.** ~50 of ~53 assertions route through `build_pools(inp, ...)`, and S4 adds required per-accession columns (`PEV_As_i`, `rel_As_i`). Add, now, a schema assertion that documents the contract:

```r
REQUIRED_ACC_COLS &lt;- c("acc", "Pr", "As", "n_partners", "n_plots", "eligible")
check(all(REQUIRED_ACC_COLS %in% names(inp$accessions)),
      "the fixture supplies every column build_pools() requires")
```
Then in S4, extend `make_inputs()` **and** `REQUIRED_ACC_COLS` together, and give `make_inputs()` a `rel = 0.3` argument generating a plausible per-accession reliability spread. One edit, not fifty.

**S1.5 — the positional-argument negative control.** See §3. Add after the signature change, not before — it must fail before and pass after.

**S1.6 — pin `power_grid()` and `contrast_se()`, which have no coverage at all.**

```r
# contrast_se: algebraic identity against the three-term formula written out
check_near(contrast_se(sigma2_within = 100, n = 20, sigma2_e = 500, P = 400,
                       interaction_frac = 0.4, delta = 10, n_loc = 5),
           sqrt(2*100/20 + 4*500/400 + (0.4*10)^2/5), tol = 1e-12,
           "contrast_se is exactly its documented formula")
# power_grid: shape and that it rebuilds pools per n
g &lt;- power_grid(inp, n_values = c(10, 20), P_values = c(300, 400),
                lambda_values = 0.7, interaction_values = 0.5)
check(nrow(g) == 4, "power_grid returns one row per (n, P, lambda, interaction)")
check(dplyr::n_distinct(g$dAs_predicted) == 2,
      "the pools are rebuilt at each n -- the trade-off is not held fixed")
check_near(g$sigma2_within[g$n == 20][1],
           suppressWarnings(build_pools(inp, 20))$sigma2_within, tol = 1e-12,
           "the grid's sigma2_within is the one build_pools computed")
```
That last one is the oracle that would catch a length-2 `sigma2_within` silently recycling through `mutate()` at `validation_functions.R:618-625`.

**S1.7 — pin the old eligibility test as a *versioned* test.** `test_validation.R:169-173` encodes the old rule. Do not delete it in S4 — rename it to say it pins the `single-v1` rule, keep it running against an `eligibility = "partners"` arm, and add the new reliability test alongside. Arm D of §0.2 needs the old rule to keep working anyway.

**Runtime:** `test_validation.R` is 2 s today; these additions keep it under 5 s.

### S2 + S3. Trial set and draw retention — one commit, one refit

**S2.1 — remove the whitelist, derive the trial set from QC.**

**S2.2 — take the refactor choice: (a), call `fit_producer_associate()`.**

You asked for both options flagged. I recommend **(a)** and would not do (b):

- The defect in finding A *is* the duplicated filter chain. Two copies of the trial logic is what let the whitelist and `apply_trial_qc()` disagree. Fixing the symptom while keeping the duplication invites a third divergence.

Concretely:
- Extend `fit_producer_associate()` (`code/dge_ige_functions.R:324-502`): change `save_effects = FALSE` to `save_effects = character(0)` — a **vector of term names** (`c("G_oat","G_pea")`, `"G_mix"`, or `TRUE` meaning all genetic terms, for back-compatibility with `test_fits.R:313` and `code/sim_fit.R`). The `saveAt`-must-be-explicit guard at `:341-345` stays and now applies to any streamed term.

Keep in the script: the monoculture filter, the design diagnostics `:202-244`, the GRM printouts `:280-287`, the 4-seed loop, the rank-stability and figure sections.
- **Behaviour difference to preserve:** `fit_producer_associate()` is called with the *full* GRMs and subsets internally (`:358-359`); the production script currently **errors** on accessions absent from the GRMs (`:258-263`), while `b4i_plot_table()` *drops* them. Keep the production script's hard error by running the existing `setdiff` check before the call. Do not quietly adopt the dropping behaviour in production.

```r
pheno_trials &lt;- sort(unique(as.character(pheno$studyName)))
if (!setequal(fit_trials, pheno_trials)) {
  stop("the fit used a different trial set from the QC-filtered phenotype table.\n",
       "  fit only:   ", paste(setdiff(fit_trials, pheno_trials), collapse = ", "), "\n",
       "  pheno only: ", paste(setdiff(pheno_trials, fit_trials), collapse = ", "), "\n",
       "This is SELF_CRITIQUE.md finding A. Re-run code/BGLR_multi_trait_model.R.",

```

Either route both through one shared filter function, or assert the trial set exactly and report both plot counts side by side in `vintage.csv` (`n_plots_pheno`, `n_plots_fit`) with a warning if they differ by more than, say, 2%. I recommend the shared-filter route: lift the monoculture + NA filter out of `BGLR_multi_trait_model.R:145-184` into a named function in `dge_ige_functions.R` and call it from both. Then exact equality is assertable, and that is the stronger guarantee.
- Add a **missing-file branch**: if `BGLR_fit_trials.csv` does not exist, `stop()` loudly. This must NOT follow `apply_trial_qc()`'s "a missing file is not a failure" pattern — a missing fit-trial record is exactly the condition that produced finding A, and silently falling back to the phenotype count reinstates the bug. Different situation, different policy; say so in the comment.

**S2.5 — per-accession PEV, computed where `L` and the draws are both in hand.**

Do this in `BGLR_multi_trait_model.R`, **not** in `validation_functions.R`. Reasons: `validation_functions.R` stays free of BGLR; nothing downstream needs the 53 MiB of `.bin` files or `L`; and it is a CSV read for every consumer.

```r
# per-accession posterior variance of the accession-scale effects.
# fit$ETA[[term]]$SD.beta is NOT a route: Var(L b) needs the full p x p
# posterior covariance of b, which BGLR does not accumulate.
acc_pev &lt;- function(prefix, term, L, roles) {
  draws &lt;- read_beta_draws(prefix, term = paste0("ETA_", term),
                           nIter = nIter, burnIn = burnIn, thin = thin,
                           p = ncol(L), traits = length(DGE_IGE_TRAITS),
                           storage_mode = "single")
  # [n_draws, p_acc] per trait, in one matmul -- no loop needed
  g &lt;- lapply(seq_along(DGE_IGE_TRAITS),
              function(k) tcrossprod(draws[, , k], L))
  names(g) &lt;- DGE_IGE_TRAITS
  Pr &lt;- g[[roles[["Pr"]]]]; As &lt;- g[[roles[["As"]]]]
  tibble::tibble(
    acc      = rownames(L),
    PEV_Pr   = apply(Pr, 2, stats::var),
    PEV_As   = apply(As, 2, stats::var),
    cov_PrAs = vapply(seq_len(ncol(Pr)), \(i) stats::cov(Pr[, i], As[, i]), 0),
    PEV_GMA  = PEV_Pr + PEV_As + 2 * cov_PrAs
  )
}
```
`read_beta_draws()` at `code/interaction_decomp.R:259-304` is reused unchanged — only `term` and `p` differ, exactly as you established. `p = ncol(L_oat) = 442`, `ncol(L_pea) = 423`. The matmul is 1700 × 442 × 442 ≈ 3×10⁸ FLOPs — instant.

Outputs:
- `output/BGLR_{oat,pea}_pev.csv` — `seed, acc, PEV_Pr, PEV_As, cov_PrAs, PEV_GMA` across all 4 seeds (the mean over seeds is what `validation_inputs()` uses, consistent with how `Pr`/`As` are already averaged at `validation_functions.R:143-144`).
- `output/BGLR_{oat,pea}_As_draws_seed12567.rds` — the `[1700, 442]` accession-scale As draw matrix for **seed 1 only** (6 MiB/species), for the §0.8 pool-level posterior diagnostic. Not needed by the eligibility filter.

**Note `PEV_GMA` is free here and is what estimand 3 needs** — the per-accession PEV of the total-yield predictor, including the Pr–As covariance. Compute it now; you will want it in S6.

**Runtime for the refit:** the 5-trial fit took ~45 s/seed (17:37→17:40 for 4 seeds). At 8 trials / ~3,180 plots expect ~2× → **~6–8 min for 4 seeds**, plus ~1 min for the PEV pass. Call it 10 min.

### S4. Reliability eligibility and pool-specific `sigma2_within`

**S4.1 — `validation_inputs()` return shape.** You asked specifically about this. The contract becomes, per species:

```r
list(
  species = "oat",
  accessions = tibble(
    acc, Pr, As, GMA,            # GMA = Pr + As, new; estimand 3's predictor
    n_partners, n_plots,         # n_partners now counted on the FITTED data
    PEV_Pr_i, PEV_As_i, PEV_GMA_i,   # per-accession, from BGLR_*_pev.csv
    rel_As_i, rel_GMA_i,         # 1 - PEV_i/sigma2_*, clamped elementwise
    eligible                     # rel_As_i &gt;= rel_min  AND  n_partners &gt;= partner_floor
  ),
  eligibility = list(rule = "reliability-v2", rel_min = ..., partner_floor = 2L,
                     n_eligible = ..., n_total = ...),
  # scalars, unchanged names and meanings -- every existing consumer keeps working
  sigma2_Pr, sigma2_As, sigma2_e, response,
  rel_Pr, rel_As, PEV_As,        # the GLOBAL versions, retained
  sigma2_GMA, rel_GMA,           # new, for estimand 3
  # provenance
  n_trials, n_plots, fit_trials, pool_index = POOL_INDEX_VERSION
)
```

**Keep `PEV_As` (the global scalar) and `rel_As`.** `validate_power.R:132` and `validate_pool_selection.R:89,110` read them, and so does `test_validation.R`'s fixture. The per-accession versions are *additions*, suffixed `_i`. Nothing's type changes. The old `min_partners` field stays too (as `eligibility$partner_floor` plus a deprecated top-level alias) so arm D of §0.2 and `validate_pool_selection.R:97,211` keep working.

Add an `eligibility` argument: `eligibility = c("reliability", "partners")`, defaulting to `"reliability"`, with `min_partners` and `rel_min` both accepted. Arm D passes `eligibility = "partners"`.

**S4.2 — choosing `rel_min`.** Do not guess. Calibrate: pick the `rel_min` whose candidate count on the fitted data is closest to what `min_partners &gt;= 3` gives on the fitted data (~248 oat / ~244 pea per SELF_CRITIQUE §A1), then **report the implied absolute reliability** and sweep ±0.05 in `power_grid()`. Guard rails that must hold: oat needs ≥ 2n = 40 candidates after the 0.5 Pr quantile, i.e. ≥ 80 eligible; pea needs ≥ 120 eligible. `build_pools()` already errors informatively at `validation_functions.R:267-271`; make sure `validate_pool_selection.R` catches that error and names `rel_min` as the knob.

Elementwise clamping: reuse the existing `rel()` closure (`validation_functions.R:156`) vectorised — `rel_As_i` can exceed 1 or go negative for individual accessions from short-chain noise, and an unclamped negative PEV flatters every power estimate exactly as the current comment warns.

**S4.3 — pool-specific `sigma2_within` by ADDITION, not mutation.** You listed six downstream breakages from making `sigma2_within` length-2. Avoid all of them:

`build_pools()` returns, **in addition to** the existing scalar `sigma2_within` (unchanged — still the mean of the two pools, which is what `var_blup_within` at `:325` already is):

```r
sigma2_within_plus  = mean(res$plus$PEV_As_i)  + var(res$plus$As),
sigma2_within_minus = mean(res$minus$PEV_As_i) + var(res$minus$As),
PEV_plus = mean(res$plus$PEV_As_i),  PEV_minus = mean(res$minus$PEV_As_i),
var_blup_plus = var(res$plus$As),    var_blup_minus = var(res$minus$As),
candidates = cand                     # for S1.3's identity oracle
```
and `sigma2_within &lt;- mean(c(sigma2_within_plus, sigma2_within_minus))`.

`contrast_se()` accepts `sigma2_within` of **length 1 or 2**:
```r
v_acc &lt;- if (length(sigma2_within) == 2L) sum(sigma2_within)/n else 2*sigma2_within/n
```
The two agree exactly when `s[1] == s[2]`, which is the back-compatibility oracle (§5.1). `power_grid()`'s `mutate(sigma2_within = bp$sigma2_within)` at `:618-625` stays length-1 and keeps working; add two more `mutate()` columns. `pool_summary.csv` and `power_grid.csv` gain columns and lose none. `validate_power.R:385` and `validate_crossval.R:429-430` get the length-2 vector passed by name. `validate_pool_selection.R:88,110` keep reading the scalar.

This is the single most important structural decision in the plan: **addition over mutation** turns a six-site breakage into zero.

**S4.4 — the eligibility rule's other call sites.** All must move together:
- `validate_pool_selection.R:97` — replace `all(bp$pools$n_partners &gt;= min_partners)` with `all(bp$pools$rel_As_i &gt;= rel_min) &amp;&amp; all(bp$pools$n_partners &gt;= partner_floor)`, and make `build_pools()` carry `rel_As_i`/`PEV_As_i` through `transmute()` at `:319-320` (otherwise `pools.csv` drops them and `validate_design.R` cannot write them into the field book).
- `validate_pool_selection.R:78` — the eligible-count line; `:211-213` — the figure subtitle *states the rule to the reader* and must be regenerated from `inp$eligibility`, not hard-coded.
- `validation_functions.R:638` (`n_eligible` in `validation_vintage()`) and the `pools.csv` columns at `:319-320`.
- `validation_report.R:78-81` — the vintage block's `eligible` column header.
- `validate_crossval.R:207-226` — its own `fake_inp` sets `PEV_As = 0` and `eligible = TRUE`. The rehearsal legitimately does not need reliability (it only needs the pools, as the comment says), so give it `eligibility = "partners"` explicitly and add `PEV_As_i = 0`, `rel_As_i = 1` to the fake accession table. Do **not** make the rehearsal reliability-filtered: computing per-fold per-accession PEV would mean streaming draws from 8 refits.

**S4.5 — the pool-level posterior diagnostic (§0.8).** New output `output/validation/&lt;vintage&gt;/pool_posterior.csv`:

computed from `BGLR_{oat,pea}_As_draws_seed12567.rds`. Four lines of code. If `ratio` is far from 1, say so in the report; it bounds the narrow-inference exposure without changing the power claim.

**S4.6 — run both arms.** `validate_pool_selection.R` builds arm R (proposed, `single-v2`) and arm D (`single-v1`, old rule on fitted data), writes `pools.csv` (arm R), `pools_armD.csv`, `pool_diff.csv` (arm D vs `2026-10-03_5trial-whitelist` = **the A4 data-churn test**) and `pool_diff_rule.csv` (arm R vs arm D = rule churn). ### S5. λ: QC the folds, two scales, by year, noise-corrected

All in `/Users/jj332/Documents/GitRepo/B4I_Fall2026/code/validate_crossval.R`.

**S5.1 — apply the QC screen (finding A2).** Insert `apply_trial_qc()` into the pheno read at `:90-98`. The folds go 9 → 8 and AL leaves every training set, so the fit-data policy finally matches production. Consequence to expect and to state: the `sd_floor` exclusion at `:323-324` currently drops AL, so after QC the `all same-line folds` and `informative folds only` sets may **converge**, which partly resolves findings G and I — λ becomes unconditional on a fold-inclusion rule chosen by looking at λ. Report both sets regardless; if they are now identical, say so, because it is a result.

**S5.2 — fix `calib()`'s SE before anything leans on it (finding G).** `calib()` at `:149-161` fits `lm(peaYield ~ oat_As + pea_Pr)` at the **plot** level, so `lambda_p` and `lambda_se` are anti-conservative. `VALIDATION_DESIGN.md` §7b uses `lambda_p` as a trial-exclusion criterion and `validate_crossval.R:425` uses `lambda_sd` for λ's CI. Two options, do **both**:
- Add an **accession-level** calibration: average the partner-Pr-adjusted response to the focal accession (the `acc_level()` machinery at `:172-187` already does this) and regress on `As`. Report as `lambda_a`, `lambda_a_se`, `lambda_a_p`.
- Keep the plot-level slope (it is unbiased) but replace its SE with a **cluster-robust** one by focal accession. `sandwich::vcovCL` if available; otherwise a one-line block-bootstrap over accessions, which needs no new dependency.

Use the accession-level or clustered SE wherever a *p*-value or a CI is taken. Label the plot-level `lambda_p` as deprecated in the fold table rather than deleting it, so §7b's worked example stays readable.

**S5.3 — λ two ways (change 4).** Add a second calibration on the within-trial-standardised response. `calib()` already has the held-out trial centred at `:140-143`; add a scaled copy:
```r
dat &lt;- dplyr::mutate(dat, peaYield_z = peaYield / stats::sd(peaYield),
                          oatYield_z = oatYield / stats::sd(oatYield))
```
(the trial mean is already removed, so this is a pure scale change). New fold columns `lambda_z`, `lambda_z_se`, `lambda_z_p`, and `scale` as a factor so `summarise_folds()` can group by it.

`summarise_folds()` gains a `scale` grouping and emits, per (species, scale, set):
```
lambda_mean, lambda_median, lambda_sd, lambda_se_of_mean,
interaction_frac,                                   # sd/|mean|, scale-free -&gt; comparable
interaction_frac_corrected,                         # S0.11, noise-removed
mean_lambda_se2,                                    # the noise being removed
interaction_sd_raw                                  # sd(lambda) * dAs, absolute (S0.1)
```
**The comparison is on `interaction_frac`, which is scale-free and therefore legitimately comparable across the two scales** — your theory is right here. Expect the standardised version to be *smaller* almost by construction (standardising removes exactly the between-trial scale variation the raw slope absorbs), so the interesting question is how much of it survives, which is what `interaction_frac_corrected` answers.

New output `output/validation/&lt;vintage&gt;/crossval_lambda_scales.csv`.

**S5.4 — λ by year (change 5).** Join `studyYear` from the pheno table (`distinct(studyName, studyYear)`) rather than parsing the trial name — the name convention is not a contract. Emit `crossval_lambda_year.csv`: per (species, scale, studyYear) the mean λ, SD, n_folds. Expect oat 2025 ≈ 1.38 (3 informative folds after AL leaves), 2026 ≈ 0.63.

**S5.5 — λ = 0.7 as the design value.** Add to `VALIDATION_DEFAULTS` (`validation_functions.R:46-69`):
```r
# The DESIGN value of lambda: a judgement, not a measurement. The pooled
# estimate is 0.83 and the 2026 folds average 0.63; 0.7 is chosen as the
# forward-looking value for a new year (SELF_CRITIQUE.md finding B) and the
# trial is sized on it. The measured value is reported alongside, always.
lambda_design = 0.7,
lambda_design_note = "judgement; see VALIDATION_DESIGN.md section 6",
```
`validate_power.R` and `validate_crossval.R` report power at **both** `lambda_design` and the measured λ, in one table with a `lambda_source` column (`"design"` / `"measured"` / `"measured_lo"`). **And at both interaction parameterisations** (§0.1) — four rows per species. That is the table the go/no-go is read off, and it must not hide the 0.70-vs-0.61 difference.

### S6. One pre-registered model, three estimands, new SE and df

**S6.1 — write the model down once, in code.** New section in `validation_functions.R`, exported as data so the report and the field book can both print it:

```r
# One random-effects structure, three instantiations. y is the response for
# focal species F against partner Pa.
PREREG_MODEL &lt;- list(
  version = "prereg-v1",
  base = "y ~ location + (1|location:block) + &lt;FIXED&gt; + &lt;AsxE&gt;
            + (1|accF) + (1|accPa) + (1|combination) + e",
  estimands = list(
    pool = list(
      fixed = "poolF + poolPa + poolF:poolPa",
      AsxE  = "(1|location:poolF) + (1|location:poolPa)",
      test  = "poolF coefficient, one-sided",
      role  = "pre-registered two-point summary of `slope`"),
    slope = list(
      fixed = "x_F + x_Pa",                      # centred predicted As BLUP
      AsxE  = "(0 + x_F | location) + (0 + x_Pa | location)",
      test  = "slope on x_F &gt; 0, one-sided",
      role  = "PRIMARY: the slope IS lambda measured in new data, on the same
               scale as the cross-validation's interaction_frac"),
    total = list(
      response = "oat_yield + w_pea * pea_yield",
      fixed = "x_total",                          # GMA_oat + GMA_pea, centred
      AsxE  = "(0 + x_total | location)",
      rand  = "(1|oat_acc) + (1|pea_acc) + (1|combination)",
      test  = "slope on x_total &gt; 0, one-sided",
      role  = "co-primary, gatekept behind `slope` (see multiplicity)")
  ),
  multiplicity = "fixed sequence: the two per-species `slope` tests at
                  one-sided alpha 0.05; `total` tested only if at least one
                  passes. Spends no alpha. Settles section 8 item 4.",
  secondary = c("poolF:poolPa", "combination variance from the anchors",
                "realised As regressed on height/flowering/maturity (H2)",
                "economically weighted total (w_pea != 1)")
)
```
`(1|combination)` drops to secondary per SELF_CRITIQUE §C's recommendation — it is estimable almost entirely from the 4 anchor combinations and will sit near the boundary. Say so in the structure, do not silently omit it.

**S6.2 — the new `contrast_se()` / `contrast_power()` signatures.** See §3 for the positional-trap handling; the semantics:

```r
contrast_se &lt;- function(sigma2_within, n, sigma2_e, P, ...,
                        interaction_frac = NULL, interaction_sd = NULL,
                        delta = 0, n_loc = 5L, df_e_lost = 4L,
                        .components = FALSE)
```
- `sigma2_within` length 1 or 2 (§S4.3).
- exactly one of `interaction_frac` / `interaction_sd`; both NULL means no interaction (back-compatible with the current `interaction_frac = 0` default — keep `0` working as "none").
- `.components = TRUE` returns `list(se, v_acc, v_int, v_plt, df_acc, df)` so `contrast_power()` and the tests can read the pieces without recomputing them. This matters: duplicating the variance split in two functions is how the df and the SE drift apart.

```r
contrast_power &lt;- function(dAs, ..., sigma2_within, n, sigma2_e, P,
                           lambda = 1, alpha = 0.05, sided = 1L,
                           interaction_frac = NULL, interaction_sd = NULL,
                           n_loc = 5L, df_e_lost = 4L, df_mode = "satterthwaite")
```
`df_mode = c("satterthwaite", "naive")`. `"naive"` returns `2n − 2` and exists for exactly one reason: to keep S1.1's pin meaningful and to let the report show the old number next to the new one. Default `"satterthwaite"`.

The df:
```r
df_acc &lt;- (A + B)^2 / (A^2/(n-1) + B^2/(n-1))        # Welch; == 2n-2 when A == B
df     &lt;- (v_acc + v_int + v_plt)^2 /
          (v_acc^2/df_acc + v_int^2/(n_loc - 1) + v_plt^2/(P - df_e_lost))
```

New returned columns, replacing/augmenting `:593-604`:
```
v_acc, v_int, v_plt,                  # the three variance contributions
pct_var_accessions, pct_var_interaction, pct_var_plots,   # sum to 100
df_acc, df, df_naive,
SE_floor,              # REDEFINED: sqrt(v_acc + v_int) -- the P -&gt; inf limit
SE_accession_only,     # sqrt(v_acc) -- the OLD SE_floor, kept under a new name
pct_SE_irreducible,    # 100 * SE_floor / SE   (now includes interaction)
interaction_mode       # "frac" | "sd" | "none" -- so the report can detect a sweep
```

**S6.3 — `SE_floor` and `pct_SE_irreducible`: yes, redefine, and here is exactly what to do with `test_validation.R:212-213`.**

You asked this directly. `SE_floor`'s *contract* in the document and in `validate_power.R:310-334`/`power_ceiling.csv` is "the standard error with infinitely many plots." With two non-P terms, the P → ∞ limit is `sqrt(v_acc + v_int)`, so:

- **`SE_floor` keeps its name and its contract, and gains the interaction term.** That is the minimal change that keeps `power_ceiling.csv`, the `power_ceiling` column at `:324-327` and the §5 "Why more plots buy so little" argument all meaning what they say.
- **The old formula survives as `SE_accession_only`.** `test_validation.R:216-218` ("more accessions per pool lowers the floor") should point at `SE_accession_only`, because that is the claim it is actually making — more accessions lower the *accession* term; they do not lower the interaction term at all. Repointing it makes the test say something true rather than something accidentally true.
- **`pct_SE_irreducible` becomes `100 * SE_floor / SE`.** It rises substantially — for oat at λ = 0.7, P = 400 it goes from ~50% to `100*sqrt(28.9)/5.96 = 90%`. That is the honest number and it strengthens §5's argument rather than weakening it: 90% of the SE is untouchable by plots, and the untouchable part is now *mostly pool × location*, which points at **more locations** as the only lever. Add `n_loc` to `power_grid()`'s sweep so the document can say so with a number. This is arguably the most useful consequence of the whole change set and it is currently invisible.

**`test_validation.R:212-213` becomes three assertions:**
```r
# 1. the convergence claim, at zero interaction -- unchanged in meaning
se0 &lt;- contrast_power(15, sigma2_within = 100, n = 20, sigma2_e = 500, P = 400)
check(abs(se_of(1e9) - se0$SE_floor) &lt; 1e-3 * se0$SE_floor,
      "with enough plots the SE converges on the floor, not on zero")
# 2. the SAME claim with interaction on -- the test that forces the redefinition
seI &lt;- contrast_power(15, sigma2_within = 100, n = 20, sigma2_e = 500,
                      P = 1e9, interaction_frac = 0.5)
check(abs(seI$SE - seI$SE_floor) &lt; 1e-6 * seI$SE,
      "SE_floor is the P -&gt; infinity limit with the interaction term too")
# 3. NEGATIVE: the old floor is not the limit
check(seI$SE_accession_only &lt; seI$SE_floor - 1e-8,
      "the accession-only floor is strictly below the P -&gt; infinity limit")
```
Oracle: algebraic limit plus a negative control. Note that assertion 1's tolerance must loosen from 0.1% to ~0.1%—still fine—because `4*sigma2_e/1e9` is 2e-6 against an `SE_floor` of ~3.2; it was always a limit test, not a value test.

**S6.4 — estimand 2's analytic power.** One new function:
```r
slope_power &lt;- function(dAs, var_x_within, sigma2_within, n, sigma2_e, P, ...,
                        lambda, interaction_frac = NULL, interaction_sd = NULL,
                        n_loc, alpha = 0.05, sided = 1L)
```
with `Var(x)` over the 2n accessions `= (dAs/2)^2 + var_x_within`, which `build_pools()` can return as `var_As_within_pools`. Your worked case: `(19.59/2)² + 3.5 = 95.9 + 3.5 = 99.4`, 3.6% more predictor variance than the two-point summary's 95.9, `t` 3.54 → 3.64. Reproduce that as a **requested-value test** (§5.4) — it is the oracle for the claim that the plan must *not* oversell estimand 2.

**S6.5 — estimand 3's analytic power.** `total_yield_power()`, same three-term/Satterthwaite skeleton, with:
- predictor `x_total = GMA_oat + w_pea * GMA_pea`, `var(x_total)` computed from the actual 2×2 cell structure of the realised design plus within-cell spread (your 318.9 — reproduce it from `field_book.csv` rather than hard-coding);
- `sigma2_e_total = var_oat + w_pea^2 * var_pea + 2*w_pea*cov_pea_oat` = 1335.6 + 655.2 − 2(98.3) = **1794.2** (you have 1793.5 — close enough that we should compute it from `BGLR_variance_components.csv` rather than either literal);
- the accession term from `PEV_GMA_i` (which S2.5 already writes) for **both** species, since both `(1|oat_acc)` and `(1|pea_acc)` are retained;
- `interaction_sd` / `interaction_frac` carried through exactly as for estimands 1 and 2 — **this is the §0.4 correction** and it must not be skipped.

New output `output/validation/&lt;vintage&gt;/estimand_power.csv`: one row per (estimand, species-or-total, P, n_loc, lambda_source, interaction_mode) with `delta_or_slope, SE, v_acc, v_int, v_plt, df, t, power`. **This one file replaces the scattered power tables as the single pre-registered power statement**, which is what change 6 asks for. `power_grid.csv` and `power_ceiling.csv` stay (for the budget/pool-size sweep) but the document's headline table comes from `estimand_power.csv`.

**S6.6 — `validate_design.R` must freeze the predictors.** Add `x_As` and `x_GMA` columns per focal accession to `field_book.csv`, read from `pools.csv` (which S4.4 extended). Add a VERDICT check that no pool member has a missing predictor. Without this, estimands 2 and 3 are not pre-registered (§0.7).

Also: fix the exit status. `validate_design.R:128-146` builds `ok` and prints it and never calls `quit()`, so a partner-balance failure writes the field book, exits 0, and `validate_refresh.R:166` records "ok". Add `quit(save = "no", status = if (ok) 0L else 1L)` after `:150`, and add a `--vintage` flag (it currently binds to today's date at `:41` with no override, so it cannot be re-run against yesterday's pools).

### S7. The null simulation with As × location

In `/Users/jj332/Documents/GitRepo/B4I_Fall2026/code/validate_power.R`.

**S7.1 — a null that is exactly a null for the pool contrast.** Your spec — "As main effects 0 but As×location nonzero with zero mean across locations" — needs care: if only the *population* mean is zero, individual accessions keep nonzero main effects and it is not a null for the pool test. The exactly-planted construction:

```r
# As x location with EXACTLY zero pool main effect and exactly zero mean over
# locations: draw one deviation per location, centre it to sum zero, then add
# +d to every As+ member and -d to every As- member at that location. The
# pool x location contrast then has SD 2*tau and mean exactly 0, so the type-I
# rate measures the thing it is supposed to.
d &lt;- stats::rnorm(n_loc, 0, tau); d &lt;- d - mean(d)
```
`tau` is set from the measured interaction: `tau = interaction_sd/2` where `interaction_sd = interaction_frac * lambda * dAs` (or the absolute form). Exact planting is the oracle style `tests/README.md` demands.

**S7.2 — also give the alternative a location structure.** Under the alternative, add the same `d` on top of the nonzero As main effects, so the simulation's SE finally includes the term the analytic formula's dominant component describes. Today `simulate_validation()` generates only location **main** effects (`:146-147`, `rnorm(n_loc, 0, 40/25)`), which is why the simulation has never tested `interaction_frac` at all.

**S7.3 — the two models to compare, without `lmer` (§0.9).** `test_one()` at `:163-180` becomes `test_pool()` with a `denominator` argument:
- `"pooled"` — the existing two-stage t on `2n − 2` df. The model **omitting** `location:pool`.
- `"location"` — compute the pool contrast of accession means **within each location**, then a one-sample t on the `n_loc` location-level contrasts, `n_loc − 1 = 4` df. This is the closed form of the pre-registered model when `location:pool` dominates.

Run **both on the same simulated data with the same seeds** and report the pair. The comparison is the point of change 6, and it must be read as a *paired difference*, not against nominal 0.05 — `simulate_validation()`'s equal-variance t is already known to sit below nominal (0.028/0.025) because of unequal replication (`validate_power.R:438-444`). The expected result: under the As×location null, `"pooled"` rejects well above its own baseline (the inflation that SELF_CRITIQUE §C1 predicts) while `"location"` stays at it. **That comparison is the finding.**

**S7.4 — reps.** Type-I at 400 reps has MC SE 0.011, too coarse to distinguish 0.05 from 0.08. Run the **null arms at 2,000 reps** (MC SE 0.005) and the alternative arms at the existing 400. Both tests are vectorised dplyr, not MCMC; 2,000 reps × 400 plots ≈ 2–4 min per arm.

**S7.5 — optional `lme4` confirmation.** 200 reps at one budget, fitting the actual `lmer` for estimand 1, asserting the realised power sits inside the `[location, pooled]` bracket. `lme4 2.0.6` is present; `lmerTest` is **not** — use `pbkrtest` for df if wanted, or just compare rejection rates at the `lmer` profile/Wald test. Make this arm **opt-in behind a flag** so the default run has no new dependency.

**S7.6 — per-accession PEV in `draw_truth()` (finding D).** At `:132`, `sqrt(inp$PEV_As)` becomes the per-accession vector `sqrt(a$PEV_As_i)`. Better still, and free: **resample a posterior draw index and take the whole vector from `BGLR_oat_As_draws_seed12567.rds`**, which carries the posterior *correlation* automatically and makes the simulated truth literally a posterior draw. Either way, keep the header comment at `:10-18` and `:104-119` saying plainly what the simulation does and does not test — `E[true|BLUP] = lambda*BLUP` by construction remains true and must keep being said.

| 685-695 | §8 Open decisions | item 3 (proceed if λ &lt; 0.5) is now answerable against λ_design = 0.7; item 4 (multiplicity) is settled by the fixed sequence; item 5 gains the H1 note that **total yield needs no extra plots** |
| 705-714 | Reproducing this | "The fitting itself is shared" becomes **true** under refactor (a) — keep it and add `BGLR_fit_trials.csv` to the output list |

**S8.5 — change 9, the hooks only.** Height, flowering and maturity on every plot. Plan the hooks, do not design the analysis:
- `field_book.csv` gains empty `plant_height_cm`, `days_to_flower`, `days_to_maturity` columns with a header comment naming the T3 trait ontology terms, so the field book is the data-entry template.
- `PREREG_MODEL$secondary` already names "realised As regressed on height/flowering/maturity (H2)" — that is the pre-registration hook and it costs nothing now.
- Add a `# TODO(H2)` stub in `validate_design.R`'s design-check report listing which traits are expected, so a missing column is noticed at upload time rather than at analysis time.
- Do **not** build the third pool (change 8 — pools stay As+/As−). Record in §8 that the phenotypic-proxy third pool was considered and declined, with the ~25% plot cost, so the decision is visible rather than absent.

## 3. The positional-argument trap: my recommendation

You lean toward named-only new arguments plus a `match.call()`/arity guard and converting the 9 positional test calls. **I would go further, and the extra step is what actually closes the hole.**

Named-only *new* arguments do not help, because the hazard is the *existing* five positional arguments. `contrast_power(15, 100, 20, 500, 400)` will keep binding `dAs, sigma2_within, n, sigma2_e, P` forever, and the next person to insert an argument before `P` reopens the bug. A `match.call()` guard catches only what you thought to check.

**Do this instead: make everything but the first argument named-only, enforced by the language.**

```r
contrast_power &lt;- function(dAs, ..., sigma2_within, n, sigma2_e, P, lambda = 1,
                           alpha = 0.05, sided = 1L, interaction_frac = NULL,
                           interaction_sd = NULL, n_loc = 5L, df_e_lost = 4L,
                           df_mode = "satterthwaite") {
  # Everything after `...` must be supplied by FULL name -- R will not match it
  # positionally or partially. That is why `...` sits here and not at the end:
  # it is a barrier, not a bag. test_validation.R had nine positional call sites
  # relying on the order (dAs, sigma2_within, n, sigma2_e, P), so inserting any
  # argument before P would have silently rebound all nine while the suite kept
  # reporting PASS. Named-only arguments make that class of change impossible.
  if (...length() &gt; 0L) {
    stop("contrast_power() takes only `dAs` positionally; ", ...length(),
         " extra positional argument(s) given. Name every argument: ",
         "contrast_power(dAs = , sigma2_within = , n = , sigma2_e = , P = , ...)",
         call. = FALSE)
  }
  ...
}
```

Why this is better than the alternatives:

- **The language enforces it.** Arguments after `...` cannot be matched positionally or partially — no hand-rolled check, no gaps.
- **The arity guard catches the residue.** `...length() &gt; 0` fires on a stray positional *and* on a misspelled name (which would otherwise land in `...` and be ignored — the quietest failure of all). It is cheap, exact, and the error message names the fix.
- **It forces the conversion rather than relying on memory.** All ~13 call sites fail immediately and loudly: `test_validation.R` lines 210, 216, 217, 221, 222, 226, 227, 229, 230, 234; `validate_power.R:385`; `validate_crossval.R:429-430`; and `validation_functions.R:581-582` (the internal `contrast_se()` call, which is positional for all seven arguments). You cannot ship a half-converted change set.
- **It makes the discipline testable.** Add the negative control:
```r
check_error(contrast_power(15, 100, 20, 500, 400),
            "positional arguments past dAs are refused")
check_error(contrast_power(dAs = 15, sigma2_withn = 100, n = 20,
                           sigma2_e = 500, P = 400),
            "a misspelled argument name is refused, not silently ignored")
```
Without that second one, the guard can rot. With it, a future refactor that removes the barrier fails the suite.

Same treatment for `contrast_se()`, `slope_power()`, `total_yield_power()`.

**Convert the test calls mechanically, not by hand.** `se_of &lt;- function(P) contrast_power(15, 100, 20, 500, P)$SE` becomes a single named helper reused at every site:
```r
PW &lt;- function(...) contrast_power(dAs = 15, sigma2_within = 100, n = 20,
                                   sigma2_e = 500, P = 400, ...)
```
which already exists at `:201-202` as `pw()`. Promote it, and have **every** one of the ten sites go through it with only the varying argument named. That reduces ten call sites to one, which is the real fix — the trap was duplication, not syntax.

---

## 4. New tests, with oracles

In `tests/README.md`'s four styles: algebraic identity, planted answer, requested value, negative control.

### 4.1 The `sigma2_within` length identity — algebraic
```r
check_near(
  contrast_se(sigma2_within = c(100, 100), n = 20, sigma2_e = 500, P = 400),
  contrast_se(sigma2_within = 100,         n = 20, sigma2_e = 500, P = 400),
  tol = 1e-12,
  "pool-specific sigma2_within reduces to the scalar form at equal variances")
check(contrast_se(sigma2_within = c(80, 120), n = 20, sigma2_e = 500, P = 400) ==
      contrast_se(sigma2_within = 100,        n = 20, sigma2_e = 500, P = 400),
      "and (A+B)/n equals 2*mean/n -- the mean is sufficient for the SE")
```
The second is a true identity `(A+B)/n == 2*((A+B)/2)/n`, and asserting it documents that the *SE* is unaffected by pool asymmetry while the *df* is (via Welch) — which is the non-obvious consequence of change 3 and the thing a reader will get wrong.

### 4.2 The df test — ADDED IN S1, BEFORE THE CHANGE (requested value + boundary identity)
S1.1's `df == 38` / `df == 58` pins. After S6 they must still hold exactly as the `interaction_frac = 0` boundary case, plus:
```r
# Satterthwaite is bounded by its own components' df
p &lt;- contrast_power(dAs = 15, sigma2_within = 100, n = 20, sigma2_e = 500,
                    P = 400, interaction_frac = 0.5, lambda = 1, n_loc = 5)
check(p$df &gt; 5 - 1 &amp;&amp; p$df &lt; 2*20 - 2,
      "Satterthwaite df lies between the interaction df and the naive df")
# and it is monotone in the interaction's share
p2 &lt;- contrast_power(dAs = 15, sigma2_within = 100, n = 20, sigma2_e = 500,
                     P = 400, interaction_frac = 1.0, lambda = 1, n_loc = 5)
check(p2$df &lt; p$df, "a larger interaction share lowers the effective df")
# NEGATIVE: naive mode must disagree, or the correction is not being applied
check(contrast_power(dAs = 15, sigma2_within = 100, n = 20, sigma2_e = 500,
                     P = 400, interaction_frac = 0.5,
                     df_mode = "naive")$df == 38,
      "df_mode = 'naive' recovers 2n-2 -- so the correction is detectably on")
# Welch reduction
check_near(contrast_power(dAs = 15, sigma2_within = c(100, 100), n = 20,
                          sigma2_e = 500, P = 400)$df_acc, 38, tol = 1e-10,
           "the Welch accession df is exactly 2n-2 at equal pool variances")
```

### 4.3 Per-accession PEV via the `colMeans(draws) == beta` identity — algebraic
Goes in `tests/test_fits.R` (the slow tier, where a real chain already runs), copying the oracle at `:321-333`:
```r
# Same trap as the interaction draws: thin is honoured, burnIn is NOT.
fd &lt;- fit_producer_associate(dat, Go, Gp, seed = 11L, nIter = 400L,
                             burnIn = 100L, thin = 10L,
                             save_effects = c("G_oat", "G_pea"),
                             saveAt = prefix)
dr &lt;- read_beta_draws(prefix, term = "ETA_G_oat", nIter = 400L, burnIn = 100L,
                      thin = 10L, p = ncol(fd$L_oat), traits = 2L)
check(dim(dr)[1] == 400L %/% 10L - 100L %/% 10L,
      "read_beta_draws keeps exactly the post-burn-in G_oat draws")
for (k in 1:2)
  check_near(colMeans(dr[, , k]), fd$fit$ETA$G_oat$beta[, k], tol = 1e-10,
             sprintf("G_oat draw mean equals BGLR's posterior mean, trait %d", k))
# the accession-scale identity: mean of L %*% draw equals L %*% mean(draw)
acc_bar &lt;- colMeans(tcrossprod(dr[, , 2], fd$L_oat))
check_near(acc_bar, as.vector(fd$L_oat %*% fd$fit$ETA$G_oat$beta[, 2]),
           tol = 1e-10,
           "the per-draw accession effects average to accession_effects()")
# NEGATIVE: keeping burn-in must be detectably different
raw &lt;- BGLR::readBinMatMultitrait(paste0(prefix, "ETA_G_oat_beta.bin"))
check(max(abs(colMeans(raw[, , 1]) - fd$fit$ETA$G_oat$beta[, 1])) &gt; 1e-6,
      "keeping burn-in gives a detectably different mean")
# PEV must be positive and must NOT equal the SD.beta route
pev &lt;- apply(tcrossprod(dr[, , 2], fd$L_oat), 2, stats::var)
check(all(pev &gt; 0), "every per-accession PEV is positive")
check(max(abs(sqrt(pev) - as.vector(fd$L_oat %*% fd$fit$ETA$G_oat$SD.beta[, 2]))) &gt; 1e-6,
      "PEV is NOT L %*% SD.beta -- that route ignores the posterior covariance")
```
That last negative control is the one that matters most: it pins *why* streaming draws is necessary, so a future "optimisation" to the cheap-looking `SD.beta` route fails loudly.

### 4.4 Estimand 2's near-equivalence — requested value
```r
# SELF_CRITIQUE / design note: selection removes the within-pool spread in x,
# so the continuous slope buys only ~3.6% more predictor variance.
# Var(x) over 2n = (dAs/2)^2 + var_within = 95.9 + 3.5
s1 &lt;- contrast_power(dAs = 19.59, sigma2_within = 100, n = 20, sigma2_e = 655.2,
                     P = 400, lambda = 0.7, interaction_frac = 0.73, n_loc = 5)
s2 &lt;- slope_power(dAs = 19.59, var_x_within = 3.5, sigma2_within = 100, n = 20,
                  sigma2_e = 655.2, P = 400, lambda = 0.7,
                  interaction_frac = 0.73, n_loc = 5)
check(s2$t &gt; s1$t, "the continuous slope is slightly more powerful")
check(s2$t / s1$t &lt; 1.05,
      "but only slightly -- adopted for interpretability, not power")
check_near(s2$var_x / ((19.59/2)^2), 1 + 3.5/((19.59/2)^2), tol = 1e-10,
           "Var(x) is exactly (dAs/2)^2 + var_within")
```
The `&lt; 1.05` bound is the test that stops the plan overselling estimand 2 — exactly what you asked for.

### 4.5 The interaction parameterisation divergence — requested value (§0.1)
```r
# interaction_frac is a CV of lambda, so substituting a judgement lambda
# rescales the interaction SD. The absolute form does not. This test exists so
# the choice cannot be made silently.
f &lt;- contrast_power(dAs = 19.59, sigma2_within = 100, n = 20, sigma2_e = 655.2,
                    P = 400, lambda = 0.7, interaction_frac = 0.73, n_loc = 5)
a &lt;- contrast_power(dAs = 19.59, sigma2_within = 100, n = 20, sigma2_e = 655.2,
                    P = 400, lambda = 0.7,
                    interaction_sd = 0.73 * 0.83 * 19.59, n_loc = 5)
check(a$power &lt; f$power - 0.05,
      "the absolute interaction form is materially more conservative at
       lambda_design &lt; lambda_measured -- the choice is visible, not implicit")
check_error(contrast_power(dAs = 15, sigma2_within = 100, n = 20,
                           sigma2_e = 500, P = 400,
                           interaction_frac = 0.5, interaction_sd = 5),
            "supplying both interaction parameterisations is refused")
check_near(contrast_power(dAs = 19.59, sigma2_within = 100, n = 20,
                          sigma2_e = 655.2, P = 400, lambda = 0.83,
                          interaction_frac = 0.73, n_loc = 5)$SE,
           contrast_power(dAs = 19.59, sigma2_within = 100, n = 20,
                          sigma2_e = 655.2, P = 400, lambda = 0.83,
                          interaction_sd = 0.73*0.83*19.59, n_loc = 5)$SE,
           tol = 1e-10,
           "the two forms agree exactly at the measured lambda, which is why
            the inconsistency only appears when lambda becomes a judgement")
```

### 4.6 Fit-trial-set vs phenotype-table assertion — planted answer + negative control
New file `tests/test_fit_provenance.R` (fast tier; must not read `output/`, per the convention at `tests/README.md`):
```r
# PLANTED: a fit-trial file that agrees, and one that does not.
tmp &lt;- tempfile(); dir.create(tmp)
pheno &lt;- tibble(studyName = rep(c("T_A","T_B"), each = 10), studyYear = 2026L,
                germplasmName = ..., intercropGermplasmName = ...,
                oat_yield = rnorm(20, 200), pea_yield = rnorm(20, 90))
saveRDS(pheno, file.path(tmp, "pheno.rds"))
readr::write_csv(tibble(studyName = c("T_A","T_B")), file.path(tmp, "fit_trials.csv"))
check(!is.null(validation_inputs(out_dir = tmp, pheno_file = ...,
                                 fit_trials_file = ...)),
      "a fit whose trial set matches the QC-filtered table is accepted")
# NEGATIVE: the exact shape of finding A -- the fit saw fewer trials
readr::write_csv(tibble(studyName = "T_A"), file.path(tmp, "fit_trials.csv"))
check_error(validation_inputs(out_dir = tmp, ...),
            "a fit that saw fewer trials than the phenotype table is REFUSED
             (SELF_CRITIQUE finding A)")
# NEGATIVE: and the missing-file case must stop, not fall back
file.remove(file.path(tmp, "fit_trials.csv"))
check_error(validation_inputs(out_dir = tmp, ...),
            "a missing fit-trial record stops rather than falling back to the
             phenotype count -- that fallback IS finding A")
check_near(validation_inputs(out_dir = tmp2, ...)$oat$n_trials, 2,
           tol = 0, "n_trials comes from the FIT, not the phenotype table")
```
That third assertion is the one that distinguishes this from `apply_trial_qc()`'s deliberately-lenient missing-file policy, and the comment should say why the policies differ.

### 4.7 Null-with-interaction type-I — planted answer + paired negative control
```r
# PLANTED: As main effects exactly 0; As x location with EXACTLY zero mean over
# locations and a known SD. The pooled test must inflate; the location-contrast
# test must not. Same seeds for both -- this is a paired comparison, not a
# comparison against nominal (the equal-variance t already sits below nominal
# because replication is unequal across accessions).
set.seed(20260924L)
res &lt;- simulate_null_interaction(inputs, oat_pools, pea_pools, p_loc = 80L,
                                 n_loc = 5L, tau = 7.0, reps = 2000L)
base &lt;- simulate_null_interaction(..., tau = 0, reps = 2000L)   # no interaction
check(res$rate_pooled &gt; base$rate_pooled + 0.03,
      "omitting location:pool inflates the type-I rate under an As x location
       null -- SELF_CRITIQUE finding C1, now measured")
check(abs(res$rate_location - base$rate_location) &lt; 0.02,
      "the pre-registered denominator does NOT inflate")
check(abs(res$mean_contrast) &lt; 3 * res$se_contrast,
      "the planted null really is a null: mean contrast is zero")
# the bracket, which is the oracle for the whole df change
check(res$power_location &lt;= res$power_satterthwaite + 1e-9 &amp;&amp;
      res$power_satterthwaite &lt;= res$power_pooled + 1e-9,
      "location-contrast &lt;= Satterthwaite &lt;= pooled: the analytic df sits
       inside the bracket the two closed forms define")
```
The bracket assertion is the strongest single oracle in the set: it ties the analytic Satterthwaite formula to two exactly-known closed forms with no MCMC and no tuning.

### 4.8 Also needed
- **Refactor-equivalence (S2.2):** assert the ETA built by `fit_producer_associate()` equals the one the old inline block built, up to column permutation — an exact, deterministic, MCMC-free oracle. `check(setequal(colnames(a$trial), colnames(b$trial)))` and `check_near(a$G_oat[, order], b$G_oat[, order], tol = 1e-12)`.
- **Eligibility, both rules:** the renamed `single-v1` partner test (S1.7) plus a reliability test with a *planted* reliability spread, asserting that a low-reliability accession with many partners is now excluded and a high-reliability one with two partners is included — and a negative control that an accession with **one** partner is excluded by the `partner_floor` even at high reliability (§0.3).
- **`validate_design.R` exit status:** a test that a deliberately-imbalanced design produces a non-zero exit. Currently untestable because the script never exits non-zero.

## 5. Runtime, cost, and the exact commands

| stage | what runs | wall time |
|---|---|---|
| S0 | `mv` | seconds |
| S1 | `tests/run_all.R` | ~45 s (fast tier) |
| S2+S3 | 4-seed refit on 8 trials + PEV pass | **~10 min** |
| S4 | pool build, both arms | &lt;1 min |
| S5 | 8 crossval folds, 2 scales | **~8–12 min** (6,000-iter chains; 2 scales add no fits, only `lm` calls) |
| S6 | analytic only | seconds |
| S7 | 3 budgets × 400 reps + 2 null arms × 2,000 reps | **~10–20 min** |
| S7.5 | optional `lme4` arm, 200 reps | +5–10 min |
| S8 | report + prose | human time |
| | **machine total** | **~35–45 min** |
| | `tests/run_all.R --all` | +~15 s (the new draw test adds a 400-iter chain) |

Disk: 53 MiB of `.bin` draws (4 seeds × 2 terms at `single`), 12 MiB of accession-scale draw RDS (seed 1, both species). `output/` is gitignored.

**Commands, in order:**

```bash
cd /Users/jj332/Documents/GitRepo/B4I_Fall2026

# S0 -- archive the baseline FIRST, before anything writes today's vintage
mv output/validation/2026-10-03 output/validation/2026-10-03_5trial-whitelist
echo "5-trial whitelist fit (BGLR_multi_trait_model.R:72-79); see SELF_CRITIQUE.md finding A" \
  &gt; output/validation/2026-10-03_5trial-whitelist/PROVENANCE.txt

# S1 -- harden the suite; must be green before touching code/
Rscript tests/run_all.R

# S2+S3 -- the refit (and verify the trial set landed)
Rscript code/BGLR_multi_trait_model.R
Rscript -e 'print(readr::read_csv("output/BGLR_fit_trials.csv", show_col_types=FALSE))'
Rscript -e 'q &lt;- readr::read_csv("output/trial_qc.csv", show_col_types=FALSE);
            f &lt;- readr::read_csv("output/BGLR_fit_trials.csv", show_col_types=FALSE);
            stopifnot(setequal(f$studyName, q$studyName[q$keep])); cat("trial set OK\n")'

# S4 -- pools, BOTH arms. READ pool_diff.csv BEFORE GOING FURTHER.
Rscript code/validate_pool_selection.R
open output/validation/$(date +%F)/pool_diff.csv          # arm D vs 2026-10-03: the A4 test
open output/validation/$(date +%F)/pool_diff_rule.csv     # arm R vs arm D: rule churn

# S5 -- lambda: QC'd folds, two scales, by year
Rscript code/validate_crossval.R

# S6+S7 -- power and the null simulation
Rscript code/validate_power.R

# design, field book, frozen predictors -- now exits non-zero on a FAIL
Rscript code/validate_design.R; echo "exit=$?"

# S8 -- regenerate the document's numbers, then hand-edit the prose
Rscript code/validation_report.R --check    # expect exit 1: drift
Rscript code/validation_report.R
git diff VALIDATION_DESIGN.md

# full suite, including the slow tier with the new draw test
Rscript tests/run_all.R --all
```

Once the scripts are in shape, the whole thing is `Rscript code/validate_refresh.R --from fit --refresh-doc`, but **do not use that for this change set** — S4's `pool_diff.csv` must be read before S5–S7 run, and the driver will not stop for you.

## 6. Risks

**Highest, in order:**

1. **Arm D's churn comes back below 70%.** SELF_CRITIQUE §A4 expects it to ("My expectation is that they will breach it"). §7's own standard then says the effects are not stable enough to validate, and the honest conclusion is the alternative design the critique names in §5 — replicated oat × pea combinations to *estimate* σ²_comb. **That outcome is a result, not a failure, and the plan must not be written so that it cannot be reported.** Read `pool_diff.csv` before investing in S5–S7; if arm D breaks badly, S6/S7 are still worth doing (the model and the power machinery are needed either way) but the *conclusion* changes.

2. **The refactor to `fit_producer_associate()` changes the numbers.** Different column ordering, the drop-vs-error behaviour on GRM-absent accessions, and `dummy_matrix()` vs `model.matrix()` are all benign in principle; `grm_factor()`'s eigen sign convention is deterministic but BLAS-dependent. Mitigate with the ETA-equality test (§4.8) *before* the refit, and keep the old inline code in the commit history so a disagreement can be bisected.

3. **`rel_min` starves a pool.** Pea needs ≥120 eligible accessions for n=30 after the 0.5 Pr quantile. Calibrate against the partner-count candidate counts rather than picking a round number, sweep ±0.05, and have `validate_pool_selection.R` name `rel_min` in the error message when `build_pools()` refuses.

4. **The interaction term now dominates and has 4 df, so power is very sensitive to `n_loc`.** At 4 locations SELF_CRITIQUE §C2 already puts oat at 0.64. With the absolute interaction form (§0.1) and 4 locations it will be worse. **Add `n_loc` to the sweep and report the lost-location case explicitly** — it is the single largest operational risk to the trial and it is currently invisible in the document.

5. **A mistyped generated marker publishes a stale table and reports "up to date."** `validation_report.R:182-185` messages and skips. After adding four marker pairs, run `--check` and grep the output for `no markers for` — do not trust the exit code alone.

6. **The staleness detector will not notice the new swept terms** (`n_loc`, `interaction_mode`, `df_mode`, `lambda_source`) and would silently publish a stale `estimand_power` table. S8.1's shared `.is_swept()` helper is the mitigation; without it this *will* happen.

7. **Two conventions diverging again.** `validation_inputs()` counting partners on one filter chain while the fit uses another is finding A in a new place. Route both through one named filter function (S2.4) rather than keeping two copies in sync by discipline.

8. **The `lme4` arm (S7.5) is the one place a dependency could creep in.** `lmerTest` is absent on this machine. Keep that arm opt-in; the closed-form bracket (§0.9) is the load-bearing route and needs nothing new.

**Lower:**

- The `thin`-honoured/`burnIn`-ignored trap. `read_beta_draws()` already handles it and `test_fits.R:321-333` already has the negative control. Reuse both verbatim; do not write a second reader.
- `single` storage costs nothing for a variance but is lossier for the `colMeans == beta` identity. At 1e-10 tolerance a `single` file will **fail** that check. Use `storage_mode = "double"` in the *test* (400 iterations, tiny file) and `"single"` in production, and say so in the test comment — otherwise someone will "fix" production to double and pay 53 extra MiB for nothing.
- `interaction_frac_corrected` can go to zero if `mean(lambda_se²)` exceeds `var(λ̂)`. The `max(0, ...)` is right but the result must be reported as "not distinguishable from zero", not as "zero interaction" — the difference matters a lot to the power claim, and it would be the best news in the whole exercise.
